# Mixed atomic leaves: an exact pair price and uniform energy decay

Status: COMPLETE JOINT CANDIDATE, NOT VERIFIER-ACCEPTED. Worker max,
cumulative round23. No optimization, numerical sweep, exact replay, or
fact_submit was used in this round. All inequalities below are textual
algebraic arguments. The only accepted results used are the original
interpolation convention in1252b8823bb5a290 and the exact benchmark bound
inb98bb807d514e80d. Previous max drafts motivate this calculation but are
not premises; every seed and join identity needed here is rederived.

## 1. Statement and scope

Write lambda=1+sqrt(2), theta=sqrt(2)-1=1/lambda, and put

 a_lo=14127/10000, a_hi=14128/10000,
 b_lo=20043/10000, b_hi=20044/10000,
 M_bar=251/250, P_pair=189/200, eta_tree=99981/100000.

At every leaf choose independently

 a_lo<a<a_hi, b_lo<b<b_hi, 0<=rho<=1, E>=0.

Define c=a-1, z=b-1, m=a+b, Klen=a+rho, eta=z/Klen,
A=1+2a+b, epsilon=sqrt(E), and vectors in R^3

 U=(1,0,0), V=(0,sqrt(eta),0), W=(0,rho sqrt(eta),epsilon).

The four gradients and four positions of the three-step atom are

 g=(U,U,V,W),
 x=(mU+aV+W, bU+aV+W, aV+W, W).

Let y_i=x_i-g_i, e_i=<g_i,y_i>, and set
f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. The word is(a,b,a), its mass is A, its
initial vector is p=x_0, and its endpoint is W. Require

                 0<=E<=(1-rho)e,  e=(a+rho-1)eta.       (1)

Build a balanced binary tree with N=2^J such leaves. At an internal node
with children of masses A_L,A_R, use the bridge step1+r, where

 r>0, r(A_L+A_R+r)=A_L A_R,
 A_parent=A_L+A_R+r.                                  (2)

Embed both children isometrically, retaining the entire left child and a
scaled copy of the entire right child, including all positions, gradients,
values, and both terminal samples. If the left endpoint is w_L and the
right initial vector has norm R_R, the right scale and alignment are

 alpha=r||w_L||/R_R,
 alpha J_R(p_R)=-r J_L(w_L).                           (3)

Right positions and gradients are multiplied by alpha, and right function
values by alpha^2; left values are retained without rescaling.

All isometries and ambient dimensions may be chosen anew at every node,
and all choices may depend on the full target horizon. A tree is legal
only if EVERY original interpolation inequality Q_ij<=0, including the
optimum, holds for its final data. No other restriction on the transverse
maps is imposed.

For any node define its actual core ratio

             kappa_node=A_node^2 ||w_node||^2/R_node^2,

where R_node is its true initial distance to the minimizer set of the
projection interpolant constructed below. Then any legal tree with J>=5
obeys

 kappa_root <= M_bar^16 eta_tree^(N/16-1).              (4)

In particular kappa_root tends to0 exponentially in the number of leaves,
uniformly over all legal choices in this family. The normalized final
function gap for its actual full word of length n=4N-1 is exactly

 2(F(x_n)-min F)/dist(x_0,argmin F)^2
                  =kappa_root/A_root^2.              (5)

Its ratio to the ORIGINAL benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/B_n tends uniformly to0.

This family contains the actual atom used in the rational15/31 drafts:
a=(311sqrt(2)-16)/300, b=(590+8sqrt(2))/300,
rho=599/600, E=1/10000. For the a,b inclusion, use
141421/100000<sqrt(2)<707107/500000, whose two strict inequalities follow
by squaring, and substitute in the affine definitions of a,b. The claim
also allows nonidentical a,b in the displayed box and arbitrary legal
rho,E, rather than just small terminal angles.

This is an obstruction to a specified witness family. It is not an upper
bound on W_n(h), a no-go theorem for arbitrary projection trajectories,
or a resolution of the original minimax problem. Section9 supplies an
explicit counterexample to the unrestricted geometric generalization.

## 2. Every atomic row, one global function, and true distance

The residuals are

 y0=(m-1)U+Klen V+epsilon e3,
 y1=zU+Klen V+epsilon e3,
 y2=(Klen-1)V+epsilon e3,
 y3=0,

where e3=(0,0,1), and the energies are(m-1,z,e,0). At receiving
sample0 the other gradient vertices0,V,W have normals

 -(m-1), -a, -a-(1-rho)z+E.

At receiving sample1 the vertices0,V,W give

 -z, 0, -(1-rho)z+E.

At receiving sample2 the vertices0,U,W give

 -e, -e, -(1-rho)e+E.

The omitted repeated U vertex has the same normal, the receiving vertex
itself has normal0, and all normals at sample3 vanish. Because0<e<z,
these inequalities hold if and only if(1) holds. This is a full atomic
audit, including the positive E charge from the third coordinate.

For any finite collection of such projection jets, define

 K_body=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n),
 F(x)=max_(v in K_body)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2).               (6)

Completing the square makes the unique maximizing vector the Euclidean
projection P_K_body(x). The two projection inequalities, added, show

 ||P_K_body(x)-P_K_body(x')||^2
       <=<P_K_body(x)-P_K_body(x'),x-x'>.

Evaluating the maximum at the two maximizers proves differentiability
with gradient P_K_body, and the displayed inequality proves that this
gradient is1-Lipschitz. Thus F is a finite convex differentiable1-smooth
function. The vertex normals imply P_K_body(x_i)=g_i and F(x_i)=f_i.
Using the signed convention of accepted1252b8823bb5a290 gives exactly

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
     =<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                                  (7)

The optimum is0, with Q_(i,star)=0 and Q_(star,i)=-e_i.
Thus a legal complete tree is interpolated by ONE function(6) with all
its original values. No successive change of the function is involved.

The minimizer set of(6) is K_body^polar. If x is in this polar, the
maximum equals0. Conversely, if F(x)=0, insert tv for each v in K_body
and0<t<=1, divide by t, and let t decrease to0; then <x,v><=0.
At a leaf p=mU+aV+W is a positive combination of retained vertices.
Consequently <p,v><=0 for v in K_body^polar, and
||p-v||^2>=||p||^2. Zero is a closest minimizer. At each join the initial
vector and these first-leaf vertices are retained by a left isometry.
The same argument therefore gives the true squared initial distance
R_node^2=||p_node||^2 at every legal node.

For a leaf, direct inner products give

 R^2=m^2+Klen z+E,
 N_leaf=||W||^2=rho^2 eta+E,
 D_leaf=<p,W>=rho z+E.                                (8)

There is no normalization loss hidden in these formulas.

## 3. Exact coordinates for the energy-angle tradeoff

Define two dimensionless coordinates of a leaf:

 sigma=rho+E/e,
 u=c sqrt((sigma-rho)/(c+rho)).                        (9)

By(1), 0<=rho<=sigma<=1. The value u is also exactly
c epsilon sqrt(eta)/e. These definitions permit zero E and zero rho.
Put

 C0=a(m^2+az), C1=m^2+2az,
 D(sigma)=C0+C1 sigma+z sigma^2,
 D1=D(1)=(a+1)(m^2+(a+1)z),
 T=(m^2+z)/c^2, M=A^2 z/D1.

Solving(9) for rho gives

 rho=(sigma-u^2/c)/(1+u^2/c^2).

Substitution in(8), with the common denominator a+sigma+u^2/c^2,
gives the exact identities

 kappa=A^2 z(sigma^2+u^2)/(D(sigma)+T u^2),
 d:=D_leaf/R^2
     =z[sigma(sigma+a)-u^2/c]/(D(sigma)+T u^2).         (10)

In particular,

 M-kappa = M * [(1-sigma){C0+(C0+C1)sigma}
                      +(T-D1)u^2]/[D(sigma)+T u^2].   (11)

The following rational bounds hold throughout the a,b box:

 233/40<A<583/100,
 34.01<D1<34.04, 74<T<75,
 C0>18.49, C1>14.51,
 34.04<A^2 z<34.14,
 1<M<M_bar=251/250.                                   (12)

All terminating decimals in this manuscript denote exact rationals.
Here is enough endpoint arithmetic to check every bound in(12):

 3.417<m<3.4172, .4127<c<.4128,
 1.0043<z<1.0044, 5.8297<A<5.83;

 2.4127[3.417^2+2.4127(1.0043)]>34.01,
 2.4128[3.4172^2+2.4128(1.0044)]<34.04;

 (3.417^2+1.0043)>74(.4128)^2,
 (3.4172^2+1.0044)<75(.4127)^2;

 1.4127[3.417^2+1.4127(1.0043)]>18.49,
 3.417^2+2(1.4127)(1.0043)>14.51;

 (5.8297)^2(1.0043)>34.04,
 (5.83)^2(1.0044)<34.14,
 34.14/34.01<251/250.

The positive coefficients make endpoint substitution legitimate in
each displayed expression. Equation(11), T>D1, and sigma<=1 show that
EVERY legal leaf has0<=kappa<=M<M_bar. This is an exact global envelope
over rho,E in this atomic family, not a numerical maximization.

## 4. Quantitative cost when a leaf has high energy

For the rest of this section suppose

 kappa>47/50=.94,
 delta=M_bar-kappa<8/125=.064.                        (13)

Then

 delta >= (5/4)(1-sigma)+u^2.                         (14)

To prove this, first sigma>19/20=.95. Indeed(10) is a weighted average
of A^2 z sigma^2/D(sigma) and A^2 z/T when u>0; for u=0 it is the first
quantity. The first ratio is increasing in sigma>=0, since the numerator
of its derivative after removing a positive factor is
sigma(2C0+C1 sigma). At sigma=.95 its value is at most

 34.14(.95)^2/[18.49+14.51(.95)+(.95)^2]<.94,

while A^2 z/T<34.14/74<.94. This proves the claim about sigma.

Dividing(11) by kappa and dropping the first nonnegative term yields

 (M-kappa)/kappa
   >= (T/D1-1)u^2/(1+u^2) >= (117/100)u^2/(1+u^2).

Here T/D1-1>74/34.04-1>117/100. If u=0 the following bound is immediate;
if u>0 the last inequality is strict. Thus(13) implies

 u^2 < (8/125)/[(47/50)(117/100)-8/125]
     =320/5179<1/16.

The denominator in(11) is therefore below34.04+75/16=38.7275.
Its coefficient of u^2, including M>1, is greater than
(74-34.04)/38.7275>1. Its coefficient of1-sigma is greater than

 [18.49+(18.49+14.51)(.95)]/38.7275>5/4.

Since delta>=M-kappa, this proves(14).

We also need the following one-sided angle estimate, valid for every
leaf and hence in particular under(13):

                      d>.071-delta/5.                (15)

For a direct proof set

 ell=[(a+1)T+D1/c]/[A^2(T-D1)],
 beta=z[(a+1)+1/c]/(T-D1),
 d0=z/[m^2+(a+1)z].

Then ell M-beta=d0. On subtracting ell kappa-beta from(10), the u^2
coefficient cancels. The remaining numerator is a polynomial in sigma,
vanishing at1, with constant beta C0>0 and linear coefficient
az+beta C1>0. It factors as

 (1-sigma)[beta C0+(az+beta C1+beta C0)sigma]>=0.

Consequently d>=ell kappa-beta=d0-ell(M-kappa).
The endpoint bounds give d0>1.004/14.11>.071. They also give

 ell < [(2.42)(75)+(34.04)(2.5)]/[(33.9)(39.9)]<1/5.

Since0<=M-kappa<=delta, inequality(15) follows.

Finally, define the perpendicular ratio of the terminal gradient by

 t_perp=||W-(<p,W>/R^2)p||/R
        =sqrt(kappa/A^2-d^2).

Under(13), d>.071-.064/5=.0582=291/5000, and
kappa/A^2<M_bar/(233/40)^2<74/2500. Hence

 t_perp^2<74/2500-(291/5000)^2
           =655319/25000000<(81/500)^2,
                         t_perp<81/500=.162.          (16)

Equations(14)-(16) retain the E/e contribution to the axial coefficient
sigma. In particular maximal legal E makes sigma=1; it is not permissible
to keep its transverse benefit while discarding this axial charge.

## 5. An original-row condition at every large bridge

Consider a node whose two children have at least two atomic leaves.
Let the LAST leaf of the left child have parameters with subscript L,
and let the FIRST leaf of the right child have subscript R. Write the
last leaf's overall scale inside the left child as b_scale>0. Its
endpoint and penultimate residual are isometric copies of

 b_scale W_L, b_scale y2_L,
 y2_L=c_L V_L+W_L.

The receiving energy is e_rec=b_scale^2 e_L>0. Direct projection onto
the W_L axis gives

 <W_L,y2_L>/e_L=sigma_L,
 ||(y2_L)_perp W_L||=c_L epsilon_L sqrt(eta_L/N_L).

Within the right child, the FIRST bridge joins its first two leaves.
If its excess is r_first and the second leaf's mass is A_second, the
source identity p_second=m_second U_second+a_second V_second+W_second
and the bridge alignment give

 m_second G4+a_second G6+G7=-r_first W_R.

Here W_R is embedded from the right child's first leaf, and G4,G6,G7
are its actual retained gradients, including all intervening scales.
Dividing by A_second produces a convex combination v_R of these three
gradients, because m_second+a_second+1=A_second. Thus

                 v_R=-tau W_R,
                 tau=r_first/A_second>0.             (17)

This vector identity is retained by EVERY subsequent left embedding,
regardless of earlier transverse choices. The initial vector of the
whole right child is the same isometric copy of p_R from its first leaf.

Apply the proposed parent alignment(3). The component of the weighted
new right gradient along the left endpoint gives exactly
r e_rec tau sigma_L d_R. Cauchy--Schwarz bounds its perpendicular
component below by-r e_rec tau u_L t_perp,R. Subtract the receiving
energy and use(7): the corresponding convex combination of ORIGINAL
cross interpolation rows satisfies

 sum weights Q / e_rec
        >= r tau [sigma_L d_R-u_L t_perp,R]-1.         (18)

This argument permits every real transverse contraction, every ambient
dimension and all earlier maps. It has not optimized a reduced Gram
matrix or deleted any interpolation rows.

For a node containing N_node leaves, the three rows in(18) have actual
indices Q_(2N_node+4,2N_node-2), Q_(2N_node+6,2N_node-2), and
Q_(2N_node+7,2N_node-2), with the positive weights in(17).

At a balanced parent of depth at least5, the factor r tau has the
uniform bound

                            r tau>169/5.              (19)

First, the mass ratio q=A_first/A_second of any two leaves is greater
than999/1000, using5.8297<A<5.83. Substitution of r_first=tau A_second
in(2) gives tau^2+(q+1)tau-q=0. This increasing polynomial is negative
at tau=207/500=.414: its value is strictly below
.585396-(.999)(.586)=-.000018. Thus tau>207/500.

Next, the positive root r in(2) is increasing in either mass: it lies
strictly between0 and min(A_L,A_R), and implicit differentiation gives
dr/dA_L=(A_R-r)/(2r+A_L+A_R)>0, with the analogous right derivative.
Equal child masses A have parent mass lambda A. Induction therefore
places every depth-j mass above lambda^j(233/40). At depth at least5
the new excess is consequently greater thanlambda^3(233/40).
Since lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2)>281/20, one obtains

 r tau>(281/20)(233/40)(207/500)
        =13552911/400000>169/5,

which proves(19).

## 6. Each middle pair costs at least a fixed product factor

We claim that every legal node of depth at least5 must satisfy

                       kappa_L kappa_R<P_pair=189/200, (20)

where these are the two LEAF ratios in Section5, not the child ratios.

Suppose instead kappa_L kappa_R>=189/200. Since each ratio is below
M_bar, each exceeds(189/200)/M_bar=945/1004>47/50. Put
delta_L=M_bar-kappa_L and delta_R=M_bar-kappa_R. The arithmetic-geometric
mean inequality gives

 delta_L+delta_R<=2M_bar-2sqrt(189/200)<8/125,

because(243/250)^2<189/200. Thus(14)-(16) apply to both leaves.
Set D_bound=.071-delta_R/5>0. Equations(14)-(16) imply

 sigma_L d_R-u_L t_perp,R
 >=D_bound[1-(4/5)delta_L+(4/5)u_L^2]-(81/500)u_L.

For0<=u_L<=sqrt(delta_L), the right side is decreasing in u_L.
Its derivative is(8/5)D_bound u_L-81/500<0, using
D_bound<=.071 and u_L<sqrt(.064)<.255. Therefore

 sigma_L d_R-u_L t_perp,R
             >=.071-delta_R/5-(81/500)sqrt(delta_L).

For delta_L+delta_R<=8/125, the largest possible subtracted expression
is(81/500)sqrt(8/125). Indeed the derivative of
(8/125-delta_L)/5+(81/500)sqrt(delta_L) is positive throughout
0<delta_L<=8/125. Since sqrt(8/125)<51/200, it follows that

 sigma_L d_R-u_L t_perp,R
         >71/1000-(81/500)(51/200)=2969/100000.         (21)

Combining(18),(19),(21) gives the strict ORIGINAL-row violation

 sum weights Q/e_rec
        >(169/5)(2969/100000)-1=1761/500000>0.

This contradicts legality and proves(20). No numerical infeasibility
flag or assumption about an optimal transverse map enters this argument.

## 7. Disjoint pairs and the exact normalization product

At each internal node, the endpoint norm is
||w_parent||^2=r^2||w_L||^2||w_R||^2/R_R^2, by(3). Its true initial
distance is R_L, by Section2. Since A_parent r=A_L A_R, one obtains
directly

                kappa_parent=kappa_child,L kappa_child,R.

Inducting gives kappa_root=product_(all leaves)kappa_leaf. If an
endpoint vanishes, this product and all subsequent endpoint energies
are zero and(4) is immediate. Thus the positive-scale argument above
loses no zero case relevant to the bound.

Number the N=2^J leaves from1 toN in chronological order. A midpoint
pair belonging to a node of depth at least5 is exactly a pair

                 (16k,16k+1), 1<=k<N/16.

To check this bijection, write a split position as2^(d-1)(2s+1): its
2-adic valuation is d-1. Thus d>=5 is equivalent to divisibility by16.
All these pairs are disjoint. Their number is M_pairs=N/16-1.
Each pair has product below P_pair by(20), and each remaining leaf has
kappa<M_bar. Hence

 kappa_root<=P_pair^M_pairs M_bar^(N-2M_pairs)
            =M_bar^16(P_pair M_bar^14)^M_pairs.        (22)

For completeness, M_bar^14<529/500 follows by setting x=1/250 and
bounding the binomial coefficients from their quadratic term onwards.
The ratio of each next term to its predecessor is at most4x for these
terms, so

 (1+x)^14<=1+14x+91x^2/(1-4x)
          =1+7/125+91/61500<529/500.

Therefore P_pair M_bar^14<(189/200)(529/500)=99981/100000.
Equation(22) proves(4). This is stronger than merely locating one
low-energy leaf inside each disjoint subtree: the actual pair costs
at ALL large split positions are charged without double counting.

## 8. The original final objective and exact B comparison

The final residual is0, so F(x_n)=||w_root||^2/2. The positive-cone
argument proves the true distance R_root, yielding(5) without a tail,
extra coordinate or boundary reset. There are exactly3N+(N-1)=4N-1
steps. By the mass monotonicity already proved,

 A_root>lambda^J(233/40).

Let p_exp=log_2(3). Accepted factb98bb807d514e80d gives the bound for
the EXACT original dominant recurrence B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1:

                  B_n<=1+4n^p_exp<1+36N^p_exp.

Consequently the actual full-word witness ratio obeys

 B_(4N-1) * 2F(x_(4N-1))/R_root^2
 <= [(1+36N^p_exp) M_bar^16 eta_tree^(N/16-1)]
       /[(233/40)^2 lambda^(2J)] ->0.                 (23)

The bound is uniform over all choices made at this horizon, including
nonidentical leaves and every transverse map. It does not require a
single atom or a single function to serve multiple horizons. For each
chosen run, however, all samples are realized by the ONE function(6).

No objective-tail lemma is used. Appending a different final tail or
altering inherited values is a different construction whose original
rows and distance require separate analysis. Equation(23) bounds the
payoff of these specified witnesses, never the supremum W_n(h).

## 9. Scope test: a different atom iterates with growing energy

It is false that every isometrically retained source-matched projection
tree must have vanishing core energy. Take the single global quadratic
F(x)=x^2/2 on R, x_0=1, and the atom h0=(2,0,0). Every sample has
g_i=x_i, y_i=0, f_i=x_i^2/2. Its endpoint is-1 and its mass is A0=3.
All original Q rows equal0, including the optimum. If desired, it is
also realized by(6), taking the finite convex hull of the gradients of
the final chosen trajectory; all sample points belong to this interval.

Define h_(j+1)=(h_j,1+r_j,h_j), A_j=3lambda^j,
r_j=theta A_j. The gradient-descent endpoint w_j from initial1 obeys

 w_0=-1, w_(j+1)=-r_j w_j^2.

The first equality is the literal atom. The second follows because the
left copy multiplies the initial point by w_j, the bridge by-r_j, and
the right copy again by w_j. Thus every child is an actual scaled
isometric copy on the SAME quadratic and the bridge obeys(2)-(3).
The true initial distance remains1. Its energy satisfies

 kappa_0=9, kappa_(j+1)=kappa_j^2,
                       kappa_j=9^(2^j).

This example violates any proposed extension of(4) to unrestricted
atomic projection trajectories. It does not violate the stated result:
its word, mass, and zero receiving residuals fall outside Section1.
It also supplies no improved schedule or uniform minimax converse.

## 10. A general boundary condition for a changed atom

For any proposed join of projection jets with the value structure(7),
let p_R be the right initial vector, R_R its
norm, and w_L the nonzero left endpoint. For any left receiving residual
y with positive energy e_rec=<g,y>, decompose y along w_L:

 a_axis=<y,w_L>/||w_L||,
 b_perp=||y-a_axis w_L/||w_L||||.

For EVERY convex combination v of retained right gradients, write
v_perp=v-(<v,p_R>/R_R^2)p_R. Alignment(3) and Cauchy--Schwarz give the
necessary original-row condition

 alpha[-a_axis <p_R,v>/R_R-b_perp||v_perp||]<=e_rec,
                  alpha=r||w_L||/R_R.                (24)

Taking the supremum of the left side over the actual retained convex
hull gives the strongest bound furnished by this single-row Cauchy
argument. This is still a necessary condition, not simultaneous
sufficiency for all receiving rows and the full transverse contraction.

The present proof uses the retained source combination(17) in(24),
then prices the leaf's transverse allowance by(11). A successful new
atom must change this quantitative relationship, the retained source
geometry, the source-matched join rule, or the value/residual structure.
The quadratic example avoids the receiving obstruction because every
y is zero. It illustrates an escape in geometry, while leaving the
original all-schedule objective-profile problem unresolved.

## Provenance and glossary

The fact ids1252b8823bb5a290 andb98bb807d514e80d are the only verified
predecessors used. The motivating shared drafts3a81e23df4b047fe and
c21e4e49db154b0c were NOT assumed to be facts. Equations(1)-(24) form one
self-contained unverified candidate argument and may not be cited as
accepted downstream until selected verification accepts it.

All atomic symbols are defined in Sections1-4. J is balanced tree depth,
N=2^J is the leaf count, and n=4N-1 is the actual number of gradient
steps. M_pairs is the number of disjoint large-split midpoint pairs.
The projection body K_body differs from the scalar Klen=a+rho. The
letter M is a leaf-dependent exact envelope; M_bar is its fixed rational
upper bound. The letter d is an atomic normalized inner product, not
ambient dimension. The coordinate u in(9) is a scalar, not a gradient.
The symbol beta in Section4 is an affine-support coefficient, not a
geometric scale; b_scale denotes the geometric scale in Section5.

# Released values: normalized full interpolation and a queried six-cycle

Status: **UNVERIFIED JOINT PROOF CANDIDATE**, worker max, cumulative round24.
This document does not add a fact. TASK.md and guidance5e2fd47b60222794
allocate no verifier calls. No optimization, numerical model evaluation,
exact arithmetic replay, installation, or new vector-template search was
used. All statements below are in one joint draft, not a chain of
unverified findings treated as facts.

The accepted facts used are1252b8823bb5a290 (the original interpolation
inequalities, their explicit global interpolant, and the precisely matched
terminal reduction) and b98bb807d514e80d (the definition of the original
benchmark B_n). The old projection-value separator is NOT a premise.

## 1. Result and exact scope

Take the actual rational31 vector template specified in section2 and
Appendix A. Form its physical63-step word by the prescribed source-matched
balanced join. Allow **every** real transverse16-by16 coupling whose full
Gram is positive semidefinite, in any finite ambient dimension. Keep all
queried positions and gradients from these child embeddings. Allow:

* arbitrary real function values at every sample;
* any one actual common minimizer, including components outside the child
  span, with all its interpolation constraints and the initial-distance
  normalization;
* any actual final gradient at sample63, with every original terminal row,
  or the exactly matched terminal completion of1252b8823bb5a290;
* arbitrary common positive spatial scaling and common translation.

Then no convex1-smooth function interpolates even the queried prefix.
The explicit cycle is

 Q_(36,30)+Q_(30,31)+Q_(31,39)+Q_(39,38)
       +Q_(38,37)+Q_(37,36)
       > (483/2000) t beta^2 e > 0,                    (C63)

where t is squared common scale, beta is the last-leaf scale of the31
child, and e>0 is the atomic receiving energy, all defined below.
Every index in this cycle is at most39, hence is QUERIED at horizon63.
All function values cancel exactly. Neither the optimum nor sample63 is
present. Thus shifting the minimizer, repricing values, enlarging the
ambient space, and choosing the final gradient cannot remove this cycle.

This excludes the specified fixed geometry, not the physical word over
all possible trajectories and not W_63 of that word. It proves no upper
bound on W_n and does not settle the original asymptotic minimax target.
The first seven-step transverse block is retained as zero. In fact the
argument does not depend on the later rational15/31 blocks: those two
blocks can be replaced by arbitrary isometric source-matched couplings
without changing the conclusion. No assertion allowing changes to the
first seven-step block, atomic queried gradients, or joining rule is made.

## 2. Full geometric definitions

Use s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1=1/lambda, and

 a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300,
 rho=599/600, eps=1/100, Klen=a+rho,
 z_atom=b-1, m=a+b, eta=z_atom/Klen, A=1+2a+b.

The atomic vectors in R^3 and its physical three-step word are

 U=(1,0,0), V=(0,sqrt(eta),0), W=(0,rho sqrt(eta),eps),
 g=(U,U,V,W),
 x=(mU+aV+W, bU+aV+W, aV+W, W), h[0]=(a,b,a).

Set p=x_0, R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Klen*z_atom+eps^2,
N=||W||^2=rho^2 eta+eps^2, and kappa=A^2 N/R^2.
The two residuals needed later are

 y_1=z_atom U+Klen V+eps*(0,0,1),
 y_2=(a-1)V+W,
 e=<V,y_2>=(Klen-1)eta,
 d_atom=(1-rho)e-eps^2.                               (D)

For a nonzero vector v, choose the explicit orthogonal Householder frame
B(v) that sends v/||v|| to the first coordinate vector: put
u=v/||v||, k=e_1-u and B(v)=I-2kk^T/(k^Tk); when u=e_1 set B(v)=I.
Its first row is v^T/||v||. At level j, the child has initial p_j,
endpoint w_j=x_end=g_end, dimension d_j=2^(j+1)+1, word h[j],
and capacity A_j=lambda^j A. Define

 r_j=theta A_j, alpha_j=r_j ||w_j||/R.

For a real (d_j-1)-square contraction C_j, write
B(w_j)v=(v_l0,v_lT), B(p_j)v=(v_r0,v_rT), and use isometries

 L_j(v)=(v_l0,v_lT,0),
 R_j(v)=(-v_r0,C_j v_rT,(I-C_j^T C_j)^(1/2) v_rT).

The square root is the positive symmetric one. The next data list consists
of L_j applied to every left position/gradient and alpha_j R_j applied to
every right position/gradient. The word is

 h[j+1]=(h[j],1+r_j,h[j]).

Indeed alpha_j R_j(p_j)=-r_j L_j(w_j), and the queried left endpoint
is x_end=g_end=w_j, so the bridge update is exact. Child updates persist.
The next initial norm is R, and its endpoint norm is alpha_j||w_j||.
These are geometric statements and prescribe no function values.

The known actual31 template uses C_0=0_(2x2),

 C_1=(1/100)*[[7,0,7,0],[1,0,0,0],
              [18,-1,-10,0],[-17,0,-11,0]],

and C_2=(1/10000) times

 [-74,144,-360,369,107,675,1350,62;
   513,4,-187,255,-814,10,-854,-1;
   -81,-60,182,-156,21,-277,-135,-57;
   129,21,17,83,-400,-45,-370,-6;
   508,-98,-292,89,185,97,-309,6;
   -7,-1,-61,22,-64,2,-68,0;
   -300,-22,-447,97,211,270,341,35;
   40,0,-14,-18,57,-1,66,0].

Their squared Frobenius norms are934/10000 and5887407/10^8, each<1.
Thus the defining square roots exist. The child at level3 has32 samples
and dimension17. Write its positions/gradients as (Z_i,G_i),0<=i<=31,
its initial point P=Z_0, and its endpoint w=Z_31=G_31. With

 beta=alpha_0 alpha_1 alpha_2,

the last atomic leaf has (G_30,Z_30-G_30,w) equal to beta times one
common isometric image of (V,y_2,W). In particular ||w||^2=beta^2 N.

The63 word is h[4]=(h[3],1+r_3,h[3]), with

 r_3=lambda^2 A, alpha_3=r_3 beta sqrt(N)/R.

At its next join C_3 is the ENTIRE16-by16 contraction, not a rank-one or
diagonal restriction. Before a common scaling, its queried jets are
(L_3 Z_i,L_3 G_i),0<=i<=31, and
(alpha_3 R_3 Z_i,alpha_3 R_3 G_i),0<=i<=30. Its last position is
alpha_3 R_3 Z_31; its last gradient is free. Values are free throughout.

## 3. Lossless normalized Gram formulation

This section models precisely the two fixed child embeddings, with no
assumption about an optimum or terminal vector lying in their span.
Let d=17 for the63 case, or d=9 for the31 calibration. In either case
take the already defined child positions Z_i, gradients G_i, initial P,
endpoint w, and fixed bridge scale alpha=r||w||/R. Child frames are fixed.

Introduce formal vectors (ell_0,ell_1,...,ell_(d-1),
v_1,...,v_(d-1)) with full Gram

 K(t,T)=[ t, 0, 0;
           0, t I_(d-1), T;
           0, T^T, t I_(d-1) ],                       (G1)

where t>=0 and T is an arbitrary real (d-1)-square matrix. An unscaled
left vector q has fixed coefficient vector (q_l0,q_lT,0), and an unscaled
right vector q has fixed coefficient vector alpha*(-q_r0,0,q_rT).
Use these coefficients to define X_i and g_i at every queried sample,
and X_n at the output. Then (G1) is exactly common squared scale t with
the two child axes identified in opposite directions. For t>0,
K(t,T)>=0 iff ||T/t||_op<=1, by completing the block square. It allows
every transverse contraction simultaneously.

Append TWO further formal vectors z_opt and v_end, with entirely free
Gram entries, and impose PSD on the whole enlarged matrix Gamma:

 Gamma=[K(t,T), k_z, k_v;
        k_z^T, d_z, d_zv;
        k_v^T, d_zv, d_v] >=0.                        (G2)

Set X_star=z_opt,g_star=0,f_star=0, and g_n=v_end. Take all other f_i
as unrestricted scalar variables. The model maximizes2 f_n subject to

 ||X_0-z_opt||^2<=1,
 f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0
       for every distinct i,j in {star,0,...,n}.       (G3)

Every inner product in(G3) is a linear expression in Gamma. In particular
the optimum constraints are BOTH f_i<=<g_i,X_i-z_opt>-||g_i||^2/2
and f_i>=||g_i||^2/2. Nothing sets the first bound to equality.
The terminal gradient is an actual free Gram vector with all its rows.
No initial-radius equality inherited from the projection function is
imposed: the distance is to the modeled common optimum.

Proof of losslessness within the specified vector-template family:
given an actual member, translate its geometric template origin to0,
divide all positions/gradients by its positive distance to the chosen
minimizer, and divide its values relative to the minimum by the squared
distance. The bridge and word are unchanged. The Gram of the scaled
frames, optimum, and output gradient satisfies(G1)-(G3). Conversely,
factor any PSD Gamma into actual vectors in finite dimension. At t>0,
the two normalized frames are isometric copies and their cross block is
T/t; the opposite-axis bridge relation and every GD update follow from
the fixed coefficients. The full interpolation construction in accepted
1252b8823bb5a290, applied after translation by z_opt, gives ONE globally
convex1-smooth function realizing all these jets and minimized at z_opt.
The same function governs the entire run. Thus the model is not a
relaxation due to a missing geometric or interpolation condition.

The enlargement(G2), rather than independent pairwise norm bounds,
retains all PSD range conditions. It neither projects z_opt or v_end
into the child span nor restricts their mutual residual components.
Conversely any isometries in any ambient dimension with the prescribed
opposite-axis bridge have the cross block in(G1), by Cauchy--Schwarz;
there is no hidden ambient-dimension restriction.

For completeness, t=0 is an included degenerate zero-trajectory case.
PSD forces T=0 and every mixed entry against the child-frame block to
vanish. All queried positions and gradients and the output position are0.
The full interpolation inequalities then force f_n=0 and v_end=0.
It is represented by a constant function and is not a positive-scale
realization of the prescribed nonzero template. In the63 model(C63)
therefore implies that the conic program has only this degenerate
geometric face and objective0; it should NOT be called an infeasible SDP.

A chosen minimizer is enough for the original W normalization. If a
constructed function has a closer minimizer, using z_opt gives a
conservative lower bound on its true-distance normalized payoff. The31
calibration below proves that0 is actually closest, so equality is known
there. No free shift of a minimizer is treated as a free gain in payoff.

There is also a direct finite-bound check on this normalization. Adding
the two star rows at sample0 gives ||g_0||^2<=<g_0,X_0-z_opt>.
Since ||g_0||^2=t, Cauchy--Schwarz and the distance constraint give t<=1
when t>0. Hence ||X_0||<=R and ||z_opt||<=R+1. Every output position
has bounded norm from its fixed coefficients, and the two output-star
rows give0<=f_n<=||X_n-z_opt||^2/2. Thus neither moving the optimum nor
varying the common scale creates an unbounded objective in this model.

## 4. Difference constraints and the exact height identity

For fixed actual points and gradients, including an actual zero-gradient
star point, put

 c_ij=<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

The original constraints are f_j>=f_i+c_ij, with directed edge i->j.
Their feasibility is equivalent to every directed cycle having total
c-weight<=0. Necessity is summation. For sufficiency, let l_i be the
maximum weight over all paths from i to star, allowing the empty star
path. Removing a nonpositive cycle never decreases a path weight, so
the maximum is finite and attained among simple paths; l_star=0.
Concatenating an edge with a path gives l_i>=c_ij+l_j. Hence f_i=-l_i
satisfies every original inequality and f_star=0. The accepted
interpolation construction then supplies a real global function.

For the following queried-only separator it is convenient to use the
geometric origin, which need not be a minimizer, and define

 aheight_i=f_i-<g_i,X_i>+||g_i||^2/2,
 Y_i=X_i-g_i,
 N_ij=<g_i-g_j,Y_j>.

Direct expansion, without any assumption on the values, gives

 Q_ij=aheight_i-aheight_j+N_ij.                       (H)

If X_j=g_j, then Y_j=0. Two such queried endpoints therefore have equal
aheight, since their two original Q rows are opposite differences.
This equality is forced by geometry, not a projection-value prescription.

More generally, fix endpoint anchors e_L,e_R in two children, both with
zero residual. For any left queried j and right queried i, add Q_(i,j),
Q_(j,e_L),Q_(e_L,e_R), and the rows of a right-internal path e_R->i.
The values cancel. The two anchor-receiving normals are zero, leaving

 N_(i,j)+sum_(right path) N_ab<=0.                    (P)

This is the exact internal path price that a positive cross normal must
pay after values are released. In particular the sender's height cannot
be lowered without paying its internal interpolation paths. No star
constraint or final-completion assertion enters(P).

## 5. The six original rows and their exact cancellation

Apply(H) to the queried indices36,30,31,39,38,37 in this order. Both31
and39 are endpoints with zero residual, so N_(30,31)=N_(31,39)=0.
The right samples36,...,39 are exactly alpha_3 times an isometric copy
of the retained samples4,...,7 in the child. Those child samples are
alpha_0 times an isometric copy of the atomic samples0,...,3.
Their three return-path normals, from the explicit atomic data, are

 N_(39,38)=alpha_3^2 alpha_0^2 <W-V,y_2>
         =-alpha_3^2 alpha_0^2 d_atom,
 N_(38,37)=alpha_3^2 alpha_0^2 <V-U,y_1>=0,
 N_(37,36)=0,                                        (R)

since <V-U,y_1>=Klen*eta-z_atom=0 and g_0=g_1=U.
Summing(H) now gives the exact value-free identity

 sum_(six displayed rows) Q
       =N_(36,30)-alpha_3^2 alpha_0^2 d_atom.         (S)

It is essential that31 and39 are queried at horizon63. Neither is
eligible for incoming-row deletion under1252b8823bb5a290.

## 6. A uniform angular bound for the cross term

This section rederives the bound directly; the earlier retained-value
separator is not imported. Let P be the actual child initial point and
v=G_4 its retained fourth gradient. Because the FIRST transverse block
was zero,

 <P,v>=-r_0 D m/R^2, ||v||=alpha_0,
 D=<p,W>=rho*z_atom+eps^2.                            (A1)

Indeed the first join has cross pairing
<L_0 u,alpha_0 R_0 v'>=-r_0<u,W><p,v'>/R^2;
take u=p,v'=U. All later LEFT child embeddings preserve these pairings.
Put q=-<P,v>/R^2=r_0 D m/R^4, and
Tperp=||v-(<P,v>/R^2)P||<=alpha_0.

Write the last atomic leaf of the LEFT31 child as beta J for an isometry
J. Its endpoint is beta J W and its receiving residual is beta J y_2.
Let h_angle=<W,y_2>=rho e+eps^2. Orthogonally decompose

 y_2=(h_angle/N)W+tperp,
 <tperp,W>=0,
 ||tperp||=(a-1)eps sqrt(eta/N).                     (A2)

The norm follows by projecting (a-1)V perpendicular to W, using
||V||^2-<V,W>^2/N=eta eps^2/N. The top bridge sends the right P axis
to the negative left endpoint axis, so its contribution to
<alpha_3 R_3 v,L_3 beta J y_2> is r_3 beta^2 h_angle q.
The remaining contribution is at least
-alpha_3 Tperp beta||tperp||, by Cauchy--Schwarz in the actual common
ambient space. Thus EVERY transverse contraction satisfies

 N_(36,30)/(beta^2 e)>=r_3 Hangle-1,
 Hangle=(rho+eps^2/e)q
      -[(a-1)eps sqrt(eta)/(R e)]Tperp.              (A3)

Here the subtracted1 is exactly the receiving normal energy
<G_30,Z_30-G_30>/(beta^2 e), not a function-value choice.

All numerical-looking constants in the next estimates are rational
inequalities proved by the displayed elementary bounds. Squaring gives
41/29<sqrt2<99/70, hence the actual atom lies in

 7/5<a<283/200, 2<b<401/200,
 99/100<rho<=1, 0<=eps<=1/100.

They imply

 29/5<A<1167/200<6, 17/5<m<171/50,
 239/100<Klen<483/200, 1<z_atom<201/200,
 2/5<eta<201/478, e>1/2,
 R^2<564943/40000<71/5, R>17/5>3,
 N<421/1000<4/9, D>99/100.

Also12/29<theta<5/12, so12/5<r_0<5/2 and
alpha_0<25/51<1/2. Therefore

 q>[(12/5)(99/100)(17/5)]/(71/5)^2
   =5049/126025>1/25.

The first term of Hangle exceeds99/2500. The second term is less than
[(1/2)(1/100)*1*(1/2)]/[3*(1/2)]=1/600. Consequently

 Hangle>99/2500-1/600>3/80.

Since r_3=lambda^2 A>(29/5)^2=841/25, equation(A3) yields

 N_(36,30)/(beta^2 e)>523/2000.                      (A4)

This inequality concerns geometric normals only. It does not yet assert
that Q_(36,30)>0 when the values are free; equation(S) supplies the
required elimination of those values.

## 7. Paying the released-height return path

Use alpha_3=r_3 beta sqrt(N)/R and alpha_0=r_0 sqrt(N)/R. Because
r_3 r_0=lambda A^2, the exact ratio of the return-path price is

 alpha_3^2 alpha_0^2 d_atom/(beta^2 e)
      =lambda^2 kappa^2 (d_atom/e).                 (A5)

The atomic equality rho=599/600 gives d_atom/e=1/600-eps^2/e<1/600.
It is positive because e>1/2 and eps^2=1/10000. The bounds above give

 kappa=A^2 N/R^2<400/289<7/5,
 lambda^2<6,
 lambda^2 kappa^2<294/25<12.

Hence(A5)<1/50. Combining(S),(A4),(A5) proves(C63) for t=1.
Common spatial scale sqrt(t) multiplies each N_ij and beta^2 e by t;
the heights still telescope for arbitrary new values. Common translation
does not change the original c_ij cycle weight. Therefore(C63) holds
for every t>0 and every translated realization as stated.

The contradiction is with a sum of six ORIGINAL inequalities, each of
which must be nonpositive. It requires no numerical optimizer, no
assumption of projection values, no projected optimum, and no terminal
jet. This resolves the concrete new63 released-value model analytically.

## 8. Analytical31 calibration and the benchmark payoff

Appendix A reproduces the exact seed/7/15/31 interpolation construction
and its integer-interval tables as a joint draft. It does not treat its
old global-memory finding as an accepted predecessor. In that
construction every normal <g_i-g_j,y_j> is nonpositive and every
<g_i,y_i> is nonnegative. Thus, taking

 Kpoly=conv(0,G_0,...,G_31),
 Fpoly(x)=max_(v in Kpoly)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2),

the maximizing gradient is the Euclidean projection onto Kpoly and
interpolates every prescribed sample. The proof that this is a finite
convex1-smooth function is included in Appendix A. The initial vector
retains p=mU+aV+W, a positive combination of queried gradients. The
minimizer set is the polar of Kpoly; consequently0 is a closest
minimizer and the true initial distance is exactly R.

In the31 version of(G1)-(G3), set t=1/R^2, T=C_2/R^2, z_opt=0,
take v_end=G_31/R in the represented actual frame, and set
f_i=(<Z_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2)/R^2. This supplies the analytical
calibration point. All star constraints, terminal constraints, the
distance constraint, and the entire PSD completion are accounted for.
This is a calibration by explicit real vectors, not by a solver status.

The capacity identity A_(j+1)=lambda A_j follows by summing the physical
word. The endpoint norm identity is N_(j+1)=r_j^2 N_j^2/R^2.
Thus kappa_j=A_j^2 N_j/R^2 satisfies kappa_(j+1)=kappa_j^2, because
lambda*theta=1. At j=3 the actual normalized full objective is

 2Fpoly(Z_31)/R^2=N_3/R^2=kappa^8/(lambda^3 A)^2.

Its exact B_31-normalized payoff is

 B_31 kappa^8/(lambda^3 A)^2,

where B_31=2S_bullet(31)+1 is the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark in
accepted b98bb807d514e80d. No shifted-sum/core-gradient quantity is
substituted for B_31. This calibration is not the optimum of the
released31 model, and no all-function upper bound is claimed. There is
no positive-scale63 candidate in the defined template family, so there
is no63 payoff to extrapolate into a uniform family.

## 9. What must change next

Equation(P) exposes the missing boundary information after releasing
projection values: each queried sender has an actual internal return-path
price. Here it is only alpha_3^2 alpha_0^2 d_atom, too small to cover the
forced cross normal. Arbitrary scalar height choices cannot enlarge it.

A continuation must change at least one ingredient of this explicit
cycle: the fixed first7 coupling and its endpoint-facing gradient; the
atomic queried gradient/position data governing d_atom; the source-matched
bridge relation; or the residual-zero endpoint anchors. Merely changing
the optimum, terminal jet, heights, or the free top16-by16 transverse
matrix leaves(C63) intact. This is a necessary interface for a new
construction, not a claim that any one proposed change suffices.

The two defined word/vector templates are the mandated actual31 child
and its63 parent. No new atom or schedule was introduced. The optional
four-evaluation/60-second allowance remains unused. The original target
is open, and this joint candidate has not passed fact_submit.

## Appendix A. Joint reconstruction of the31 calibration

This appendix contains the full finite interpolation check needed for
the analytical calibration. Its interval tables are copied defining
evidence from the existing exact31 manuscript, without executing their
source or recalculating them. The logical arithmetic enclosure procedure
is stated explicitly below. This is part of the present UNVERIFIED joint
candidate; those inherited tables have not acquired verifier status.
The63 separator in sections4-7 does not need any table in this appendix.

### A1. Atomic normal list, function, and distance

Use exactly the atom in section2. Its residuals are
y_0=(m-1)U+Klen V+eps*(0,0,1),
y_1=z_atom U+Klen V+eps*(0,0,1),
y_2=(Klen-1)V+eps*(0,0,1),y_3=0.
Its normal energies are(m-1,z_atom,(Klen-1)eta,0).
At receiving index0, the vertices0,V,W give respectively
-(m-1),-a,-a-(1-rho)z_atom+eps^2; vertex U gives0.
At receiving index1, vertices0,V,W give
-z_atom,0,-(1-rho)z_atom+eps^2; vertex U gives0.
At receiving index2, vertices0,U both give-(Klen-1)eta,
vertex W gives-(1-rho)(Klen-1)eta+eps^2, and V gives0.
At receiving index3 every normal is0. The elementary estimates in
section6 give e>1/2,z_atom>1 and rho=599/600, so both possible small
positive terms eps^2 are strictly smaller than their subtracted terms.
Thus all atomic normals are nonpositive, and all energies nonnegative.

For any finite vector/position data with these two properties set
Kpoly=conv(0,g_0,...,g_n) and
Fpoly(x)=max_(v in Kpoly)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2).
Completing the square shows that the unique maximizing vector is the
Euclidean projection P_Kpoly(x). The normal inequalities at x_i prove
P_Kpoly(x_i)=g_i. If p=P_Kpoly(x) and q=P_Kpoly(y), the two projection
inequalities give ||p-q||^2<=<p-q,x-y>; hence this projection is
1-Lipschitz. Evaluating each defining maximum at the other's maximizing
vector bounds the differentiability remainder between0 and||x-y||^2.
Thus Fpoly is finite, convex, differentiable, and1-smooth, with gradient
P_Kpoly. At the data points its values are
f_i^proj=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.
Expanding the original Q gives Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>,
Q_(i,star)=0, and Q_(star,i)=-<g_i,y_i>. This covers both optimum
directions, not only the finite/finite normals.

The minimizer set is Kpoly^polar={u:<u,v><=0 for all v in Kpoly}.
For necessity, use tv in the defining maximum for0<t<=1, divide by t,
and let t decrease to0; sufficiency follows by evaluating the maximum.
The identity p=mU+aV+W is a positive combination of retained gradients.
It persists through every later left embedding. Thus <p,u><=0 for any
minimizer u, and ||p-u||^2>=||p||^2 with equality at u=0. This proves
the exact closest-minimizer distance R at every finite calibrated join.

### A2. The seven-step join

For C_0=0 the exact cross pairing of section2 is
<L_0 u,alpha_0 R_0 v>=-r_0<u,W><p,v>/R^2.
All seed coordinates in the four lists g_i,y_i,p,W are nonnegative.
Therefore each cross inner product between a gradient and a residual
is nonpositive. Subtracting the receiving energy proves every cross
normal. All within-child and star rows are atomic rows or their
alpha_0^2 multiples. Applying A1 realizes the full7 word, including
both endpoint directions, with the retained projection values.

### A4. Exact enclosure procedure specifying the tables

For the copied15 tables use Dint=10^18, and for the31 tables use
Dint=10^24, as in their original recorded calculations. Represent a real
enclosure by two integers[l,u], meaning[l/Dint,u/Dint]. Integers k are
[kDint,kDint].
Addition adds endpoints; negation maps[l,u] to[-u,-l]. Multiplication
takes the min and max of the four integer endpoint products, divides
by Dint, and rounds outwards. Division by an interval avoiding0 takes
the min and max of the four endpoint quotients a*Dint/b and rounds
outwards. Square root maps a nonnegative interval to
[floor(sqrt(lDint)),ceil(sqrt(uDint))], using integer squaring to fix
the endpoints. Each operation encloses the exact result by endpoint
monotonicity or by taking all endpoint extrema.

For the15 rows it suffices to evaluate the exact atom, B(W),B(p),
alpha_0, all7 vectors, then B(w_1),B(p_1),alpha_1 and(AT). For the31
rows one additional square root is needed for the actual15 input:
H_1=(I-C_1^T C_1)^(1/2). Let Dmat=C_1^T C_1. Its operator norm is at
most934/10000<1/10. The scalar binomial series and real symmetric
diagonalization give

 H_1=I-sum_(k>=1)c_k Dmat^k,
 c_1=1/2, c_(k+1)=c_k(2k-1)/(2k+2), 0<c_k<1.

After20 terms the omitted tail has operator norm at most
sum_(k>=21)(1/10)^k=1/(9*10^20). This also bounds every entry.
Evaluate those20 matrix powers with the outward operations and add
this rational radius to every entry. Form the EXACTLY prescribed15
vectors with these H_1 enclosures, then their two Householder frames,
alpha_2 and both directions of(AT). Normalize by the explicit positive
energies and round each upper endpoint upwards to the grid1/10000.
The resulting bounds are the four displayed tables. No spectral
truncation or alternate Gram square root is substituted.

Every denominator is separated from0 by the explicit nonzero axes and
positive energies; the source's complete interval endpoints are in
max_changed_geometry31_exact_replay.json. A1-A2 prove the exact zero
rows without interval tests. At15 the96 nonzero cross rows are strictly
negative, with largest upper bound-2/10000; the remaining32 cross rows
are zero. At31 the384 nonzero cross rows have largest upper bound
-4037/10000; the remaining128 are zero. The512 within-child rows and65
optimum incidences including the diagonal total1089 original31 rows
with the512 cross rows. All are covered by A1-A4.

Thus the calibration invokes an explicit global interpolant with all
original rows and an exact radius, as used in section8. The present
round copies the already recorded finite rational enclosure evidence
and its complete reasoning; it does not claim to have executed or
independently verified the arithmetic replay.

# Value prices, all-map cycles, and the translated-join boundary

Status: **UNVERIFIED JOINT PROOF CANDIDATE**, worker max, cumulative round25.
This is the second and last round of the eleventh launch, cap25. TASK.md and
master_guidance5e2fd47b60222794 allocate no fact_submit calls. No optimization,
new model evaluation, exact arithmetic replay, installation or sub-agent was
used. No assertion below is a newly accepted fact. All new algebra is proved
in this one document; earlier unverified findings are motivation, not premises.

Accepted inputs are1252b8823bb5a290 for the original smooth-convex interpolation
inequalities, terminal completion and explicit global interpolant, and
b98bb807d514e80d for the ORIGINAL benchmark B_n and its bound
B_n<=1+4*n^(log_2(3)). Neither a projection-value prescription nor an old
projection-only separator is assumed.

## 1. Results and scope

For the fixed three-vector atom and source-matched balanced physical words
defined below, allow arbitrary, separately chosen isometric embeddings at
EVERY internal node, in any finite dimension. In particular the first7
coupling need not be zero, and different occurrences of the same child need
not have the same coupling. Retain the specified queried gradients and GD
positions. Allow every function value to be reselected for the whole known
horizon, an arbitrary actual common minimizer with honest distance, and an
entirely free actual terminal gradient.

(I) Every legal31-step realization in this class has

  B_31 * 2(f(x_31)-min f) / dist(x_0,argmin f)^2
       < 47/200 < 1/4.                                      (1)

Thus even all these freedoms cannot make this specific geometric family
reach one quarter of the ORIGINAL function-gap benchmark. This is an upper
bound on the performance of the prescribed family of realizations, NOT an
upper bound on unrestricted W_31 of its word. The terminal gradient is not
set equal to the prescribed endpoint position in this assertion.

(II) No positive-scale127-step realization in this source-matched family is
interpolable at all, even just at its queried samples. A positive combination
of three cycles of ORIGINAL queried inequalities is strictly greater than
22/25 times a positive receiving energy. All free values cancel exactly.
This extends the retained-geometry exclusion to simultaneous changes of ALL
transverse maps and ALL function values. It does not change the atom or allow
relative translations of children. No claim is made that every free-map63
geometry is impossible; the earlier fixed-first-map63 draft had a different
restriction and is not used here.

(III) Relative translations genuinely change the model. We derive their exact
cross-row/anchor conditions and give a legal scalar example with a nonzero
translation and fully priced minimizer. It disproves the proposed shortcut
that zero-residual child endpoints force every relative translation to vanish.
It supplies no target-relevant63 construction or uniform large-horizon family.

These statements concern one static global function for each given horizon.
There is no alteration of a function or values during a GD run. The original
all-schedule asymptotic target remains unresolved.

## 2. Atom, physical words, and every allowed isometry

Use s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1=1/lambda and

  a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300, rho=599/600, eps=1/100,
  Klen=a+rho, z_atom=b-1, eta=z_atom/Klen, m=a+b,
  A=1+2a+b=m+a+1.

In R^3 define

  U=(1,0,0), V=(0,sqrt(eta),0), W=(0,rho*sqrt(eta),eps),
  (g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(U,U,V,W),
  (x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3)=(mU+aV+W,bU+aV+W,aV+W,W).

The atomic word is h[0]=(a,b,a). Its initial vector is p=x_0. Define

  R^2=||p||^2=m^2+Klen*z_atom+eps^2,
  N=||W||^2=rho^2*eta+eps^2,
  D=<p,W>=rho*z_atom+eps^2,
  kappa=A^2*N/R^2,
  y_1=z_atom*U+Klen*V+eps*(0,0,1),
  y_2=(a-1)*V+W,
  e=<V,y_2>=(Klen-1)*eta,
  d=(1-rho)*e-eps^2=e/600-eps^2.                      (2)

Here y_i=x_i-g_i. Direct substitution gives

  <V-U,y_1>=0,
  <W-V,y_2>=-d,
  y_3=0.                                               (3)

Let h[j+1]=(h[j],1+r_j,h[j]), A_j=lambda^j*A and
r_j=theta*A_j. These are the actual physical words: h[j] has length
n_j=4*2^j-1 and 1+sum h[j]=A_j, because 2+theta=lambda.

At each internal node with two depth-j children, let P_R be the right initial
vector and w_L the left endpoint. Every child has initial norm R. Its
prescribed endpoint has norm sqrt(N_j), where N_0=N. Choose ANY two linear
isometries Lmap,Rmap into ANY common finite-dimensional Euclidean space with

  alpha_j*Rmap(P_R)=-r_j*Lmap(w_L),
  alpha_j=r_j*sqrt(N_j)/R.                              (4)

Embed the left positions/gradients by Lmap and the right ones by
alpha_j*Rmap. Equation(4) is precisely the GD bridge from x=g=w_L with
step1+r_j. It imposes no other transverse restriction. All earlier choices
may differ between nodes. Values are not prescribed. The next initial norm
is still R; its prescribed endpoint norm squared is

  N_(j+1)=alpha_j^2*N_j=r_j^2*N_j^2/R^2,
  kappa_j=A_j^2*N_j/R^2=kappa^(2^j),
  alpha_j^2=theta^2*kappa^(2^j).                         (5)

The last equality follows from the first two and lambda*theta=1, starting
from j=0. The last atomic leaf of a depth-j child is scaled by
beta_j=product_(0<=k<j)alpha_k, so N_j=beta_j^2*N.

Every prescribed endpoint that later participates in GD remains queried
with x=g. Only the final gradient of the WHOLE word is free. In particular
when studying horizon31, x_31 is the prescribed endpoint vector of squared
norm N_3, but its actual gradient need not be that vector. This distinction
does not change any earlier query or update.

A common positive spatial scale c and common translation b0 do not change
the normalized payoff: replace f by (f(b0+c*x)-min f)/c^2. Its gradient
at x is grad f(b0+c*x)/c and its minimizer distances scale by1/c. Thus
the subsequent unit-left-scale calculations cover all positive common
scalings and translations as well.

The actual rational31 template of the earlier rounds is a member of this
family (its first map is zero and the next two were specified rationally).
The proofs here are uniform over the larger family and do not need those
rational entries, their numerical feasibility, or their interval tables.

## 3. Elementary bounds, with rational checks

Squaring proves 41/29<s<99/70. Substitution in the two displayed affine
expressions for a,b gives

  7/5<a<283/200, 2<b<401/200,
  29/5<A<1167/200<6, 17/5<m<171/50,
  239/100<Klen<483/200, 1<z_atom<201/200,
  2/5<eta<201/478,
  1/2<e<3/5,
  279/20<R^2<564943/40000<71/5,
  N<421/1000<4/9, D>99/100.                            (6)

For e, its lower bound follows from
(Klen-1)*eta>(139/100)*(2/5)>1/2. Its upper bound is at most
(283/200)*(201/478)=56883/95600<3/5. For R^2 the lower bound is
(17/5)^2+239/100=279/20; the upper bound follows by adding
(171/50)^2+(483/200)*(201/200)+1/10000. For N use
rho<=1 and201/478+1/10000<421/1000. Consequently

  0<d<1/1000,
  12/29<theta<5/12,
  12/5<r_0<5/2,
  alpha_0<25/51<1/2.                                   (7)

The lower bound on d follows from e>1/2 and
1/1200-1/10000>0. The alpha_0 estimate uses sqrt(N)<2/3 and R>17/5.

For later payoff bounds, (6) gives

  kappa < (1167/200)^2*(421/1000)/(279/20)
         =573355269/558000000 <21/20,
  kappa^4<5/4, kappa^8<3/2,
  alpha_2^2=theta^2*kappa^4<125/576<1/4,
  A_3=lambda^3*A>(12/5)^3*(29/5)>80.                   (8)

The finite powers in (8) can be checked as
21^4=194481<200000=(5/4)*20^4 and
21^8=37822859361<38400000000=(3/2)*20^8.
No arithmetic program was run for these displayed algebraic checks.

## 4. Free heights: exact original inequalities and their price

Take ANY actual convex1-smooth function realizing the specified queried
geometry and possessing a minimizer z. Subtract its minimum from all values,
so f_star=0. The geometric origin need not be z. For a queried sample define

  aheight_i=f_i-<g_i,x_i>+||g_i||^2/2,
  y_i=x_i-g_i,
  N_ij=<g_i-g_j,y_j>.

Direct expansion of the ORIGINAL convention in1252b8823bb5a290 gives

  Q_ij=aheight_i-aheight_j+N_ij,
  Q_(i,star)=aheight_i+<g_i,z>,
  Q_(star,i)=-aheight_i-<g_i,y_i>.                            (9)

All must be nonpositive. If two queried samples have residual zero,
their two original pair rows force their heights to be equal. Call the
common value aheight_common. Their star lower rows also give aheight_common>=0.

In an atomic leaf scaled by gamma, whose endpoint is queried, the rows
from each sample into that endpoint give aheight_i<=aheight_common. The reverse path from the
endpoint through samples2,1,0, using (3), gives

  aheight_common-gamma^2*d<=aheight_2<=aheight_common,
  aheight_common-gamma^2*d<=aheight_0=aheight_1<=aheight_common.                             (10)

Indeed Q_(3,2)=aheight_common-aheight_2-gamma^2*d<=0,
Q_(2,1)=aheight_2-aheight_1<=0, and Q_(1,0)=aheight_1-aheight_0<=0.
The reverse row Q_(0,1) gives aheight_0=aheight_1 because those gradients coincide.
Every row used is inside the queried prefix; no free terminal row is used.

Consider ANY entirely queried7-step subtree scaled by gamma. Its exact
first-bridge vector identity is

  r_0*g_3 + m*g_4 + a*g_6 + g_7=0.                    (11)

Here the local indices refer to this subtree. Its right leaf has scale
gamma*alpha_0. Multiply the star upper rows in(9) by r_0,m,a,1 and add.
Their z terms cancel by(11). Applying(10) to the right leaf yields

  (A+r_0)*aheight_common<=(A-1)*gamma^2*alpha_0^2*d.

Thus define the exact height price

  K_height=((A-1)/(A+r_0))*alpha_0^2*d;
  0<=aheight_common<=gamma^2*K_height.                              (12)

This is forced by actual optimum rows and actual internal interpolation.
It is not an imposed value prescription. As A+r_0=sqrt(2)*A,
A<6 and sqrt(2)>7/5 give

  (A-1)/(A+r_0)<25/42,
  K_height< (25/42)*(1/4)*(1/1000)=1/6720.             (13)

There is a simultaneous cost for changing the minimizer. In the first
atomic leaf, p=m*g_0+a*g_2+g_3. Multiply its three star upper rows by
m,a,1 and use(10):

  -<p,z> >= A*aheight_common-(A-1)*d.

Since the initial vector is p up to an isometry,

  ||x_0-z||^2
     >=R^2+2*A*aheight_common-2*(A-1)*d+||z||^2
     >R^2-1/100.                                      (14)

This holds for EVERY minimizer z, including components outside the child
span. Hence it bounds the distance to the entire minimizer set. Its infimum
still exceeds R^2-1/100 because the stronger uniform bound
R^2-2*(A-1)*d already has a strict positive gap from that value.

Finally, take any queried zero-residual anchor u=x_i=g_i. Smoothness at that
actual sample gives, for every x in the ambient space,

  f(x)<=f_i+<u,x-u>+||x-u||^2/2=||x||^2/2+aheight_common.          (15)

This bound controls a FREE final gradient as well as a prescribed one.
It follows from an actual queried jet; it deletes no incoming query row.

## 5. Applying the price to the ORIGINAL31-step function gap

The depth3 word has queried samples0,...,30. Its subtree at indices16,...,23
is an entirely queried7-step child scaled by gamma=alpha_2. Its endpoints19
and23 are queried and have residual zero. Equations(8),(12),(13) give

  0<=aheight_common<1/26880.                                        (16)

The final position has squared norm N_3 even if the actual final gradient is
chosen freely. By(15), 2(f(x_31)-min f)<=N_3+2*aheight_common. Equations(5),(8) give

  N_3/R^2=kappa^8/A_3^2<3/12800.

From accepted b98bb807d514e80d, writing p_exp=log_2(3),

  B_31<=1+4*31^p_exp<1+4*32^p_exp=973.                 (17)

By(6),(14), the true initial squared distance exceeds R^2-1/100, and

  R^2/(R^2-1/100)<1001/1000,
  R^2-1/100>697/50.

Consequently the contribution of the geometric endpoint norm to the
B_31-normalized gap is less than

  (2919/12800)*(1001/1000)<229/1000,

where 2919*1001=2921919<2931200=229*12800. The contribution of the
free common height is less than

  973/(13440*(697/50))=48650/9367680<6/1000,

where 48650000<56206080. Adding proves (1), with no assumption on the actual
terminal gradient, its span, or the position of any minimizer. All other
interpolation rows can only narrow this family, so no omitted row can
invalidate this conditional performance upper bound.

This is NOT an estimate of W_31. It says these particular queried vectors
cannot produce a strong witness, even after values and normalization are
optimized. The earlier actual31 projection realization remains only a draft
calibration; this proof does not use its feasibility as a premise.

## 6. A convex sender independent of EVERY first coupling

Now consider a purported depth5,127-step realization. Its two depth4
children have63 steps each. Denote the initial vector of the right child by
P, and its first atomic endpoint by W_first, with ||P||=R and
<P,W_first>=D. Inside its first7 subtree, the exact bridge relation gives

  m*G_4+a*G_6+G_7=-r_0*W_first.

The coefficients (m,a,1)/A are positive and sum to1, so

  v=(m*G_4+a*G_6+G_7)/A=-theta*W_first,
  q=-<P,v>/R^2=theta*D/R^2,
  T_perp=||v-(<P,v>/R^2)*P||<=theta*sqrt(N).            (18)

All later left embeddings preserve these identities. They depend on no
transverse coupling, including the first. This is why a convex sender is
needed in place of the single G_4 from the older63 separator.

Put beta=beta_4. The last atomic leaf of the LEFT63 child is an isometric
image scaled by beta. Its endpoint, receiving gradient and receiving
residual are respectively beta*W,beta*V,beta*y_2, up to that isometry.
Its receiving energy is E_L=beta^2*e>0. The top bridge has

  r=r_4=lambda^3*A,
  alpha=alpha_4=r*beta*sqrt(N)/R.

Write h_angle=<W,y_2>=rho*e+eps^2. The component of y_2 perpendicular to W
has norm

  ||y_2-(h_angle/N)W||=(a-1)*eps*sqrt(eta/N).           (19)

To prove(19), project (a-1)V perpendicular to W and use
||V||^2-<V,W>^2/N=eta*eps^2/N.

Under the top isometries, define the weighted receiving normal

  N_bar=<alpha*Rmap(v)-Lmap(beta*V), Lmap(beta*y_2)>.

The bridge sends P to minus the endpoint direction. Its axial contribution
to the first inner product is r*beta^2*h_angle*q. The transverse contribution
is bounded below by Cauchy--Schwarz and(19), in the ACTUAL common space.
Subtracting the receiving energy gives

  N_bar/E_L >= r*H_angle-1,
  H_angle=(rho+eps^2/e)*q
     -((a-1)*eps*sqrt(eta)/(R*e))*T_perp.              (20)

This is a bound on a geometric normal, not yet on a Q row with free values.
The exact value elimination is supplied in the next section.

From theta>2/5 and(6),

  q>99/3550, T_perp<1/3.

Thus the positive term in H_angle is greater than9801/355000; using
a-1<1/2, eps=1/100, sqrt(eta)<1, R>3, e>1/2 and T_perp<1/3,
the subtracted term is less than1/900. Hence

  H_angle>9801/355000-1/900>1/40.                      (21)

For the last inequality, 1/40+1/900=47/1800 and
9801*1800=17641800>16685000=47*355000.
Also r=lambda^3*A>80 by(8), so

  N_bar/E_L>1.                                        (22)

## 7. Three original cycles eliminate every free value

Global indices64,...,127 belong to the right child. Its retained first-right
atomic leaf has indices68,...,71 and total scale alpha_4*alpha_0.
Use the three queried cycles

  C_68 = Q_(68,62)+Q_(62,63)+Q_(63,71)
                         +Q_(71,70)+Q_(70,69)+Q_(69,68),
  C_70 = Q_(70,62)+Q_(62,63)+Q_(63,71)+Q_(71,70),
  C_71 = Q_(71,62)+Q_(62,63)+Q_(63,71).

Every index is at most71 and therefore queried at horizon127. The positive
weights m/A,a/A,1/A sum to1. Define

  S_cycle=(m/A)*C_68+(a/A)*C_70+(1/A)*C_71.             (23)

In each cycle the heights in(9) telescope. The receiving residuals at63
and71 are zero, and the atomic return normals are exactly(3), scaled by
alpha_4^2*alpha_0^2. Consequently

  S_cycle=N_bar-((A-1)/A)*alpha_4^2*alpha_0^2*d.       (24)

There is no prescribed value in(24). It is an identity for arbitrary
real values at the displayed samples.

Because r_4*r_0=lambda^2*A^2, the price relative to E_L is

  ((A-1)/A)*alpha_4^2*alpha_0^2*d/E_L
    =((A-1)/A)*lambda^4*kappa^2*(d/e).

Here d/e<1/600, lambda^2<6 and kappa<21/20<7/5. The price is therefore
strictly less than

  36*(49/25)/600=1764/15000<3/25.                     (25)

Equations(22),(24),(25) prove S_cycle>(22/25)*E_L>0. Each original Q in
this positive combination would have to be nonpositive for an actual
smooth-convex function. This is the desired contradiction. It uses neither
the final output nor an optimum, so terminal or minimizer freedom cannot
affect it. No feasibility of an intermediate child was presumed.

A common spatial scale c>0 multiplies every cycle normal and E_L by c^2.
A common translation leaves the original cycle weights unchanged. Thus no
positive-scale translated copy of the whole specified geometry escapes.
At c=0 the trivial constant-function face survives; a conic model including
that face must not be described as wholly infeasible.

## 8. A genuinely different join: relative translations

This section is a direct screen of an uncovered change, not an assertion
that it repairs the actual63 instance. Take two fixed child transcripts with
initial point p_R and left endpoint x=g=w_L. Choose isometries Lmap,Rmap and
an arbitrary right scale alpha>0. Put

  u_i=Lmap(g_L,i), Y_i=Lmap(x_L,i-g_L,i),
  v_j=alpha*Rmap(g_R,j), Z_j=alpha*Rmap(x_R,j-g_R,j),
  t_join=-r*Lmap(w_L)-alpha*Rmap(p_R).

Use actual positions Lmap(x_L,i) on the left and
t_join+alpha*Rmap(x_R,j) on the right. The bridge1+r is exact, and all
internal GD updates are unchanged. Introduce intrinsic heights

  aheight_L,i=f_L,i-<u_i,Lmap(x_L,i)>+||u_i||^2/2,
  aheight_R,j=f_R,j-<v_j,alpha*Rmap(x_R,j)>+||v_j||^2/2.

Expanding every original cross row gives exactly

  Q_(L_i,R_j)=aheight_L,i-aheight_R,j+<u_i,t_join>+<u_i-v_j,Z_j>,
  Q_(R_j,L_i)=aheight_R,j-aheight_L,i-<v_j,t_join>+<v_j-u_i,Y_i>. (26)

Within-child rows retain their intrinsic-height expressions. If queried
endpoint anchors have intrinsic residual zero, their heights are common
within each child, denoted aheight_L,aheight_R. Their cross rows are equivalent to

  max_(left anchors u) <u,t_join>
       <= aheight_R-aheight_L
       <= min_(right anchors v) <v,t_join>.             (27)

In particular <u-v,t_join><=0 for each such pair. The direction in(27)
matters: reversing it would incorrectly exclude legal translated examples.

For an actual common minimizer z with value zero, the ORIGINAL star rows
also require

  -<u_i,Y_i> <= aheight_L,i <= -<u_i,z>,
  -<v_j,Z_j> <= aheight_R,j <= <v_j,t_join-z>.               (28)

The initial-distance bound is ||Lmap(p_L)-z||^2, and a free actual final
gradient still needs all terminal rows (or the precisely matched completion
from1252b8823bb5a290). Equations(26)-(28) price a relative translation; it is
not an arbitrary independent offset in the values or optimum.

### A real counterexample to the claim that t_join must vanish

Fix r>0 and 0<q<r. Use two empty scalar children with intrinsic endpoint
position/gradient1. Take the left isometry as identity, the right as sign
reversal and right scale alpha=q. The one physical step is h=1+r. The
actual samples are

  (x_0,g_0)=(1,1), (x_1,g_1)=(-r,-q), t_join=q-r!=0.

Put

  mu=(1+q)/(1+r), z=(q-r)/(1+q),
  f(x)=(mu/2)*(x-z)^2.

Here 0<mu<1, so this is one globally convex1-smooth function with unique
minimizer z. Its gradients at the displayed points are1 and-q, and GD with
step1+r maps1 to-r. For any two samples, including z, its original residual
is -mu*(1-mu)*(x_i-x_j)^2/2<=0; hence all rows hold. The intrinsic heights are

  aheight_L=(r-q)/(2*(1+q)), aheight_R=q^2*aheight_L,
  aheight_R-aheight_L=(q-1)*(r-q)/2.

They satisfy the exact anchor interval
-(r-q)<=aheight_R-aheight_L<=q*(r-q). The TRUE initial distance and payoff are

  |x_0-z|=(1+r)/(1+q)=1/mu,
  2(f(x_1)-min f)/|x_0-z|^2=q^2*mu.                   (29)

This pays the minimizer displacement explicitly. As a small illustration,
r=1,q=1/2 gives z=-1/3, mu=3/4, distance4/3 and payoff3/16; the geometric
distance1 to the old origin would have given a different, incorrect
normalization. If both displayed endpoints must be QUERIED, append one step of size1
on this same quadratic: the displayed samples remain unchanged and both
are queried in the longer run. One may also choose r=theta to match the
balanced bridge excess of two empty children. This example disproves
translation impossibility only. It does not imply that the old multi-gradient children can be translated
compatibly, nor does it improve the all-function worst case of that word.

### What the direct construction attempts do and do not establish

Keeping the old opposite-source alignment and old right scale forces
t_join=0 by the bridge identity, so changing only value constants cannot
produce the new mechanism. Dropping that alignment permits the scalar
example above. For a multigradient child, endpoint interval(27) alone does
not control the residual terms in(26), all of which remain required.
We have neither a compatible full63 translated realization with useful
normalized payoff, nor a separator for all such translated realizations.

For clarity, this is still a concrete finite model if pursued later. A full
Gram block for arbitrary scaled left/right frames is

  [ t_L*I, S; S^T, t_R*I ] >=0,

where I is the identity on each fixed child frame, t_L,t_R>=0 are its
squared scales, and S is the completely unrestricted cross block. Define t_join
as the displayed linear combination of represented vectors, then append
arbitrary optimum and actual terminal vectors in ONE full PSD Gram, impose
all original rows and ||x_0-z||^2<=1, and maximize2f_end. No guessed span or
PSD range condition is discarded. The old source-matched model is the
additional vector equality t_join=0. This formulation has not been solved,
and no numerical allowance is inferred from writing it down. Equations(1)
and(23)-(25), rather than this open model, are the complete new results.

## 9. Literature and handoff

Matlas was queried twice for smooth convex integration, conjugate residuals,
and cycle/convex-combination mechanisms; its hits were unrelated. Web search
located Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur, arXiv1502.05666. The exact already
downloaded paper downloads/1502.05666.txt was read at Theorem4 and proof,
Corollary1, and Remarks1 and3. That literature confirms the relevant regime:
complete smooth-convex interpolation, with values eliminated by all cycles.
No external theorem is imported into this proof;1252b8823bb5a290 already
supplies the exact original conditions and constructive sufficiency. Relative
translations do not introduce a different function class or warrant a new
literature layer before the elementary screen above.

The two plans are dea316c4076eeb64 (all-map released cycles) and
c1f744194935dbeb (translated joins). The first has a complete scoped joint
candidate, supplemented by the original-objective bound(1). The second has
exact conditions and a real counterexample to an overstrong shortcut; its
target-relevant construction remains open. Do not restart the excluded
source-matched family by retuning values, minimizers, final gradients or
transverse maps alone. A relative translation, changed queried atom, or
another actual joining rule is a substantive uncovered change, and must
satisfy all original rows and pay the original normalization.

All new claims remain unverified. No existing fact, shared truth store,
closed numerical ledger or proof gate was changed. Cumulative round25 is
the explicit cap; no additional round or verifier allowance is inferred.
