# A uniform terminal-charge reduction for the chronological birth kernel

Status: **unverified proof draft**, fifth launch, second and final allocated
round. This file gives exact horizon truncation identities, an exact formula
for the constant column, and a proved conditional terminal-charge estimate.
It does **not** prove the remaining coefficient hypotheses. No fact submission
is authorized, so none of the new assertions is a fact-graph building block.

The underlying problem is still the original exact-profile minimax problem.
Even positivity of this entire kernel would only exclude its specified
reciprocal-level temporal family, not resolve the original problem.

## 1. Definitions, with the same original chronological word

Fix m>=128, epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c, and d_k=1+a*c^k.
For this file extend the formula for d_k to every integer k>=0, keeping the
SAME epsilon. The extension is a device for writing coefficients; no new
steps are added to the original trajectory or interpolation constraints.

Let H_0 be empty and H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j). The queried word is H_m with
its final d_0 removed, with q=2^m-2 steps h_i, elapsed times
s_i=sum_(r<i)h_r, and S=s_q. For vectors v,w_0,...,w_(m-1), define

 p_ij=product_(r<i)(1-h_r/d_j),
 g_i=v+sum_j p_ij*w_j,  x_i=x_0-sum_(r<i)h_r*g_r.

All vectors use one arbitrary common real Gram matrix. The first physical
d_j kills its modal column, so g_q=v and
x_q=x_0-S*v-sum_j d_j*w_j.

For one common value array put
Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2, including the zero optimum jet.
Necessity of Q_ij<=0, with this orientation, follows from the accepted
fact1252b8823bb5a290. Define

 J=sum_(i<q){2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>
       +s_(i+1)||g_(i+1)-g_i||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2}.

The exact identity is

 ||x_0||^2-2S f_q+2sum_i h_i Q_(i,star)
          +2sum_i s_(i+1)Q_(i+1,i)=||x_q||^2+J.             (1)

To check it, the optimum terms sum to
2sum h_i f_i-||x_0||^2+||x_q||^2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2;
summation by parts in the chain terms gives
2S f_q-2sum h_i f_i plus the other two terms in J. This also shows that
all selected Q multipliers are actual nonnegative original constraints.

Define phi_k(z)=product_(r<k)(1-d_r*z),
V_k=v+sum_j phi_k(1/d_j)w_j, U_k=V_k-v, and y_k=d_k U_k.
For k=m put U_m=0. Set z=(S+1)v.
The triangular evaluation matrix has nonzero positive diagonal, so this
change of coordinates is invertible on the residual vectors.

There is a birth array B_ik, initially B_00=1, all other B_0k=0, with

 B_(i+1),k=(1-h_i/d_k)B_ik+(h_i/d_(k-1))B_i,(k-1),          (2)

where the second summand is zero at k=0. It gives g_i=sum_k B_ik V_k.
This follows from
(1-hz)phi_k=(1-h/d_k)phi_k+(h/d_k)phi_(k+1).
On the infinite ruler word, an occupied state k always has d_k>=h_i:
at the first d_j all states below j have already vanished, and the
transition at d_j kills state j. Consequently all occupied transition
probabilities are nonnegative, sum_k B_ik=1, and

 B_ik=0 if k>i or i>=2^k.                                  (3)

The first assertion follows from advancing at most one state per step.
For the second, the first d_k is step2^k-1, with all smaller states already
zero, and its transition moves every remaining mass out of state k.

## 2. A row-finite infinite matrix and the exact constant-column identity

Run (2) formally on the infinite ruler word with the same d_k. In each
individual summand defining J put g_i=sum_k (B_ik/d_k)y_k, with no v.
Define E_kl to be its accumulated symmetric quadratic coefficient.
This is well-defined without summing an infinite numerical series: if a
term contains y_k, (3) forces its step index i<=2^k-1.
Also no state larger than i+1 can occur in that term. Therefore

 E_kl=0 when k<l and l>2^k.                                (4)

Every row has finite support. For 0<=k,l<m, E_kl is EXACTLY the residual
matrix entry for the original queried word, since all steps that can
contribute have already occurred before the last deleted d_0.

Write the actual form as

 J=sum_(k,l<m)E_kl<y_k,y_l>
       +2sum_(k<m)ell_k<y_k,z>+D||z||^2,
 D=S/(S+1).

Then its unnormalized constant load is

 ellhat_k:=(S+1)ell_k=sum_(l>=0)E_kl*d_l.                   (5)

In particular ellhat_k, like each E_kl, is independent of the enclosing
depth once the physical parameters epsilon,c,a have been fixed.

Proof of (5). In the extended birth system set every y_l=d_l*v. The
resulting g_i equals v because sum_l B_il=1. Polarizing against the single
coordinate y_k therefore gives the actual cross coefficient with the
constant gradient. Only finitely many steps and finitely many l can
contribute to that coefficient, by (3)-(4), so this substitution requires
no interchange of infinite sums. After restoring z=(S+1)v the identity
is precisely (5). Transient coefficients B_ik for k<m are unaffected by
the extension, and setting V_l=v at every l>=m recovers the original
absorbing state. Thus no artificial gradient or interpolation condition
has entered the actual data.

As direct checks, put t=d_0,u=d_1. The first two steps give

 E_00=(2-t)/t, E_01=(t-1)/u,
 E_11=[2t+u(2-u)]/u^2,
 E_12=(t+u)(u-1)/(u*d_2),
 ellhat_0=1, ellhat_1=t^2/u+t+1.                            (6)

For example, E_00*d_0+E_01*d_1=(2-t)+(t-1)=1, agreeing
with the first constant load. The second row likewise gives (6).

## 3. The exact effect of a right boundary

All definitions in this section use the infinite coefficient array (4).
Set an entry with a negative index to zero, and define

 A_i=E_(i,i+1), e_i=E_(i,i+2),
 b_i=A_i-2(e_(i-1)+e_i),
 tau_i^- =sum_(0<=l<=i-3)|E_il|,
 tau_i^+ =sum_(l>=i+3)|E_il|,
 t_i=E_ii-A_(i-1)-A_i+e_(i-2)-tau_i^-.                     (7)

The letter t_i in (7) is a diagonal margin, distinct from the isolated
scalar t=d_0 in (6). Both tail sums are finite by (4).

For the finite m-by-m residual matrix, extract second-neighbor triple
squares and adjacent pair squares using

 e_i^[m]=E_(i,i+2) (0<=i<=m-3),
 b_i^[m]=E_(i,i+1)-2(e_(i-1)^[m]+e_i^[m]),
 r_i^[m]=E_ii-E_(i-1,i)-E_(i,i+1)+e_(i-2)^[m]+e_i^[m]
                   -sum_(0<=l<m,|l-i|>=3)|E_il|.            (8)

Out-of-range entries in the FINITE definitions (8) are zero. Substitution
into (7) gives the exact formulas

 b_i^[m]=b_i                         (0<=i<=m-3),
 b_(m-2)^[m]=b_(m-2)+2e_(m-2),

 r_i^[m]=t_i+e_i-sum_(i+3<=l<m)|E_il| (0<=i<=m-3),
 r_(m-2)^[m]=t_(m-2),
 r_(m-1)^[m]=t_(m-1)+A_(m-1).                              (9)

Thus a proof of the infinite bulk remainder alone can miss the entire
e_(m-2) term at the penultimate row. Formula (9) retains that charge.

In particular, the following bounds imply all needed finite residual
square signs and uniform lower bounds for the denominators used below:

 e_i>=0, tau_i^+<=e_i, t_i>=0,
 b_i>=2/15, and A_i>=2/5 for i>=2.                          (10)

Under (10), e_i^[m]>=0, b_i^[m]>=2/15, r_i^[m]>=0, and
r_(m-1)^[m]>=2/5. These are direct consequences of (9).
No claim that (10) has been proved for the actual whole family is made.

## 4. A complete conditional bound on the terminal charge

Here is a sufficient condition using the actual known array, with no
pseudoinverse or eigenvalue hypothesis. In addition to (10), suppose

           2d_i<=ellhat_i<=(11/4)d_i for every i>=2.         (11)

Only the indices needed in (9) and below are necessary; uniform bounds on
the infinite arrays are a convenient stronger formulation.
Then the COMPLETE local-square constant remainder satisfies

                         Delta>1/100                       (12)

for every actual m>=128. This is a proved implication, not a proof of
its hypotheses (10)-(11).

First define alpha_(-1)=0, alpha_i=ell_i-alpha_(i-1), and

 C_charge=sum_(i<m-1)alpha_i^2/b_i^[m]
                    +alpha_(m-1)^2/r_(m-1)^[m],
 Delta=D-C_charge.                                         (13)

The exact full square identity is

 J=sum_(i<m-1)r_i^[m]||y_i||^2
   +r_(m-1)^[m]||y_(m-1)+(alpha_(m-1)/r_(m-1)^[m])z||^2
   +sum_(i<m-1)b_i^[m]||y_i+y_(i+1)+(alpha_i/b_i^[m])z||^2
   +sum_(i<m-2)e_i||y_i+2y_(i+1)+y_(i+2)||^2
   +sum_(k<l<m,l-k>=3)|E_kl| ||y_k+sgn(E_kl)y_l||^2
   +Delta||z||^2.                                         (14)

Indeed the triple squares supply the distance-two entries and twice their
weights to each adjacent entry. The pair squares then supply exactly
b_i^[m], and the long-range squares supply precisely their signed entries.
Their diagonal cost is the one subtracted in (8). Finally
ell_i=alpha_i+alpha_(i-1) proves the constant cross terms, and the completed
squares spend exactly C_charge. Define a zero-weight signed square as zero.

We now bound (13). The actual parameters satisfy

 2<c<39/16, a>2/5, 7/5<d_0<3/2,
 199/100<d_1<2, d_2>3, and d_i>2d_(i-1) for i>=3.          (15)

For c<39/16 use lambda<29/12, whence
c<(29/12)(129/128)=3741/1536<39/16.
For a>2/5 use a>(127/128)/(39/16)=127/312>2/5.
The remaining first-level inequalities follow directly. For doubling,

 c(c-2)=1+2lambda(lambda-1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2
       >1+6epsilon,

so (c-2)a*c^2=(1-epsilon)c(c-2)>1+5epsilon-6epsilon^2>1.
Hence d_3>2d_2, and the inequality persists at every larger index.

Equations (6),(15) give 1=ellhat_0<ellhat_1<4, whereas
ellhat_2>=2d_2>6. For i>=3, (11),(15) give
ellhat_i>=2d_i>4d_(i-1)>(11/4)d_(i-1)>=ellhat_(i-1).
Thus the loads are positive and increasing. Also the upper bound
ellhat_i<=(11/4)d_i holds at i=0,1 by (6),(15).
Induction in alpha_i=ell_i-alpha_(i-1) now proves

                    0<=alpha_i<=ell_i.

Consequently (10) implies

 C_charge <= (121/16)/(S+1)^2
       *[(15/2)sum_(i=0)^(m-2)d_i^2+(5/2)d_(m-1)^2].       (16)

The actual, unreset elapsed clock obeys

 S+1=a*c^m/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))*2^m-d_0
     >a*c^m/(c-2).                                       (17)

For the strict inequality, c(c-2)<2 and
c(c-2)-(1-epsilon)=[2lambda(lambda-1)+1]epsilon
                        +lambda^2 epsilon^2>7epsilon.
Thus 1-a/(c-2)>3epsilon, so its 2^m contribution exceeds d_0.

Expanding d_i^2=(1+a*c^i)^2, summing the geometric terms and using
c-2<1/2,a>2/5,c>2 gives

 sum_(i<m-1)d_i^2/(S+1)^2
    <=(c-2)^2/[c^2(c^2-1)]+2^(1-m),
 d_(m-1)^2/(S+1)^2 <=(c-2)^2/c^2+2^(-m).                 (18)

Here the cross term in the first line is at most
[2(c-2)^2/(a(c-1))]c^(-m-1)<2^(-m), and its constant term is
at most (25/16)m*2^(-2m)<2^(-m). The two lower-order terms in
the second line sum to less than 2^(-m), by the same bounds.

Therefore (16) is bounded above by F(c)+2^(-120), where

 F(c)=(121/16)(c-2)^2/c^2 *[15/(2(c^2-1))+5/2].           (19)

For c>2 this F is increasing: after removing positive factors the
derivative sign is that of
4(c^2+2)(c^2-1)-6c^2(c-2)
=2c^3(2c-3)+16c^2-8>0.
Exact rational arithmetic gives

 F(39/16)=1095787/1119456,
 49/50-F(39/16)=31997/27986400>1/1000.

It follows that C_charge<49/50. Meanwhile (17) gives
D=1-1/(S+1)>1-2^(1-m)>999/1000.
Thus Delta>19/1000>1/100, proving (12).

The additional original-space term in (1) is
||x_0-S*z/(S+1)-sum_j d_j*w_j||^2. Its coordinate change has
unit triangular determinant. Thus (14) together with this square retains
the full original x_0 coordinate and every constant-column range condition.

## 5. An exact inverse formula for attacking the remaining scalar bounds

Set xi_j=d_j*w_j. The triangular birth transformation is y=R*xi with
R_kj=(d_k/d_j)phi_k(1/d_j) for j>=k, and zero otherwise.
For 0<=j<=k its inverse has the closed formula

 (R^(-1))_jk = product_(0<=r<=k,r!=j)(1-d_r/d_j)^(-1).       (20)

One proof uses Newton interpolation at theta_j=1/d_j. The coefficients
c_j=[product_(r!=j)(theta_j-theta_r)]^(-1) annihilate every polynomial
of degree less than k and give its leading coefficient at degree k:
this follows by comparing the degree-k coefficient in the Lagrange
interpolation formula. Apply it to phi_l, 0<=l<=k, whose leading
coefficient at l=k is (-1)^k product_(r<k)d_r. This gives the inverse
of the evaluation matrix, and multiplying row j by d_j and column k
by1/d_k yields (20). The formula is also valid at k=0 by empty products.

For the ACTUAL affine-geometric levels, let q_c=1/c and
P_r=product_(s=1)^r(1-q_c^s), with P_0=1. Then (20) becomes

 (R^(-1))_jk
  =(-1)^(k-j) q_c^((k-j)(k-j+1)/2)
       *(d_j/(d_j-1))^k/(P_j P_(k-j)).                     (21)

This is exact, not a replacement of d_j by a*c^j. To see it, factor
1-d_r/d_j=[(d_j-1)/d_j](1-c^(r-j)) in each of the k factors
in (20), then split r<j and r>j. In particular, the alternating sign
and the fast decay with distance k-j have explicit formulas.

For later error estimates, P_r>1/4 uniformly. Indeed q_c<5/12, and
product_(s=2)^r(1-q_c^s)>=1-sum_(s=2)^r q_c^s
>=1-q_c^2/(1-q_c)>59/84. Multiplying by 1-q_c>7/12 gives
P_r>413/1008>1/4 for r>=1 (P_0=1). Therefore

 |(R^(-1))_jk| <16 q_c^((k-j)(k-j+1)/2)
                     *exp(k/(a*c^j)).                    (22)

The last step uses (1+1/(a*c^j))^k<=exp(k/(a*c^j)). The exponential
factor cannot be dropped uniformly at small j; it is the exact effect
of the additive1 in the physical levels. Formula (22) is a coefficient
estimate, not yet a bound on all entries of the transformed kernel.

## 6. What remains unproved, and failed transfers

For the factor allocation in Section4, the outstanding work is to prove
(10)-(11). In that allocation, the one-sided margin t_i>=0 is required
at the actual penultimate row; proving only the infinite interior remainder
t_i+e_i-tau_i^+>=0 does not establish it. Section7 below gives a different
allocation that removes this particular requirement.

The earlier exact boundary calculation already showed that the first
nontrivial such margin tends to zero with epsilon: t_1=r_1 is asymptotic
to (3sqrt(2)-1)epsilon/2. Thus there can be no positive uniform lower
bound for ALL t_i. This defeats a direct compactness argument based on
the smallest finite-horizon margin. It does not refute t_i>=0.

The new row-sum identity (5) does not imply (11) from positivity or total
nonnegativity alone: it contains the nonconstant weights d_l, including
future levels. Likewise the inverse formula (21) has alternating terms;
positive node factors do not turn its congruence into an entrywise bound.
The actual legal negative-swap example06fc37ec32b366f7 remains a regression
against assigning every local chronological swap a positive sign.

No additional direct kernel case was run in this round. Reading retained
matrix entries and one trivial exact rational evaluation of (19) did not
assemble or evaluate a new kernel. The public ledger remains at8/12 cases
and53.087538152998604/120 measured seconds. No optimization, installation,
fact submission, new launch, or renewed numerical allowance is inferred.

## 7. A better right-boundary allocation: keep the shortened triples

There is no need to force the t_i sign in (10). Instead define the full
interior remainder

 u_i=E_ii-A_(i-1)-A_i+e_(i-2)+e_i-tau_i^--tau_i^+
    =t_i+e_i-tau_i^+.                                     (23)

Assume the following explicit bounds on the same extended actual array:

 e_i>=0, u_i>=0, b_i>=2/15 for all i>=0;
 u_i>=1/16 and e_i>=1/15 for i>=127;
 2d_i<=ellhat_i<=(11/4)d_i for i>=2.                        (24)

These bounds remain UNPROVED. Under (24), however, a different complete
square identity yields a uniform remainder

                         Delta_new>1/200.                 (25)

Here is the identity and its proof. For 0<=i<m set

 R_i=u_i+sum_(l>=m,l-i>=3)|E_il|
               +1_(i=m-1)(b_(m-1)+e_(m-1)).               (26)

Then the finite residual form has the exact decomposition

 sum_(k,l<m)E_kl<y_k,y_l>
  =sum_(i<m)R_i||y_i||^2
   +sum_(i<m-1)b_i||y_i+y_(i+1)||^2
   +sum_(i<m-2)e_i||y_i+2y_(i+1)+y_(i+2)||^2
   +e_(m-2)||y_(m-2)+2y_(m-1)||^2
   +sum_(k<l<m,l-k>=3)|E_kl| ||y_k+sgn(E_kl)y_l||^2.        (27)

To prove it, first expand the infinite pair/triple/far decomposition with
diagonal remainder u_i from (23), and then set y_i=0 for i>=m. Every
row is finite, so only finitely many summands survive. The triple starting
at m-2 becomes the SHORTENED square displayed in (27); it is not removed.
The triple starting at m-1 contributes e_(m-1)||y_(m-1)||^2, and the
pair starting there contributes b_(m-1)||y_(m-1)||^2. Each far edge
crossing the boundary contributes its absolute weight to the lower-index
diagonal. These are precisely (26). The identity can also be checked
entry by entry, without positing any sign of u_i beforehand.

Thus (24) gives R_i>=0 and, since m-1>=127,

 R_(m-1)>=1/16+2/15+1/15=21/80.                            (28)

Keep the same exact alternating load alpha_i=ell_i-alpha_(i-1).
Complete the pair squares and the final diagonal in (27), leaving the
shortened triple unloaded. The resulting FULL J identity is (27) with

 b_i||y_i+y_(i+1)||^2 replaced by
 b_i||y_i+y_(i+1)+(alpha_i/b_i)z||^2,

 R_(m-1)||y_(m-1)||^2 replaced by
 R_(m-1)||y_(m-1)+(alpha_(m-1)/R_(m-1))z||^2,

 and the additional term Delta_new||z||^2, where

 C_new=sum_(i<m-1)alpha_i^2/b_i+alpha_(m-1)^2/R_(m-1),
 Delta_new=D-C_new.                                       (29)

As in (14), ell_i=alpha_i+alpha_(i-1) checks every constant cross term.
The original x_0 square from (1) is retained. This provides the complete
constant-column condition; no range information is suppressed.

A sharper estimate on alpha pays the smaller final diagonal (28).
First, d_j/d_(j-1)>12/5 for every j>=8. In fact
d_7=1+(1-epsilon)c^6>=1+lambda^6>198:
the function (1-epsilon)(1+epsilon)^6 increases on [0,1/128].
Using lambda>241/100 and c-1<23/16 gives

 d_j/d_(j-1)=c-(c-1)/d_(j-1)
       >241/100-23/3168>12/5.

The positivity and monotonicity argument before (16) uses only the load
bounds, so 0<=alpha_i<=ell_i still holds. For i>=9,

 (S+1)alpha_i
  <=ellhat_i-ellhat_(i-1)+ellhat_(i-2)
  <=(11/4)d_i-2d_(i-1)+(11/4)d_(i-2)
  <=(11/4)d_i-(41/48)d_(i-1)
  <[11/4-41/(48c)]d_i
  <(1123/468)d_i<(12/5)d_i.                               (30)

The first inequality uses alpha_(i-2)<=ell_(i-2). The fourth uses
d_i<c*d_(i-1). The last uses c<39/16 and
12/5-1123/468=1/2340>0. This gain explicitly uses the alternating load;
replacing alpha_i by ell_i would fail to obtain the following bound.

Equations (28)-(30) imply

 C_new <=(144/25)/(S+1)^2
       *[(15/2)sum_(i<m-1)d_i^2+(80/21)d_(m-1)^2]
       +2^(26-2m).                                       (31)

The correction covers all i<9: their entire charge is at most
(121/16)(15/2)*9*d_8^2/(S+1)^2<576*2^(16-2m)<2^(26-2m),
because d_8<2a*c^8 and (17) give d_8/(S+1)<2^(8-m).
It is harmless to add their full charge even though the first term in
(31) already gives them some budget.

Apply (18) to (31). The result is at most G(c)+2^(-119), where

 G(c)=(144/25)(c-2)^2/c^2 *[15/(2(c^2-1))+80/21].          (32)

This G increases on 2<c<5/2. For a function of the form
(c-2)^2/c^2 [A/(c^2-1)+B], with A=15/2 and B=80/21,
the derivative sign reduces to
4B(c^2-1)^2+2A[4c^2-c^3-2]>0 on this interval.
The latter bracket equals c^2(4-c)-2>4, proving the sign.
Exact rational values are

 G(39/16)=3170048/3206775,
 99/100-G(39/16)=18637/12827100>1/1000.

Therefore C_new<99/100. Since D>999/1000 by (17),
Delta_new>9/1000>1/200, as claimed.

This alternative is the preferred next interface: establish the bulk
inequalities (24), using the exact inverse and chronology if helpful.
It does NOT require the stronger one-sided penultimate margin, or any
new sign for local swaps. All hypothesized scalar bounds concern known
explicit coefficients, and are still the unsolved part of this route.

## 8. Unconditional uniform tail bounds from the birth paths

The infinitely extended rows in (24) can be reduced to a fixed width with
explicit error, without a numerical kernel evaluation or an extra sign
assumption. The following bounds hold for the ACTUAL parameters above.

For an integer r>=0 define

 p_r=min{1, (7/2)^r/[r! 2^(r(r-1)/2)]}.

For r>=1 put

 H_r=(77/4)p_r+(343/24)2^(-r)[(5/2)p_(r-1)+p_r].           (33)

Then for every k>=2 and r>=1,

 |E_(k,k+r)| <=(d_k/d_(k+r))H_r <=2^(-r)H_r.              (34)

Here is a direct path proof. All steps that contribute to row k occur
by the first physical d_k. Their total elapsed time is
T=S_(H_k)+d_k. The explicit word sum and a/(c-2)<1 give

 S_(H_k)=a*c^k/(c-2)+(1-a/(c-2))*2^k-1<=c^k-1,
 T<(1+1/a)d_k<(7/2)d_k.                                  (35)

Every one of these steps is at most d_k. Once a birth path first reaches
state k, to reach state k+r by this time it must make r further births
on distinct increasing steps. Condition on its first arrival at k.
For any specified r birth steps the probability is at most their step
product divided by d_k*d_(k+1)*...*d_(k+r-1); the intervening stay
probabilities and any continuation probability are at most one.
The sum of these products is at most T^r/r!, because the expansion of
T^r contains every product of r distinct steps r! times and all other
terms are nonnegative. By (15), the denominator is at least
d_k^r*2^(r(r-1)/2). The resulting bound holds for every possible
arrival time, hence also without conditioning. It proves

       B_(i,k+r)<=p_r for every i<=2^k.                    (36)

This estimate applies to both ends of every step contributing to row k.

To bound the actual quadratic coefficient, write v_ij=B_ij/d_j for the
coefficient of y_j in g_i; this symbol v_ij is unrelated to the constant
gradient vector v. Equation (2) implies

 |v_(i+1),k-v_ik| <=h_i(c+1)/d_k^2,
 |v_(i+1),l-v_il| <=h_i(c*p_(r-1)+p_r)/d_l^2,
                    l=k+r,                               (37)

because every adjacent ratio d_j/d_(j-1) is less than c.
Also |v_ik|,|v_(i+1),k|<=1/d_k and
|v_il|,|v_(i+1),l|<=p_r/d_l.

Let M=7/2. The first and third terms of J therefore contribute in
absolute value at most

 [2sum_i s_(i+1)h_i+sum_i h_i^2]p_r/(d_k*d_l)
 <=[T^2+2d_k*T]p_r/(d_k*d_l)
 <=(M^2+2M)(d_k/d_l)p_r.                                 (38)

The middle term of J, using (37), contributes at most

 M^2(c+1)(d_k/d_l^2)[c*p_(r-1)+p_r],                       (39)

since sum_i s_(i+1)h_i^2<=T*sum_i h_i^2<=d_k*T^2.
Now d_l>=2^r*d_k>3*2^r, c+1<7/2, and c<5/2.
Combining (38)-(39) yields (33)-(34). This proof estimates absolute
values, retaining all signed cross terms; it assigns no sign to E itself.

The constants are small after r=6. For r>=7, each summand in H_(r+1)
is at most 1/256 of its corresponding summand in H_r. For the terms
p_r this follows from
p_(r+1)/p_r=(7/2)/[(r+1)2^r]<=7/2048<1/256;
for 2^(-r)p_(r-1) the ratio is (7/2)/(r*2^r)<=1/256.
All p involved here are below one, so the displayed exact ratios apply.
Consequently exact rational arithmetic gives

 sum_(r>=7)H_r <=(256/255)H_7
   =6636933037/197138998886400 <1/25000.                   (40)

Symmetry and (34) now show, for EVERY k>=2,

 sum_(|l-k|>=7)|E_kl| <1/1000000,                         (41)

 sum_(|l-k|>=7)|E_kl|*(d_l/d_k) <1/20000.                 (42)

For a right entry, (34) supplies respectively 2^(-r)H_r and H_r.
For a left entry l=k-r>=2, use (34) with base l; the corresponding
bounds are 2^(-r)H_r and 2^(-2r)H_r. Any distant entry with l=0 or1
is exactly zero by (4). Thus the two tails in (41) total at most
2^(-6)sum_(r>=7)H_r<1/1600000<1/1000000. The tails in (42)
total at most (1+2^(-14))sum_(r>=7)H_r<1/20000.

For a concrete finite-width reformulation, define

 u_i^[6]=E_ii-A_(i-1)-A_i+e_(i-2)+e_i
                         -sum_(3<=|l-i|<=6)|E_il|,
 L_i^[6]=sum_(|l-i|<=6)E_il*d_l/d_i,

where all indices l are nonnegative. Then for i>=2,

 u_i^[6]-1/1000000 < u_i <=u_i^[6],
 |ellhat_i/d_i-L_i^[6]|<1/20000.                           (43)

The first two rows have exact finite support by (4). Thus proving the
remaining sufficient conditions (24) does not require an uncontrolled
infinite tail or any exponentially long trajectory enumeration: a fixed
thirteen-entry band, together with (43), contains the missing estimates.
The row index and epsilon still vary, and uniform estimates for those
band entries are NOT established here. Equations (34),(41)-(43) are actual
unconditional mathematical claims with proofs in this draft; they remain
unverified because this launch has no fact_submit allowance.

## 9. Why the tail theorem does not decide the near-diagonal signs

The older same-multiset foil also satisfies the hypotheses used in the
tail proof. This gives a concrete failure test for promoting locality to
positive semidefiniteness. Keep the actual d_k and exchange only the third
and fourth steps, changing (t,u,t,d_2,...) to (t,u,d_2,t,...).
For k=2 its killing step and elapsed time move earlier; for every k>=3
the prefix through the first d_k has unchanged total time and maximum
step. The k=0,1 killing times are unchanged. Thus extinction still occurs
by2^k and the entire path proof (35)-(42) continues to apply.

Nevertheless choose v=0, V_0=0,V_1=1,V_2=-1, and V_j=0 for j>=3.
The first three transitions in this permuted word are pure births,
so its scalar gradients are exactly (0,1,-1,0,0,...).
This is a genuine rank-one formal Gram in the same invertible birth
coordinates, with y_1=u,y_2=-d_2 and all other y zero. Its quadratic is

 J=t+[4(t+u)-2u(t+u)+u(1-u)]+[t+u+2d_2-d_2^2]
  =6t+6u-3u^2-2tu+2d_2-d_2^2.

Writing tau=sqrt(2), the actual values are
t=tau-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)], u=2-epsilon,
d_2=tau+2-lambda epsilon^2. The coefficient of t is6-2u>0.
Replacing t by its upper bound tau and expanding the remaining expression
therefore gives

 J<=-2+(6+2tau)epsilon-3epsilon^2
                    +2lambda^2 epsilon^2-lambda^2 epsilon^4
   <-2+9epsilon+12epsilon^2<-19/10.

The last inequality uses 9/128+12/128^2<1/10.
This rederives, for the present tail comparison, the formal obstruction
previously recorded as501aba74c8acfcdf. It is NOT a negative direction
of the ORIGINAL chronological kernel. It is also NOT legal interpolation
data: g_0=0 forces x_1=x_0, while g_1=1, so
Q_01+Q_10=||g_0-g_1||^2=1 for every value array.

Hence the new unconditional tail inequalities leave a real mathematical
obligation at the near-diagonal band; they cannot settle its sign merely
by retaining the level multiset, birth support and clock upper bounds.
The original lower-step repeats must enter those remaining estimates.

## 10. Source check and exact finite cancellation

The main-agent pointer research/SOURCE_Q_PRODUCTS_20260927.md and the
primary NIST DLMF17.2 and17.5 pages were read. DLMF17.2.35 gives the finite
q-binomial identity, while17.2.37 is an infinite series with |z|<1.
These are optional algebraic pointers, not premises of any proof above;
no external theorem has been imported as a fact. The targeted Matlas
search for the birth-tail bound returned other Markov-process statements,
not an applicable theorem. Section8 supplies its own finite path proof.

One exact cancellation that already follows from (20), without an external
identity, is sum_(j=0)^k(R^(-1))_jk=1. In fact
(R^(-1))_jk=d_j^k/product_(r<=k,r!=j)(d_j-d_r), and Lagrange
interpolation of X^k shows that these leading coefficients sum to1.
Thus sum_j xi_j=sum_k y_k exactly, so the original final-position square
can also be written ||x_0-S*v-sum_k y_k||^2. This retains the additive1
in every node and all alternating signs of the inverse; an absolute-value
estimate of (21) would lose this cancellation.

No unproved q-series positivity or infinite limit is used in this file.
The outstanding estimate is still uniform control of the band entries,
in both the row index and actual enclosing-horizon parameter.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000691 -->
# Complex polynomial transcripts and interpolation cycles

Status: exploratory derivation, not a verifier-accepted fact. This does not resolve the exact Zhang--Jiang profile comparison. Normalize L=1, x_*=0, f_*=0 and ||x_0||=1.

## 1. Counterexample to local curvature lifting

Take h_0=1 and x_0=(1,0), g_0=(1/2,1/2), x_1=(1/2,-1/2), g_1=(1/2,0), x_*=g_*=0.
The GD equation holds. Every pair satisfies <g_i-g_j,x_i-x_j>=||g_i-g_j||^2.
Also g_0=P_0 x_0 and g_1=P_1 x_1, where P_0 projects onto span((1,1)) and P_1 projects onto the first axis. Both are symmetric PSD contractions.
Nevertheless, smooth-convex interpolation against the optimum forces f_0=1/4 and f_1=1/8. The inequality from point 1 to point 0 requires f_0-f_1>=3/8, a contradiction. The three-cycle * -> 1 -> 0 -> * has positive total lower-increment weight 1/4.

## 2. The schedule-specific algebraic object

Fix n>=1 and any real nonnegative schedule h_0,...,h_{n-1}. Define

    P_0(z)=1, P_t(z)=product_{s=0}^{t-1}(1-h_s z), 1<=t<=n.

For z=a+ib identify C with R^2 and set x_t=P_t(z), g_t=z P_t(z), x_*=g_*=0.
Multiplication by z is the real matrix A_z=[[a,-b],[b,a]]. It need not be a gradient map globally. Only the finite sampled transcript is proposed for interpolation. GD holds exactly, with the original ordered real schedule. Put c=a-a^2-b^2.

For every ordered pair in I={*,0,...,n}, define the edge weight from j to i by

    q_ij = <g_j,x_i-x_j> + (1/2)||g_i-g_j||^2
         = (a/2)(|x_i|^2-|x_j|^2)
           + b Im(conj(x_j) x_i) - (c/2)|x_i-x_j|^2.

For a directed simple cycle C=(i_0,...,i_{r-1},i_r=i_0), put

    A_C = sum_{k=0}^{r-1} Im(conj(x_{i_k}) x_{i_{k+1}}),
    E_C = sum_{k=0}^{r-1} |x_{i_{k+1}}-x_{i_k}|^2.

The sum of q weights on C is b A_C-c E_C/2. Both orientations occur.
The finite data admit smooth-convex interpolating values if and only if

    c E_C >= 2 |b A_C| for every simple cycle C.                     (C)

Write D_h for this set of complex parameters. It is a closed semialgebraic set whose defining polynomials are obtained from all prefix polynomials. The two-cycle between * and 0 forces c>=0, so D_h is in the compact disk (a-1/2)^2+b^2<=1/4. No restriction to rational or algebraic schedules occurs.

Here is the value-selection argument. Give the complete directed graph on I weights q_ij on j->i. Under (C), every cycle has nonpositive weight. Let ell_i be the maximum weight of a path i->*, permitting the empty path at *. Removing repeated nonpositive cycles cannot decrease weight, so the maximum is over finitely many simple paths; ell_*=0. Set F_i=-ell_i. Appending a maximum path i->* to edge j->i, then removing cycles, gives ell_j>=q_ij+ell_i. Hence F_i-F_j>=q_ij.
Conversely, summing feasible differences along each path i->* yields F_i<=-ell_i. Thus these values simultaneously maximize every F_i for the fixed transcript.

A constructive interpolant is f=H^*, where

    H(g)=max_i { <g_i,x_i>-F_i + <x_i,g-g_i>
                 +(1/2)||g-g_i||^2 }.

The pair inequalities imply that the i-th quadratic is active at g_i, that H(g_i)=<g_i,x_i>-F_i, and that x_i belongs to the subdifferential of H at g_i. The finite function H is 1-strongly convex. Its conjugate is finite, convex, differentiable and 1-smooth. Conjugacy gives f(x_i)=F_i and grad f(x_i)=g_i. The sample at * gives f(0)=0 and grad f(0)=0, so 0 is a global minimizer. This is the constructive argument of Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur, arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem 4, Corollary 1 and Remark 2; the exact downloaded statement and proof were read.

Thus each z in D_h yields a legal witness

    B_h(z)=2 F_n=-2 ell_n <= W_n(h).                                   (B)

The optimum-pair inequalities give

    |z|^2 |P_n(z)|^2 <= B_h(z)
                    <= (2a-|z|^2)|P_n(z)|^2.                        (V)

All prefix information enters D_h and ell_n; the terminal polynomial alone cannot determine these cycle constraints.

## 3. A universal legal lens

Put N=n+2, theta=pi/N. The schedule-independent set

    K_n={a+ib : a-a^2-b^2 >= |b| cot(theta)}

lies in D_h for every schedule. For any complex r-gon v_j,

    |sum_j Im(conj(v_j) v_{j+1})|
      <= (1/2) cot(pi/r) sum_j |v_{j+1}-v_j|^2.                     (A)

Proof without simplicity or convexity of the polygon: write
v_j=sum_{k=0}^{r-1} d_k exp(2 pi i k j/r). Orthogonality makes the area expression r sum_k |d_k|^2 sin(2 pi k/r), and the energy 4r sum_k |d_k|^2 sin^2(pi k/r). The zero mode contributes nothing. For each other mode the absolute ratio is (1/2)|cot(pi k/r)|, at most (1/2)cot(pi/r). Since r<=N, (C) follows.

The finite cyclic monotonicity viewpoint is also discussed by Bartz, Bauschke, Borwein, Reich and Wang, Fitzpatrick functions, cyclic monotonicity and Rockafellar's antiderivative (2007), Example 4.6. That exact paper and its Fourier/circulant proof were downloaded and read. Inequality (A) is derived here instead of invoked as a black box.

## 4. Quantitative obstruction: the universal lens is too weak

K_n supplies legal nonquadratic witnesses but cannot prove the silver exponent on its own. An explicit Chebyshev schedule defeats this mechanism.

Let m=2n+1. The polynomial

    p(z)=(-1)^n T_m(sqrt(z))/(m sqrt(z))

has degree n, p(0)=1 and roots

    lambda_k=cos^2((2k-1) pi/(2m)), k=1,...,n.

It equals product_k(1-h_k z) for positive h_k=1/lambda_k, in any order. Here T_m(cos u)=cos(mu). Oddness makes the quotient a polynomial with a well-defined value at zero.

For n>=2 set t=tan(pi/(n+2)) and u=asinh(t/2). For z=a+ib in K_n, 0<=a<=1 and |b|<=t a(1-a). With s=a(1-a),

    (2a-1)^2/cosh^2(u) + (2b)^2/sinh^2(u)
      <= 1-4s+16s^2 <= 1.

Thus K_n is in the filled ellipse with foci 0,1 parametrized by z=(1+cos(w))/2 with |Im w|<=u. A square root is cos(w/2), so

    |z| |p(z)|^2 = |T_m(sqrt(z))|^2/m^2
                 <= cosh^2(m u/2)/m^2.

Since u<=t/2 and tan(theta)<=4 theta/pi on [0,pi/4],
m u/2<=m/(n+2)<2. From (V), every legal interpolating function for every z in K_n has terminal gap

    2 F_n <= 2 cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2.                                  (O)

This is an upper bound only for the restricted witness family. It is not a full-class upper bound on the Chebyshev schedule.

Adding the canonical flat-region Huber witness does not repair this rate loss.
Differentiating the Chebyshev differential equation
(1-x^2)T_m''-x T_m'+m^2 T_m=0 at zero gives
T_m'''(0)=-(m^2-1)T_m'(0), with T_m'(0)=(-1)^n m. Consequently

    sum_k h_k=-p'(0)=(m^2-1)/6=2n(n+1)/3.

For S=sum h, the canonical Huber function is f_delta(x)=x^2/2 if |x|<=delta and delta|x|-delta^2/2 otherwise, with delta=1/(2S+1).
Starting at x_0=1, GD stays in its positive affine-gradient region: all prefixes have x_t>=x_n=(S+1)/(2S+1)>=delta. Its normalized terminal gap is exactly 1/(2S+1), also O(n^-2).

The target benchmark has order n^-rho with rho=log_2(1+sqrt(2))<2.
Thus the guaranteed-lens-plus-canonical-Huber mechanism loses a factor tending to zero relative to the target on these schedules. This kills the universal-lens simplification, not D_h and not the original minimax conjecture.

## 5. Remaining branch decision

The full D_h construction gives a concrete legal candidate mechanism. No uniform estimate of sup_{z in D_h} B_h(z) matching S_bullet(n) is proved. Merely postulating that estimate is a theorem-strength blocked comparison.
A reopening needs a distinct structural estimate on prefix-polynomial point clouds or a stronger moment class; replacing D_h by K_n is quantitatively ruled out by (O).

# Earlier reset weights: a quantitative bound and an exact obstruction to naive propagation

Status: unverified supplement to the full-certificate descent search. All claims below are proved directly; no earlier unverified finding is used as an assumed theorem.

## 1. A reset criterion for any earlier star-outgoing multiplier

Normalize L=1, f_*=0. Consider a full feasible certificate at level q for a length-n schedule h. Let nu_i=lambda_{*,i}>0 for some i<n. Define

    delta_i=f(x_i)-||g_i||^2/2 >=0,
    A_i=prod_{t=i+1}^{n-1} max{1,(h_t-1)^2}.

The full PSD certificate, with the term nu_i Q_{*,i}=-nu_i delta_i isolated, gives for every legal function

    2f(x_n)+nu_i delta_i <= q||x_0-x_*||^2.                 (1)

Replace h_i by one, leaving every other step in place. The changed trajectory agrees with the old one through x_i and reaches y=x_i-g_i at the next iteration. Unit-step descent gives f(y)<=delta_i.

For any nonnegative step a and any current point z, smoothness gives

    f(z-a grad f(z)) <= f(z)+(a^2/2-a)||grad f(z)||^2.

When 0<=a<=2, this is at most f(z). When a>2, use ||grad f(z)||^2<=2f(z) to obtain an upper bound (a-1)^2 f(z). Thus the unchanged suffix multiplies the objective by at most A_i, and

    W_n(h with h_i replaced by 1) <= (2A_i/nu_i) q.          (2)

In particular nu_i>2A_i gives a genuine fixed-length strict improvement. Every tight certificate at a globally minimizing schedule must therefore obey

    nu_i<=2A_i, 0<=i<n.                                    (3)

At i=n-1, direct endpoint convexity gives the much stronger conclusion nu_{n-1}=0 by the explicit last-step descent already recorded separately. Bound (3) is useful for earlier indices but does NOT assert that their multipliers vanish.

The product A_i is a proved worst-case growth bound, not a guess about conditional history. It can be large and does not itself yield the exact Zhang--Jiang profile.

## 2. Exact failure of carrying final-step convexity through even one later unit step

A tempting extension is to move an earlier step toward one and then use convexity to average the two resulting terminal objective values after the unchanged suffix. This fails for a single unchanged unit step.

Define an everywhere finite convex 1-smooth function on the real line by

    f(x)=x^2/2,                       x<=1;
         x-1/2,                       1<=x<=2;
         (x-1)^2/2+1,                 2<=x<=3;
         2x-3,                        x>=3.

Its derivative is, respectively, x, 1, x-1, and 2. It is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. The unique minimum is f(0)=0.

Write T(x)=x-f'(x), the unit GD update, and F(x)=f(T(x)). The following exact values hold:

    F(3/2)=1/8,
    F(5/2)=1/2,
    F(2)=1/2 > (F(3/2)+F(5/2))/2 =5/16.                   (4)

Thus f composed with a unit GD step is not convex, even though f itself is convex and 1-smooth.

The points in (4) arise from exactly the proposed schedule interpolation. Start at x_0=9/2, where g_0=2. The first step b=3/2 gives x_1=3/2. Replacing it by the unit step gives x_1=5/2. Taking their midpoint step b=5/4 gives x_1=2. Follow all three by the same final unit step. Their final objective values are respectively 1/8, 1/2, and 1/2. Hence the middle schedule's value exceeds the convex average of the endpoint schedules by 3/16, with a common function and common initial point.

This refutes the exact propagation step needed to extend the penultimate convex-blend proof unchanged to earlier times. It does not refute a different simultaneous schedule/certificate perturbation, and the example is not claimed to be a matching worst-case stationary tuple.

## 3. Consequence for the active program

The final-step descent uses literal convexity of f along the final update line. Earlier reset changes enter a nonlinear suffix, so that proof cannot simply be repeated backward. The quantitative fallback (2) survives, but requires sufficiently large multiplier weight relative to the proved suffix-growth factor.

Likewise the terminal-pair Schur-range descent controls a perturbation at the final point directly; an earlier gradient relation cannot be treated as if the rest of the GD trajectory were affine. Any propagation mechanism must retain or bound the actual suffix response. This is the concrete obstruction to the attempted backward extension, not a conclusion that all full-certificate approaches fail.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000693 -->
# Quantitative constraints on the full PEP certificate matrix

Status: new algebraic derivation under the updated assignment; not verifier-submitted. The initial selected submission was already used for fact dfde2fa326c908bf.

## 1. The actual certificate, including PSD

Normalize L=1, x_*=0 and f_*=0. Fix n>=1 and h in [0,infinity)^n. Put S=s(h), P_i=sum_{t<i}h_t for 0<=i<=n, P_*=0, and extend h by h_n=0. The P_i are prefix sums, not the polynomial notation Poly_i.

Use the signed interpolation inequalities

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <= 0,

with g_*=x_*=0. Let lambda_ij>=0 for all unequal indices i,j in {*,0,...,n}; put lambda_ii=0. A standard full PEP upper certificate at level q consists of

    out_i-in_i=2*1_{i=n}, 0<=i<=n,                                 (F)
    M_cert(q,lambda,h) >= 0,                                       (PSD)

where out_i=sum_j lambda_ij, in_i=sum_j lambda_ji, and M_cert is the matrix of the quadratic expression

    q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_{i,j}lambda_ij Q_ij

after substitution x_i=x_0-sum_{t<i}h_t g_t. Condition (F) cancels every f_i. The matrix is evaluated on (x_0,g_0,...,g_n). Because every Q_ij<=0, (PSD) proves W_n(h)<=q. This statement is only the sufficient certificate direction; it does not assume strong duality.

Set mu_i=lambda_{i,*}, nu_i=lambda_{*,i}, Nu=sum_i nu_i and U=sum_i mu_i. Summing (F) gives U=2+Nu. The matrix has block form

    M_cert=[[q,-mu^T/2],[-mu/2,A]].                                (M)

Here A is the full gradient block; it is not replaced by its diagonal or row sums. Positive semidefiniteness forces q>0, because q=0 would force mu=0, contradicting U>=2. Equivalently,

    Z_cert=A-mu mu^T/(4q) >=0.                                     (SC)

The equivalence follows by completing the square in the x_0 coordinate.

## 2. Exact chronological entries

For 0<=i<n define Forw_i=sum_{j=i+1}^n lambda_ij, and Forw_n=0. Direct expansion gives

    A_ii=out_i-1_{i=n}-h_i Forw_i.                                (D)

For 0<=i<j<=n,

    2A_ij=-(lambda_ij+lambda_ji)
          -h_j sum_{k=j+1}^n lambda_{ik}
          +h_i(mu_j+sum_{k=0}^i lambda_{jk}).                      (O)

Explanation: the squared-gradient differences supply minus the two directed edge weights off the diagonal, and half of out_i+in_i on the diagonal. In <g_i,x_j-x_i>, the coefficient of ||g_i||^2 is -h_i exactly when j>i. For the cross term <g_i,g_j>, i<j, a term based at i contributes -h_j if its destination is after j, while a term based at j contributes h_i if its destination is at most i or is *. Using (F) simplifies the diagonal.

Thus every feasible certificate satisfies, with z_i=A_ii-mu_i^2/(4q),

    z_i>=0,
    [A_ij-mu_i mu_j/(4q)]^2<=z_i z_j for i<j.                    (P2)

These are necessary consequences of the full Schur-complement PSD condition. They are not asserted to replace higher principal minors.

## 3. A flow-weighted quantitative Huber inequality

Let e denote the all-ones vector in R^{n+1}. Then the exact scalar identity is

    e^T A e=2S+1+sum_i nu_i(P_i+1).                               (E)

One way to check it is to substitute all gradients equal to a scalar t and x_0=1. Formally take f_i=t-(P_i+1/2)t^2. Then every Q_ij with finite i,j and every Q_{i,*} vanishes, while Q_{*,i}=-t+(P_i+1)t^2. The resulting polynomial identity in t is

    (1,t e)^T M_cert (1,t e)
      =q-(2+Nu)t+[2S+1+sum_i nu_i(P_i+1)]t^2.                    (T)

This is an identity in formal data; no assertion of interpolation for every t is needed to evaluate a PSD quadratic form. Its minimum over real t is nonnegative, so

    q >= (2+Nu)^2/[4(2S+1+sum_i nu_i(P_i+1))]
      >= (2+Nu)^2/[4(2S+1+(S+1)Nu)]
      >= 1/(2S+1).                                              (H)

The last inequality follows on multiplying through: with w=2S+1, the numerator difference is
w(2+Nu)^2-4[w+(S+1)Nu]=4S Nu+w Nu^2>=0.
All steps are exact for arbitrary nonnegative real h, including zero entries.

A more local version retains chronological residuals. Define

    drift_i=sum_{j finite or *}lambda_ij(P_i-P_j),
    tailnu_i=sum_{j=i+1}^n nu_j,
    R_i=2h_i+nu_i+drift_i+h_i tailnu_i-2S mu_i.                    (R)

The exact row identity is

    2Ae=2h+mu+nu+drift+h .* tailnu.                               (Rows)

The entrywise product term h .* tailnu is necessary when multipliers leave the optimum. It must not be discarded.
For a derivation, write c_i=-sum_{t<i}h_t e_t and c_*=0. The gradient part of each interpolation term is sym(e_i(c_j-c_i)^T), for finite i; the i=* terms contribute only squared gradients. Its product with e has first part drift/2. The second part is half of
sum_{i finite,j}lambda_ij(c_j-c_i)
 = -2c_n-sum_j nu_j c_j
 = 2h+h .* tailnu,
by the flow equations. The squared-gradient terms supply (mu+nu)/2. This proves (Rows).

Put tau=1/(2S+1), and define the nonnegative PSD evaluation

    eta=(1,tau e)^T M_cert (1,tau e)
       =q-tau-tau sum_i nu_i[1-tau(P_i+1)] >=0.                   (eta)

For S>0, P_i<=S implies

    Nu <= (q-tau)/(tau^2 S).                                     (Leak)

Furthermore, the i-th gradient coordinate of M_cert(1,tau e) is tau R_i/2. Cauchy--Schwarz for a PSD quadratic form, applied to this vector and the i-th coordinate vector, yields

    R_i^2 <= 4 A_ii eta/tau^2.                                   (Residual)

This is a quantitative restriction on the actual multipliers and PSD diagonal, not merely a recurrence for a scalar schedule statistic.

## 4. The exact Huber face

Assume S>0 and q=tau. The positivity of 1-tau(P_i+1)>=tau S implies nu=0 from (eta). The PSD quadratic form then vanishes on (1,tau e), so its entire matrix product with that vector vanishes. Consequently

    sum_i mu_i=2,
    2S mu_i=2h_i+sum_j lambda_ij(P_i-P_j),                        (B)
    Ae=(2S+1)mu/2,
    Z_cert e=0,    Z_cert>=0.                                    (Face)

The last PSD constraint is essential. The balance equations (B) alone are not a valid certificate.

If S=0, all steps vanish and W_n(h)=1, witnessed by f(x)=||x||^2/2. The division by S in (Leak) and the assertion nu=0 are not used in this degenerate case. Equations (F), (M), (D), (O), (E), (T), (Rows) and (eta) remain valid.

## 5. An exact pseudo-certificate test, and a feasible check

Take one step h_0=a>0 and demand q=1/(2a+1). If a full certificate exists, the exact-face formulas force nu=0. Put t=mu_0. Flow and (B) then give

    mu_1=2-t,
    lambda_01=2(1-t),
    lambda_10=2-t,
    0<=t<=1.

The two-dimensional Schur complement has zero row sums. Its first diagonal is

    Z_00=(2-2a)+(2a-1)t-(2a+1)t^2/4.                            (Z1)

For a>=3/2 this is increasing on 0<=t<=1: its derivative is
2a-1-(2a+1)t/2 >= a-3/2. Its maximum is therefore

    Z_00|_{t=1}=(3-2a)/4.

For a>3/2 it is negative, proving that no full PSD certificate at the Huber level exists, despite the nonnegative flow and row equations admitting t=1 for every a.

For 0<a<=3/2, t=1 gives

    mu=(1,1), lambda_10=1, lambda_01=0, nu=0,
    Z_cert=((3-2a)/4)[[1,-1],[-1,1]] >=0.

Thus the full matrix really is feasible and proves W_1(a)<=1/(2a+1); the canonical Huber witness makes this equality. At a=3/2 the Schur complement is exactly zero, reproducing the exact 1/4 objective bound.

For a=2, the same reduced data t=1 pass flow, all nonnegativity conditions and the Huber row equations, yet Z_00=-1/4. They would falsely claim W_1(2)<=1/5 if PSD were discarded; the legal quadratic f(x)=x^2/2 from x_0=1 has normalized terminal error 1. This is an exact hostile test of the scalar-only reduction.

## 6. Scope and next obstruction

The inequalities (H), (Residual) and (P2) are quantitative necessary conditions for every feasible full interpolation certificate. They do not rely on a global minimizer of the stepsize problem, differentiability of W, or stationarity, and therefore do not exclude unbounded or zero steps.

The all-schedule exact-profile problem is still open. Inequality (H) alone is only a refined sum-based Huber test. The mechanism worth pursuing is the interaction of the residual equations with the complete PSD matrix (SC), including the chronological entries (O). No claim is made that its row equations or its 2-by-2 minors suffice. Declaring the existence of an additional separating inequality of target strength would not close the problem.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000694 -->
# A globally minimizing stationary schedule with an unavoidable forward edge

Status: UNVERIFIED exact counterexample package. No additional verifier call
is allocated. The two-step optimum is used here as a fully certified calibration
example for a FACE-LEVEL support claim, not as a solution to the asymptotic target.

## 1. The precise property being refuted

The proposed property is:

    At a globally minimizing positive schedule, one can reselect a tight
    full interpolation certificate with lambda_(i,j)=0 for every
    finite chronological forward pair i<j.

This property is false. The example below is BOTH globally minimizing
among all nonnegative two-step schedules AND a full matching stationary
primal-dual tuple. Its ENTIRE optimal certificate set is a singleton,
and its multiplier lambda_01 is strictly positive.

The forced edge0->1 is ADJACENT. This does NOT refute the narrower
unproved property that only nonadjacent remote forward edges can be removed.

## 2. Exact constants and a complete full certificate

Define

    u=sqrt(2), a=u-1,
    b=(-1+sqrt(9+8u))/4,
    r=a b, q=r^2,
    h=(u,1+b).                                      (1)

The useful exact identities are

    a^2+2a=1,  u=1+a,  1/a=a+2,
    2b^2+b=a+2,
    r(2b+1)=1,
    q=1/[1+2(u+1+b)].                               (2)

For example, r(2b+1)=a(2b^2+b)=a(a+2)=1, and
1+2(u+1+b)=2a+2b+5=(2b+1)^2.
Also 2/5<a<1/2, 3/4<b<1, 0<r<1/2.

Normalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Use the complete inequalities

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.

Set all unlisted multipliers to zero and take

    mu_0=lambda_(0,star)=2u r^2,
    mu_1=lambda_(1,star)=2u b r,
    mu_2=lambda_(2,star)=2r,

    lambda_01=2r^2,
    lambda_10=2r^2/a,
    lambda_21=2(1-r).                               (3)

All are positive. There are no star-outgoing multipliers.
The finite flow equations are

    mu_0+lambda_01=lambda_10,
    mu_1+lambda_10=lambda_01+lambda_21,
    mu_2+lambda_21=2.

They follow from (2); for example 2r^2/a+r=1 and
u r+u b+1=2b+1=1/r.

After x_1=x_0-u g_0 and x_2=x_1-(1+b)g_1, the FULL quadratic identity is

    q||x_0||^2-2f_2+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
         =||r x_0-u r g_0-u b g_1-g_2||^2.          (4)

One way to check all coefficients is to write its gradient block as

    A=(u r,u b,1)^T(u r,u b,1),

whose diagonals are (2r^2,2b^2,1) and off-diagonal entries are
A_01=2rb, A_02=u r, A_12=u b.
Its x_0 cross coefficients are minus the three mu_i from (3).
Thus every multiplier sign, flow, and full PSD entry is explicit.

This proves W_2(h)<=q.

## 3. Three exact matching legal functions

Each scalar function starts at x_0=1 and is minimized at zero.

H: the even Huber function of threshold q, with derivative
max(-q,min(q,x)). Its vector (x_0,g_0,g_1,g_2) is

    v_H=(1,q,q,q),

and its final value is f_2=q/2. At every sampled point its
star-outgoing slack is strictly positive:

    f_i-||g_i||^2/2=q[1-q(P_i+1)]>0,               (5)

where P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, including P_2=u+1+b.
The strict inequality follows from P_i<=P_2 and
q=1/(2P_2+1), with P_2>0.

Q: the quadratic function x^2/2. Its transcript vector is

    v_Q=(1,1,-a,r),

and f_2=r^2/2=q/2.

C: the one-sided capped quadratic

    f_C(x)=x^2/2 for x<=a,
           a x-a^2/2 for x>=a.

Its derivative min(x,a) is continuous, nondecreasing, and 1-Lipschitz.
The trajectory is x_0=1, x_1=a, x_2=-r, with

    v_C=(1,a,a,-r),  f_2=q/2.                       (6)

All are actual globally legal smooth-convex functions. They establish
W_2(h)>=q and hence equality with the full certificate (4).

## 4. Global optimality among ALL two-step schedules

The only verified predecessor used here is fact b98bb807d514e80d:
its exact Huber and both single-cut lower bounds apply to arbitrary
nonnegative schedules. The elementary quadratic witness adds its own
explicit lower bound. Thus for every X,Y>=0,

    W_2(X,Y)>=max{
       1/(1+2X+2Y),
       (X-1)_+^2/(1+2Y),
       (Y-1)_+^2/(1+X)^2,
       (X-1)^2(Y-1)^2
    }.                                             (7)

The last term is the error on x^2/2; the other three are the verified
single-cut/Huber bounds. Suppose W_2(X,Y)<=q=r^2. The Huber term gives

    X+Y>=u+1+b=2+a+b>3.                            (8)

If X<=1, then Y>1 and the third term in (7) gives
Y-1<=r(1+X), whence X+Y<=2+2r<3, contradicting (8).
If Y<=1, then X>1 and the second term gives
X-1<=r sqrt(1+2Y)<=r sqrt(3), whence X+Y<3, again impossible.

We may therefore set U=X-1>0 and V=Y-1>0. Equation (7) implies

    U V<=r,
    V<=r(U+2),
    U^2<=r^2(2V+3).                                (9)

For U<=a, use the second inequality:

    U+V<=U+r(U+2)<=a+r(a+2)=a+b,

with equality only at U=a,V=b.
For U>=a, the first and third inequalities imply

    U^3-3r^2 U-2r^3<=0,
    (U-2r)(U+r)^2<=0,

so a<=U<=2r. Also V<=r/U. The strictly convex function
U+r/U attains its maximum on [a,2r] at an endpoint. Its values are

    a+r/a=a+b,
    2r+1/2.

The first is strictly greater, since

    (a+b)-(2r+1/2)=a^2(b-1/2)>0.

Thus U+V<=a+b, again with equality only at U=a,V=b.
Combining with (8), every schedule with W_2(X,Y)<=q is exactly
(X,Y)=(u,1+b). In particular (1) is the UNIQUE global minimizer, and

    W_2^*=q.                                       (10)

All nonnegative schedules, including zero steps and arbitrary large
steps, were included. This is an exact two-step argument, not an
asymptotic profile claim.

## 5. The ENTIRE optimal certificate set is a singleton

Take ANY full nonnegative-flow PSD certificate at this fixed h,q.
Every actual instance in Section3 attains q. Therefore PSD and the
interpolation signs force the certificate matrix M to annihilate
v_H,v_Q,v_C, and every multiplier to vanish on a strict interpolation
slack of any one of these instances.

The three transcript vectors are independent. Indeed, v_H-v_C has
equal, nonzero g_0 and g_1 coordinates, whereas v_Q-v_C has those
coordinates 1-a and -2a, which are unequal. Both differences have
x_0 coordinate zero, while v_C has x_0 coordinate one. The nonzero
claim uses q<a, which follows from a b^2=(1-r)/2<1/2.

Their common orthogonal complement is generated by

    N=(r,-u r,-u b,-1).

It annihilates v_Q and v_C by (2), and N dot v_H=0 follows from
u r+u b+1=1/r. Since M is PSD and its x_0,x_0 coefficient is q=r^2,

    M=N N^T.                                       (11)

This is the WHOLE matrix for every optimal certificate. No assumption
about a numerical maximal rank or a chosen observed kernel is used;
three explicit legal equality instances force it.

Equation (5) forces every star-outgoing multiplier nu_i to be zero.
The x_0 row of (11) fixes mu uniquely at the values in (3).
The final gradient diagonal satisfies

    A_22=out_2-1=1+in_2

by the full flow equation. In (11), A_22=1, so every incoming
terminal multiplier is zero: lambda_02=lambda_12=nu_2=0.

For function C a direct substitution gives

    Q_20=(a-1)(a+r)<0.

Therefore lambda_20=0 for every optimal certificate as well.
The first gradient diagonal now reads

    A_00=mu_0-(u-1)lambda_01
         =2u r^2-a lambda_01.

In (11) it equals 2r^2, hence

    lambda_01=2r^2=2q>0.                            (12)

Flow then forces lambda_10=2r^2/a and lambda_21=2(1-r), leaving
exactly (3). All directed pairs have now been accounted for.

Thus the optimal certificate set is a singleton, and re-selection,
relative-interior selection, and maximal-rank selection all leave
the positive adjacent forward edge unchanged.

## 6. An ACTUAL matching stationary primal, with no assumed regularity

To check stationarity directly, set

    omega_H=1/(1+a^2 b^3),
    omega_C=omega_H*a^2 b^2*(b-a)/(1+a^2),
    omega_Q=omega_H*a^3 b^2*(1+a b)/(1+a^2).          (13)

They are positive and sum to one:
omega_C+omega_Q=omega_H a^2 b^3.
Construct the actual three-dimensional function

    F(z_H,z_Q,z_C)
       =sum_(J in {H,Q,C}) omega_J f_J(z_J/sqrt(omega_J))

and start at (sqrt(omega_H),sqrt(omega_Q),sqrt(omega_C)).
It is convex and 1-smooth, the initial squared distance is one, and
its full trajectory Gram matrix is
G=sum_J omega_J v_J v_J^T. Its final normalized value is q.

For the certificate (3), the two formal matrix derivatives evaluated
on any scalar/vector realization are

    D_0=2b^2<g_0,g_1>+2r<g_0,g_2>-2r^2||g_0||^2,
    D_1=2<g_1,g_2>.                                (14)

Their values on the three equality instances are

              H              Q                C
    D_0       2r^4           -2a b^2           2a r^2
    D_1       2r^4           -2a r             -2a r.

These values are obtained from (14), not by differentiating a
nonsmooth family of extremal functions. In particular the fixed
capped function C has a gradient kink at x_1=a, so that shortcut
would be unsafe.

The identities

    omega_Q-a^2 omega_C=omega_H a^3 b^2,
    omega_Q+omega_C=omega_H a^2 b^3

make both weighted derivatives zero. Therefore

    <G,partial M/partial h_0>=<G,partial M/partial h_1>=0.

All primal interpolation inequalities, PSD, normalization, full
matrix complementarity, multiplier complementarity, and both
stepsize stationarity equations hold for this actual instance.
Moreover rank(G)=3, rank(M)=1, and the formal matrix size is4.
This example even has strict complementarity; a hidden nonphysical
common kernel is not responsible for the obstruction.

## 7. Consequences and precise limits

This is a complete counterexample to removing ALL forward finite
multipliers by re-selection, even at a positive globally minimizing
schedule with a full stationary matching instance and no extra
common kernel. It closes a possible attempt to turn the triangular
sufficient hypothesis in the earlier suffix-deformation candidate
into a universal property of optimal schedules.

It does not refute removal of ONLY nonadjacent remote forward
edges: lambda_02=0 in the example. It also has q equal to the
Huber value, every nu_i=0, and Z=0; consequently it is not a
counterexample to global Huber tightness. The asymptotic exact-profile
question and the mass-cap bridge remain unresolved.

No literature result beyond the verified predecessor b98bb807d514e80d
was used as a proof brick. The targeted Matlas query returned no
applicable statement. The global two-step calculation is included
solely to make the counterexample's global and stationary status
reviewable without relying on a numerical optimum.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000695 -->
# Compatibility screen: interval matrices and full joint descent

Status: UNVERIFIED algebraic screen requested by master_guidance31b8d52469caa452.
It uses max4's cd0f93f08a50828f as a lead, not as a verified building block.
All identities needed below are derived explicitly. No numerical or verifier
allocation is used.

## 1. Exact dictionary, including the star edges

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Use
Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0.
A full certificate has lambda>=0, finite flow out-in=2 e_n, and
M(q,lambda,h)=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij>=0.

Let C be an (n+1)-square real matrix with C1=0; its off-diagonal
entries may have either sign. Put b=C^T1. Define T(h) by T_ii=1,
T_ij=h_j when j<i, and T_ij=0 when j>i, and set
H(C,h)=CT(h)+T(h)^T C^T.

The exact sign dictionary is

2 sum_(i!=j) C_ij Q_ij
  =-2 sum_i b_i f_i - sum_(i,j)H_ij<g_i,g_j>
    +sum_i b_i||g_i||^2.                             (1)

For verification, the function terms are -2b^Tf because row sums
vanish. The position term is 2sum C_ij<g_i,x_j-x_i>
=2sum C_ij<g_i,x_j>, since C1=0. Substituting
x_j=x_0+g_j-(Tg)_j and again using C1=0 gives
2sum C_ij<g_i,g_j>-2sum(CT)_ij<g_i,g_j>.
The squared-gradient difference contributes
b^T(||g_i||^2)-2sum C_ij<g_i,g_j>. Combining proves (1).

Thus the following simultaneous finite/star multiplier change preserves
all flow equations and keeps mu_i=lambda_(i,star) fixed:

lambda'_ij=lambda_ij+2C_ij   (finite distinct i,j),
nu'_i=nu_i-2b_i,             nu_i=lambda_(star,i).     (2)

Indeed finite out-in changes by -2b_i, which is canceled by the
change -2b_i in incoming star flow. Also sum b_i=0.

At a FIXED schedule, the exact complete matrix change is simply

M(q,lambda',h)-M(q,lambda,h)=-H(C,h),                  (3)

embedded in the gradient block, with the x_0 row unchanged. The
function terms and b_i||g_i||^2 terms in (1) cancel against
sum(-2b_i)Q_(star,i). This is why the star adjustment must not be
omitted.

The ACTUAL sign constraints are

lambda_ij+2C_ij>=0  (i!=j),   nu_i-2b_i>=0.           (4)

They are neither positivity of interval coordinates nor positivity of
every C entry. Negative C entries are allowed exactly to the extent
that the old finite multipliers supply them.

## 2. Simultaneously changing the schedule

Let h'=h+a>=0. Define the full cut vector

z_t(lambda)=sum_(i,j)lambda_ij
 (1_(t<i)-1_(t<j))g_i,

where either indicator is zero at the star. Define the quadratic form

K(a;lambda)=sum_(t<n)a_t<g_t,z_t(lambda)>.

Then (1)--(3), now applied at h', give the exact identity

M(q',lambda',h') =
M(q,lambda,h)+(q'-q)||x_0||^2+K(a;lambda)-H(C,h').    (5)

This includes all remote forward-cut terms. For i<j, the matrix entries
of K in the gradient block are

2K_ij=a_i[mu_j+sum_(l<=i)lambda_(j,l)]
       -a_j sum_(l>j)lambda_(i,l),
K_ii=-a_i sum_(j>i)lambda_(i,j).                     (6)

The expression in (6) follows directly by collecting the two cut
contributions to <g_i,g_j>.

Consequently a diagonal H(C,h') does NOT diagonalize the full
joint perturbation: K still has the mixed entries (6). The exact
zero-mixed-entry target is H_ij=K_ij for i!=j, not H_ij=0.

For example, if every finite forward edge is zero, (6) becomes
2K_ij=a_i c_(i,j), where
c_(i,j)=mu_j+sum_(l<=i)lambda_(j,l).
Summing flow over the suffix shows sum_(j>i)c_(i,j)
=2+sum_(j>i)nu_j>0. Thus if H is diagonal AND K-H is
required to have no mixed entries, each a_i must be zero.
This is a precise incompatibility of those two simultaneous
requirements. It does not rule out allowing a non-diagonal PSD
remainder in (5).

## 3. Inactive-constraint costs survive this coordinate change

Suppose the old full certificate matches one legal stationary primal:
||x_0||=1, 2f_n=q, M vanishes on its data, lambda_ij Q_ij=0, and
<g_t,z_t(lambda)>=0. Put sigma_ij=-Q_ij>=0 on these SAME data.

If (4) holds, evaluating the fixed-h change (3) on the old data gives

sum_(i,j)H(C,h)_ij<g_i,g_j>
 =sum_(i,j)lambda'_ij sigma_ij>=0.                  (7)

The sum on the right includes the star edges. At the new schedule,
PSD of (5) necessarily gives

q'-q >= sum_(i,j)H(C,h')_ij<g_i,g_j>.                (8)

Writing DeltaT=T(h')-T(h), the right side is exactly the nonnegative
cost in (7) plus

2 sum_(i,j)(C DeltaT)_ij<g_i,g_j>.

Thus a descent q'<q must pay every inactive-interpolation cost through
this bilinear term. Diagonalization does not erase the earlier
joint-descent obstruction.

## 4. The triangular elimination can be made inhomogeneous

Assume h'_i>1 for i<n. Specify the strict upper entries U_ij=C_ij.
For j>i define L_(j,i)=sum_(l<=i)C_(j,l), L_(j,-1)=0.
The desired off-diagonal matrix H has the exact equation

(h'_i-1)L_(j,i)+L_(j,i-1)
 =U_ij+h'_j sum_(l>j)U_(i,l)-H_ij.                 (9)

At j=n the displayed sum is empty and the auxiliary h'_n is irrelevant.
Indeed (CT)_ij=U_ij+h'_j sum_(l>j)U_(i,l), while
(CT)_ji=-(h'_i-1)L_(j,i)-L_(j,i-1), proving (9).

Taking H_ij=K_ij makes (9) a scalar elimination for the mixed
cancellation in (5). After it, one STILL has to check every sign in
(4), all diagonal entries or the full remaining PSD matrix, and
strict output improvement. Nothing in the elimination proves those
conditions automatically.

## 5. Compatibility with the actual new deformation

The independently derived candidate8c998d29efd3da99 uses
C_(j,k)=eta p_j/2 for j>k and C_(j,j)=-eta p_j/2, with all other
entries zero. It has b_k=eta/2 and b_j=-eta p_j/2.
These are exactly the star changes in that candidate.

Its H(C,h') is generally NOT diagonal. The specific suffix step
rescalings make its mixed entries equal to K_ij, and its remaining
diagonal difference is eta diag(p). This is a concrete solution of
(5), whereas max4's homogeneous H_ij=0 modes do not by themselves
supply that cancellation.

Conclusion of the bounded screen: the interval coordinates give a
useful exact dictionary and an inhomogeneous elimination, but the
published zero-remainder interval subclass is not a ready-made repair
for the new deformation's cut-profile mismatch. It retains the same
aggregate-sign and inactive-cost obligations. No all-schedule descent,
Huber saturation, or exact-profile lower bound follows.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000696 -->
# Exact cost of a joint certificate perturbation above the Huber level

Status: unverified quantitative derivation, following the updated q>tau assignment. The historical GS interface is not re-proved. The initial verification allowance is already used.

## 1. Definitions and the exact finite-perturbation inequality

Normalize L=1, x_*=g_*=f_*=0. For a fixed positive length-n schedule h, let (q,lambda,G,f) be a full stationary primal-dual tuple. Explicitly:

* lambda_ij>=0 for distinct i,j in I={*,0,...,n}, with out_i-in_i=2*1_{i=n};
* M(q,lambda,h)>=0 is the matrix of q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij after GD substitution;
* G>=0 is the Gram matrix of (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), G_00=1, and Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0;
* M G=0 and lambda_ij Q_ij=0;
* <g_t,z_t(lambda)>=0 for t<n, where
  z_t(b)=sum_{i,j} b_ij(1_{t<i}-1_{t<j})g_i.
  The indicator is zero for the star, and g_*=0.

All objects in these conditions refer to the SAME full legal transcript. No subset of these conditions is used as a replacement for the full system.

Set sigma_ij=-Q_ij>=0. Consider ANY second feasible upper certificate (q',lambda',h'), where h'>=0 and lambda' has the same flow. The new certificate need not be optimal. Put a=h'-h and b=lambda'-lambda. Define the explicit bilinear form

    B_G(a,b)=sum_{t<n} a_t <g_t,z_t(b)>.

Because M is affine separately in h and lambda, its exact finite expansion, paired with the original G, is

    <M(q',lambda',h'),G>
      = (q'-q)-sum_{i,j}lambda'_ij sigma_ij+B_G(a,b).           (1)

To verify the formula, the first-order h term vanishes by stationarity. The lambda term is sum b_ij T_ij(G), with T_ij=Q_ij-(f_i-f_j). Flow equality cancels the f differences, while sum lambda_ij Q_ij=0 changes sum b_ij Q_ij to -sum lambda'_ij sigma_ij. The only remaining mixed term is B_G(a,b). The q coefficient is G_00=1. There are no higher-order terms.

Both matrices in the left side are PSD. Therefore EVERY new feasible certificate satisfies

    q'-q >= sum lambda'_ij sigma_ij - B_G(h'-h,lambda'-lambda). (2)

This is a concrete quantitative obstruction to a proposed joint descent. A descent by Delta=q-q'>0 must obey

    B_G(h'-h,lambda'-lambda)
       >= Delta+sum lambda'_ij sigma_ij.                       (3)

In particular:

* Holding h fixed cannot lower q, as expected from matching primal feasibility.
* Holding lambda fixed cannot lower q for ANY new h, not just for a small change: then both terms on the right of (2) vanish.
* The gain from simultaneous motion has to overcome the exact cost of placing multiplier mass on interpolation inequalities that are strict for the old stationary primal.

Let R_g=max_i ||g_i||. Direct Cauchy--Schwarz bounds give

    |B_G(a,b)|<=R_g^2 ||a||_1 ||b||_1,
    Delta+sum lambda'_ij sigma_ij
       <=R_g^2 ||h'-h||_1 ||lambda'-lambda||_1                 (4)

for every feasible descent. Thus joint movement, not a single-block update, is essential. This is a necessary inequality, not a proof that a descent exists when q exceeds tau.

For a differentiable path through a stationary tuple, any first-order newly positive multiplier on an edge with sigma_ij>0 costs first order, while the useful bilinear term starts at second order. Hence a path with q'(0)=0 must have zero first derivative on every such inactive edge. This conclusion also follows directly from (2) by dividing by the path parameter.

## 2. One-step q>tau certificates with unavoidable star-outgoing flow

The full one-step system provides a useful hostile test of attempts to remove lambda_{*,i} without moving the schedule.

Take h_0=1+a with a>=1. The true worst-case value is q=a^2, certified by

    lambda_{0,*}=2a^2,
    lambda_{*,0}=2a^2-2,
    lambda_{1,0}=2,

and all other multipliers zero. The flow equations hold. Its entire PSD identity is

    q||x_0||^2-2f_1+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
      =a^2||x_0-g_0||^2+||a g_0+g_1||^2.                    (5)

The quadratic f(x)=x^2/2 from x_0=1 gives g_0=1, g_1=-a, f_1=a^2/2 and makes the right side zero. Thus primal and dual match and W_1(1+a)=a^2. This value is strictly above tau=1/(2a+3) for a>=1.

For a>1, every tight certificate at this very same schedule needs nonzero star-outgoing flow. To prove this, suppose nu_i=lambda_{*,i}=0. Then mu_0+mu_1=2, where mu_i=lambda_{i,*}. Pairing the optimal PSD slack with the matching quadratic vector (1,1,-a) gives M(1,1,-a)^T=0. Its x_0 row yields

    mu_0-a mu_1=2a^2.

Combining with mu_0+mu_1=2 gives

    mu_1=2(1-a)<0,

a contradiction. Thus a general-q proof cannot simply remove all star-outgoing multipliers while holding h and q fixed.

There IS an actual full feasible descent in this example. For any 0<epsilon<a-1, replace a by a-epsilon in all displayed variables. The nonnegative multipliers, both squares in (5), the chronological GD equation and the matching quadratic primal all remain valid. The objective drops by

    a^2-(a-epsilon)^2=2a epsilon-epsilon^2>0.

This verifies a schedule-and-flow motion with the full cone and the same legal function, rather than just a formal linearized descent. These tuples are not stationary, so the example does not decide the hypothetical global-minimizer question.

For completeness, the path continues below a=1. For 1/2<=a<=1 take

    lambda_{0,*}=2a, lambda_{1,*}=2(1-a), lambda_{1,0}=2a,

all other multipliers zero, and q=a^2. The gradient block is [[2a,1],[1,1]], while mu=(2a,2(1-a)). Its Schur complement is

    Z=(2a-1) [[1,1/a],[1/a,1/a^2]] >=0.

The quadratic again annihilates the full slack and attains q. Decreasing a keeps all constraints feasible until a=1/2, at which point q=tau=1/4. Thus the full one-step non-Huber region has an explicit piecewise-algebraic descent to the Huber-tight optimum. The change of formula at a=1 is necessary to preserve nonnegative multipliers.

## 3. Why the general-q residual alone has not supplied the descent

For an arbitrary full feasible certificate, the earlier exact Huber evaluation is

    eta=(1,tau e)^T M(1,tau e)
       =q-tau-tau sum_i nu_i[1-tau(P_i+1)]>=0,
    tau=1/(2S+1), S=sum h_t, P_i=sum_{t<i}h_t.

Its residual estimates control M applied to this one explicit Huber vector. A feasible descent from a stationary certificate also has to control the ENTIRE nullspace of M and the cost of activating strict primal interpolation inequalities. Formula (2) makes the missing interaction concrete: a positive value of q-tau by itself neither gives an allowed first-order multiplier support nor a positive value of B_G large enough to pay the required costs.

Two attempted shortcuts therefore fail for explicit reasons:

1. Freeze the optimal multipliers and use the Huber slack to move only h: equation (2) proves that q cannot decrease for any h'.
2. Remove star-outgoing flow at fixed h and q: the exact a>1 example proves such a tight certificate need not exist.

These are restrictions on the indicated operations. They do not refute a general simultaneous feasible descent theorem at global minimizers. The old full stationary tuple must remain attached to (2); using source/cut/energy identities with a nonlegal Gram would invalidate the inequality.

The current open task is to build a simultaneous variation with a favorable explicit B_G and PSD feasibility from the specific GD structure, or obtain a different quantitative consequence from the complete matrix. No Huber-tightness or asymptotic profile comparison has been proved here.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000697 -->
# Joint suffix deformation with exact PSD preservation

Status: complete conditional derivation, UNVERIFIED. It is a new J1 candidate,
not a claim that all global minimizers have the hypotheses. No further
verification allocation has been made.

## Full certificate and the additional triangular hypothesis

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be
nonnegative and finite. For distinct indices in I={star,0,...,n}, set

Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Assume a complete upper certificate (q,lambda,h): q>0, lambda_ij>=0,
out_i-in_i=2 1_(i=n) at finite indices, and the quadratic form

M=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij

after x_j=x_0-sum_(t<j)h_t g_t is positive semidefinite. It is a
quadratic form in the independent formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n);
flow cancels every function-value coefficient. Thus 2f_n<=q||x_0||^2
on every legal trajectory.

For this candidate ONLY, additionally assume

lambda_ij=0 whenever 0<=i<j<=n.                         (T)

All finite-index multiplier flow goes backward. Star edges may go either
way. Write mu_j=lambda_(j,star), nu_j=lambda_(star,j), and, for t<j,

c_(t,j)=mu_j+sum_(l=0)^t lambda_(j,l).

Every c is nonnegative. The complete matrix entries are

M_(x_0,x_0)=q,   M_(x_0,g_j)=-mu_j/2,
M_(g_j,g_j)=out_j-1_(j=n),
2 M_(g_t,g_j)=h_t c_(t,j)-lambda_(j,t),   t<j.          (1)

To check (1), the squared-gradient terms contribute
(out_j+in_j)/2=out_j-1_(j=n) on the diagonal. A backward
edge j->t contributes -lambda_(j,t)<g_j,g_t> from the squared
difference, and h_t times the total flow from j to star or an index at
most t contributes h_t c_(t,j)<g_j,g_t>. The star pair contributes
-mu_j<x_0,g_j>. This accounts for every term; no principal submatrix
replaces M.

## Deformation

Fix k<n with nu_k>0. Define C=sum_(j>k)c_(k,j). By summing flow over
{k+1,...,n}, hypothesis (T) gives C=2+sum_(j>k)nu_j>0.
Put p_j=c_(k,j)/C for j>k, so p_j>=0 and sum p_j=1.

Assume the following explicit proportionality condition on later cuts:

for every k<t<n there is r_t>=0 such that
p_j=r_t c_(t,j) for all j>t.                           (P)

Zero p entries are allowed. If all p_j vanish beyond t, one may take
r_t=0. Set r_k=1/C.

Choose any 0<eta<=nu_k. Change only these multipliers:

nu'_k=nu_k-eta,
nu'_j=nu_j+eta p_j             (j>k),
lambda'_(j,k)=lambda_(j,k)+eta p_j   (j>k).             (2)

Keep every mu_j and every other finite edge unchanged. Change the schedule by

h'_t=h_t                                      (t<k),
h'_k=(h_k+eta r_k)/(1+eta r_k),
h'_t=h_t/(1+eta r_t)                           (k<t<n). (3)

All new steps and multipliers are nonnegative. At k the increased
incoming backward flow eta cancels the removed incoming star flow eta;
at j>k the new outgoing edge eta p_j cancels the new incoming star
edge eta p_j. Thus every flow equation stays exact.

The NEW full matrix, on the same formal vector coordinates, satisfies

M(q,lambda',h') =
M(q,lambda,h)+eta sum_(j>k)p_j ||g_j||^2.               (4)

Proof: diagonal j>k increases by eta p_j, while all x_0 entries and
all other diagonals stay fixed. For t<k, c_(t,j), h_t and lambda_(j,t)
are unchanged. At t=k,

h'_k(c_(k,j)+eta p_j)-(lambda_(j,k)+eta p_j)
=h_k c_(k,j)-lambda_(j,k),

using p_j=r_k c_(k,j). At t>k the edge lambda_(j,t) is unchanged and

h'_t(c_(t,j)+eta p_j)=h_t c_(t,j),

using (P). Therefore EVERY mixed entry is unchanged, proving (4).
In particular the whole new matrix remains PSD, including every old
kernel direction. No forward edge is created and no preexisting edge
is decreased except the explicitly available star multiplier nu_k.

## Strict worst-case improvement

Identity (4) gives, on every trajectory of the NEW schedule,

q R^2-2f_n >= eta sum_(j>k)p_j ||g_j||^2,               (5)

where R=||x_0||>0. Here the old M on the right of (4) is PSD on arbitrary
formal vectors; the new transcript need not be a trajectory of the old
schedule.

Set B=product_(t=0)^(n-1)(1+h'_t) and
D_j=product_(t=j)^(n-1)(1+h'_t), with D_n=1.
These finite positive quantities are deliberately crude.

Since g_star=0 and the gradient is 1-Lipschitz, ||g_t||<=||x_t||, hence
||x_n||<=B R. Convexity gives f_n<=<g_n,x_n><=B R||g_n||.
The same Lipschitz property gives
||g_(t+1)||<=||g_t||+||x_(t+1)-x_t||=(1+h'_t)||g_t||,
so ||g_n||<=D_j||g_j||.

Writing E=2f_n/R^2, these inequalities and (5) yield

q-E >= gamma E^2,
gamma=eta/(4B^2) sum_(j>k) p_j/D_j^2 >0.              (6)

Consequently the actual fixed-length new schedule obeys

W_n(h') <= 2q/[1+sqrt(1+4 gamma q)] < q.               (7)

This is a uniform bound over every dimension and every legal function,
not a calculation on the old active instance. Neither stationarity nor
q=1/(2sum h+1) was used. In particular, if the old certificate is tight
at a globally minimizing schedule, hypotheses (T),(P),nu_k>0 cannot
hold simultaneously.

## Two remaining steps: condition (P) is automatic

Take k=n-2. Define

c_1=mu_(n-1)+sum_(l<=n-2)lambda_(n-1,l),
c_2=mu_n+sum_(l<=n-2)lambda_(n,l),
c_3=mu_n+sum_(l<=n-1)lambda_(n,l)=2+nu_n>0.

Then C=c_1+c_2, p_(n-1)=c_1/C, p_n=c_2/C, and
r_(n-1)=p_n/c_3 satisfies (P), including c_2=0. Formula (3) changes
BOTH of the final two steps:

h'_(n-2)=(C h_(n-2)+eta)/(C+eta),
h'_(n-1)=h_(n-1)c_3/(c_3+eta p_n).

The complete slack gains exactly
eta p_(n-1)||g_(n-1)||^2+eta p_n||g_n||^2.
Thus for a triangular tight certificate at a global minimizer,
nu_(n-2) must vanish. At horizon n=2 this excludes nu_0>0 under (T).
This is a genuine earlier-step joint deformation, not the already
recorded final-step convex blend.

## Precise limitation

For k<t<n, if positive ratios p_j/c_(t,j) differ as j varies, no single
h'_t can satisfy h'_t(c_(t,j)+eta p_j)=h_t c_(t,j) for all j>t when
h_t>0. Thus this one-column transport cannot simply be declared to
work on arbitrary longer suffixes. Additional multiplier changes,
a non-diagonal PSD residual, or a different mechanism would be needed.

Nor is (T) established for arbitrary optimal certificates. Forward
edges produce additional mixed terms when suffix steps change.
The candidate does NOT establish global Huber tightness or the
Zhang--Jiang exact-profile lower bound.

## Hostile checks within the derivation

- No division by h_t, h_t-1, p_j, or nu_j for j>k is used.
  Zero steps, zero weights, and steps on either side of1 are covered.
- If h_k=1, that coordinate stays fixed; positive later r_t can still
  change the suffix. If every affected coordinate were unchanged,
  (5)--(7) would show that q was not a tight certificate.
- Even p_n=0 leaves gamma>0, since some earlier p_j is positive and
  D_j is finite.
- The old matching primal is never reused as if it satisfied new GD
  recursions. Uniform legality enters only in (5)--(7).

Retrieval: fact_search found no matching result. Targeted Matlas search
returned unrelated statements; no external theorem is used in this proof.
