# Selected complex-polynomial bridge and lens obstruction

Status: verifier accepted as fact dfde2fa326c908bf. The fact graph is authoritative; this is the submitted proof artifact.

## Statement

Complex-polynomial transcripts, an explicit legal spectral lens, and a quantitative limit of that lens.

For each integer n>=1 and each real nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_{n-1}), define Poly_0(z)=1 and Poly_i(z)=product_{t=0}^{i-1}(1-h_t z) for 1<=i<=n. Identify the complex plane with the real Euclidean plane, with inner product <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). For z=alpha+i beta set xi_i=Poly_i(z), gamma_i=z Poly_i(z) for 0<=i<=n, and xi_*=gamma_*=0. Put kappa=alpha-|z|^2 and I_n={*,0,...,n}. For each ordered pair define
q_ij=<gamma_j,xi_i-xi_j>+||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2/2,
regarded as the weight on the directed edge j->i. For each directed simple cycle C in I_n define
Area_C=sum_{j->i in C} Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i),
Energy_C=sum_{j->i in C}|xi_i-xi_j|^2.
Define D_h to be the set of z satisfying kappa Energy_C>=2|beta Area_C| for every such cycle.

(a) The data (xi_i,gamma_i), including the optimum sample *, are interpolable by a finite everywhere-defined convex differentiable function f:R^2->R with 1-Lipschitz gradient and grad f(0)=0 if and only if z belongs to D_h. For z in D_h let ell_i be the largest q-weight of a directed path i->*, allowing the empty path at *, and put v_i=-ell_i. The maxima exist over simple paths and ell_*=0. There is such an f with f(xi_i)=v_i for every i. These values are simultaneously maximal among all interpolating f satisfying f(0)=0. Its exact GD recursion for h starts at xi_0=1, and its normalized terminal error is
Gap_h(z):=-2ell_n.
In particular
|z|^2|Poly_n(z)|^2 <= Gap_h(z) <= (2alpha-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2.

(b) The parameter lens
Lens_n={alpha+i beta: alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2))}
is contained in D_h for every h.

(c) Let Cheb_j be the polynomials defined by Cheb_0(x)=1, Cheb_1(x)=x and Cheb_{j+1}(x)=2xCheb_j(x)-Cheb_{j-1}(x). For every n>=2 define the positive schedule Chi_n, in any permutation, by the n entries
sec^2((2k-1)pi/(2(2n+1))), k=1,...,n.
Its terminal polynomial is (-1)^n Cheb_{2n+1}(sqrt(z))/((2n+1)sqrt(z)), interpreted as a polynomial including z=0, and its sum is s(Chi_n)=2n(n+1)/3. For all z in Lens_n,
Gap_{Chi_n}(z) <= 2cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2.

For an arbitrary schedule of length n>=1 define
V_lens(h)=max{sup_{z in Lens_n} Gap_h(z), 1/(2s(h)+1)}.
Let W_n(h) be the project worst-case normalized objective error, namely the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite dimension, convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, using x_{t+1}=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t). Here L>0 is fixed and s(h)=sum h_t. Then V_lens(h)<=W_n(h). For the exact benchmark U_n^ZJ=1/(2S_bullet(n)+1) defined by the recursions in predecessor b98bb807d514e80d, and p=log_2 3 from that predecessor, the explicit schedules satisfy
0<=V_lens(Chi_n)/U_n^ZJ
 <= 2cosh^2(2)(1+4n^p)/n^2 ->0.
Thus the universal-lens witnesses, even augmented by the canonical sum-based Huber witness, cannot prove the required uniform matching lower bound. This is a limitation of that witness family only, not an upper bound on W_n(Chi_n) and not a decision of the unrestricted minimax target.

## Proof

1. Necessary smooth-convex inequality.
For any convex differentiable 1-smooth f and any x,y, put k(u)=f(u)-<grad f(y),u>. Convexity and grad k(y)=0 imply k(y)<=k(u). Integrating the Lipschitz gradient along a segment gives k(u+d)<=k(u)+<grad k(u),d>+||d||^2/2. Take d=-grad k(u) and combine these inequalities to obtain
f(x)>=f(y)+<grad f(y),x-y>+||grad f(x)-grad f(y)||^2/2. (1)
Applied to any proposed interpolant, (1) says its values obey f_i-f_j>=q_ij.

2. Cycle identity and necessity.
For complex u,v, <u,v>=Re(conj(u)v). Expand
<z xi_j,xi_i-xi_j>
=alpha Re(conj(xi_j)(xi_i-xi_j))+beta Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i).
The real part is (|xi_i|^2-|xi_j|^2-|xi_i-xi_j|^2)/2. Also
||gamma_i-gamma_j||^2=|z|^2|xi_i-xi_j|^2.
Therefore
q_ij=(alpha/2)(|xi_i|^2-|xi_j|^2)+beta Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i)
      -(kappa/2)|xi_i-xi_j|^2. (2)
The norm-difference terms telescope on a cycle, whose weight is beta Area_C-kappa Energy_C/2. Both orientations are available, and their areas have opposite signs but equal energies. Consequently nonpositivity of every cycle is equivalent to the definition of D_h. Summing (1) on cycles proves the necessary implication in (a). The cycle *->0->* also forces kappa>=0.

3. Values from paths.
Assume z is in D_h. Every closed walk is a sum of simple directed cycles, after splitting at repeated vertices; thus every closed walk has nonpositive weight. Any path to * can have repeated vertices deleted, and deleting the intervening nonpositive cycle cannot reduce the weight. There are finitely many simple paths on I_n, so ell_i is finite and is a maximum over them. At * the empty path has weight zero and every closed walk has weight at most zero, so ell_*=0.
Concatenate edge j->i with a maximum path i->*, then delete cycles if needed. It follows that ell_j>=q_ij+ell_i. Hence v_i=-ell_i satisfies v_i-v_j>=q_ij. For any other feasible values F_i with F_*=0, summing the inequalities along a path i->* yields -F_i at least the weight of that path. Thus F_i<=-ell_i=v_i for every i.

4. A finite-dimensional global interpolant.
Define on the real plane
H(g)=max_{i in I_n}{<gamma_i,xi_i>-v_i+<xi_i,g-gamma_i>+||g-gamma_i||^2/2}.
This is the maximum of finitely many quadratics with Hessian equal to the identity. It is finite, continuous and 1-strongly convex. The inequality v_j-v_i>=<gamma_i,xi_j-xi_i>+||gamma_j-gamma_i||^2/2 is exactly the assertion that at g=gamma_i the j-th quadratic is at most the i-th quadratic. Thus
H(gamma_i)=<gamma_i,xi_i>-v_i.
The i-th quadratic is active and has derivative xi_i at gamma_i. Its supporting affine function is below H, so xi_i belongs to the subdifferential of H at gamma_i.

Define f(x)=sup_g{<x,g>-H(g)}. For each fixed x, the maximizer g(x) exists and is unique: H(g)-<x,g> is continuous, strongly convex, and coercive because even any one term of the defining maximum has leading term ||g||^2/2. Optimality says x belongs to the subdifferential of H at g(x). Strong convexity implies that for all x,y,
<x-y,g(x)-g(y)>>=||g(x)-g(y)||^2.
To justify this implication, strong convexity and x in partial H(u) imply H(v)>=H(u)+<x,v-u>+||v-u||^2/2: combine H((1-t)u+tv)>=H(u)+t<x,v-u> with the strong-convexity upper bound along the segment, divide by t>0, and let t decrease to zero. Write this inequality for u=g(x),v=g(y) and in the reverse direction, then add. Cauchy--Schwarz gives ||g(x)-g(y)||<=||x-y||.
The definition of f yields, for every displacement d,
<g(x),d><=f(x+d)-f(x)<=<g(x+d),d>.
It follows that the difference between f(x+d)-f(x) and <g(x),d> lies in [0,||d||^2]. Therefore f is differentiable with gradient g(x), and that gradient is 1-Lipschitz. It is finite by existence of the maximizers and convex as a supremum of affine functions. The subgradient condition xi_i in partial H(gamma_i) gives g(xi_i)=gamma_i and f(xi_i)=v_i. At the optimum sample this gives f(0)=0 and grad f(0)=0, so convexity makes 0 a minimizer.

The prefix-polynomial identity Poly_{i+1}=(1-h_i z)Poly_i gives xi_{i+1}=xi_i-h_i gamma_i. Thus the interpolation is a legal GD trajectory, with ||xi_0||=1. Apply (1) in both directions between n and *:
v_n>=||gamma_n||^2/2,
v_n<=<gamma_n,xi_n>-||gamma_n||^2/2.
Since <gamma_n,xi_n>=alpha|Poly_n(z)|^2, these are the two bounds in (a).

5. A discrete area-energy inequality.
Consider any r>=2 complex numbers w_0,...,w_{r-1}, with w_r=w_0. Put
d_k=(1/r)sum_{j=0}^{r-1}w_j exp(-2pi i k j/r), 0<=k<r.
The finite geometric-sum identity reconstructs w_j=sum_k d_k exp(2pi i k j/r). The same identity, on expanding the quadratic sums, gives
sum_j Im(conj(w_j)w_{j+1})=r sum_k |d_k|^2 sin(2pi k/r),
sum_j |w_{j+1}-w_j|^2=4r sum_k |d_k|^2 sin^2(pi k/r).
The k=0 terms vanish. For 1<=k<r, the ratio of the absolute sine coefficient in the first sum to the coefficient in the second is (1/2)|cot(pi k/r)|<=cot(pi/r)/2. The triangle inequality proves
|sum_j Im(conj(w_j)w_{j+1})| <= (cot(pi/r)/2)sum_j |w_{j+1}-w_j|^2. (3)
This also covers r=2, where the left side and cot(pi/2) are zero.
Every simple cycle on I_n has length r<=n+2. The function cot(pi/r) increases as the integer r>=2 increases. From (3) and the defining inequality for Lens_n one gets kappa Energy_C>=2|beta Area_C|. This proves (b).

6. Explicit real positive Chebyshev steps and their sum.
The recurrence for Cheb_j, the cosine addition formula, and induction give Cheb_j(cos t)=cos(jt). Its parity is the parity of j and its degree is j. Put m=2n+1. The odd polynomial Cheb_m(x) is divisible by x; hence (-1)^n Cheb_m(sqrt(z))/(m sqrt(z)) is a real polynomial of degree n. Differentiating Cheb_m(cos t)=cos(mt) at t=pi/2 gives Cheb_m'(0)=(-1)^n m, so its value at z=0 is 1.
The positive roots of Cheb_m are cos((2k-1)pi/(2m)), k=1,...,n. Squaring them gives exactly the distinct roots lambda_k=cos^2((2k-1)pi/(2m)) in (0,1) of the degree-n polynomial. It follows from the value at zero that the polynomial equals product_k(1-z/lambda_k). Thus its reciprocal roots are precisely the stated positive schedule, regardless of their order.

Twice differentiating the cosine identity and eliminating the angle gives the polynomial identity
(1-x^2)Cheb_m''(x)-xCheb_m'(x)+m^2 Cheb_m(x)=0.
Differentiation at x=0 gives Cheb_m'''(0)=-(m^2-1)Cheb_m'(0). The coefficient of z in (-1)^n Cheb_m(sqrt(z))/(m sqrt(z)) is consequently -(m^2-1)/6. The coefficient of z in the product of schedule factors is minus their sum. Hence s(Chi_n)=(m^2-1)/6=2n(n+1)/3.

7. The lens lies in an ellipse of small width.
Let n>=2, N=n+2, theta=pi/N, t=tan(theta)>0, and u=asinh(t/2)>0. For z=alpha+i beta in Lens_n,
0<=alpha<=1 and |beta|<=t alpha(1-alpha),
because alpha-alpha^2-beta^2>=0 and dropping beta^2 only weakens the defining inequality. Put s=alpha(1-alpha), so 0<=s<=1/4. Since sinh(u)=t/2 and cosh(u)>=1,
(2alpha-1)^2/cosh^2(u)+(2beta)^2/sinh^2(u)
 <=1-4s+16s^2<=1. (4)

For completeness (4) implies there is a complex w with |Im w|<=u and z=(1+cos w)/2. If beta=0, take a real w with cos w=2alpha-1. If beta!=0, the continuous function
v -> (2alpha-1)^2/cosh^2(v)+(2beta)^2/sinh^2(v)
decreases from infinity as v increases from zero and is at most 1 at v=u. Choose v in (0,u] where its value is 1, and choose a real angle a with
cos a=(2alpha-1)/cosh(v), sin a=-2beta/sinh(v).
Then w=a+i v has cos w=2alpha-1+2i beta.
A square root of z is cos(w/2). The cosine identity for Cheb_m holds for complex arguments as well, because it follows from polynomial recurrence and the complex cosine addition formula. Thus
|z| |Poly_n(z)|^2
 =|Cheb_m(sqrt(z))|^2/m^2
 =|cos(mw/2)|^2/m^2
 <=cosh^2(m u/2)/m^2. (5)
The polynomial interpretation covers z=0 as well; both sides of the equality before the bound are zero there.

The inequality asinh(t/2)<=t/2 follows by integrating its derivative, which is at most 1 on the nonnegative line. The function tan is convex on [0,pi/4], so it lies below its chord and tan(theta)<=4theta/pi=4/N. Therefore m u/2<=m t/4<=m/N<2. Combining (5) with (a) and alpha<=|z| proves
Gap_{Chi_n}(z)<=(2alpha-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2
 <=2|z||Poly_n(z)|^2<=2cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2.

8. Canonical Huber witness and the exact-profile comparison.
For any h>=0 let S=s(h) and delta=1/(2S+1). In dimension one define
f_delta(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=delta, and delta|x|-delta^2/2 otherwise.
The matching derivative is x clipped to [-delta,delta], hence is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. The function is convex, differentiable, 1-smooth and minimized at 0. Starting from x_0=1, every partial sum of h lies in [0,S]. Induction gives x_i=1-delta sum_{t<i}h_t>=1-delta S=(S+1)/(2S+1)>=delta. Thus every gradient equals delta and
2f_delta(x_n)=2delta(1-delta S)-delta^2=1/(2S+1).
Multiplying either this function or the interpolant from part 4 by L produces the same normalized error for the specified arbitrary L>0. Consequently each term defining V_lens(h) is a legal lower-bound witness for W_n(h), proving V_lens(h)<=W_n(h).

For Chi_n the Huber term is
1/(1+4n(n+1)/3)<=3/(4n^2).
Part 7 bounds its lens term by 2cosh^2(2)/(2n+1)^2. Since 2cosh^2(2)>=2>3/4, both terms are at most 2cosh^2(2)/n^2.
Verified predecessor b98bb807d514e80d states, for exactly the project phi/psi recursions and the project normalization, that B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1<=1+4n^p with p=log_2 3<2. Multiply the preceding bound on V_lens by B_n to obtain the displayed ratio estimate. It tends to zero because n^{-2}->0 and n^{p-2}->0. This uses no assumed limit for S_bullet(n)/n^rho and no numerical estimate of the profile constant.

Only the explicitly restricted witness family was upper bounded. None of the calculations gives an upper bound for the unrestricted W_n on the displayed schedules.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000699 -->
# Exact order sensitivity beyond the terminal schedule polynomial

Status: complete exploratory derivation, not submitted to the verifier. This is a counterexample to a proposed algebraic reduction, not to the project's asymptotic conjecture.

For every H>2,

    W_2(H,1)=(H-1)^2/3
    > W_2(1,H), since W_2(1,H)<=1/3+(H^2-2H)/4.

In particular W_2(3,1)=4/3 while W_2(1,3)<=13/12.
The two schedules have identical terminal polynomial (1-z)(1-Hz) and identical total step sum. Thus neither the full terminal polynomial, nor that polynomial together with the sum, determines the full smooth-convex worst-case objective.

All proofs below apply in arbitrary finite dimension. Normalize L=1, x_*=0, f_*=0, ||x_0||=1 by translating and rescaling the function and coordinates; the ratio defining W is unchanged.

## Elementary inequalities

For convex differentiable 1-smooth f,

    f(u)>=f(v)+<grad f(v),u-v>+(1/2)||grad f(u)-grad f(v)||^2.       (I)

For a self-contained proof, put k(x)=f(x)-<grad f(v),x>. Convexity and grad k(v)=0 show v minimizes k. Integrating the 1-Lipschitz gradient on a segment gives k(x-grad k(x))<=k(x)-||grad k(x)||^2/2. Combine with k(v)<=k(x-grad k(x)) and rearrange.

Adding both orientations of (I) gives
<grad f(u)-grad f(v),u-v>>=||grad f(u)-grad f(v)||^2.
Taking v=0 shows <grad f(u),u>>=||grad f(u)||^2, and Cauchy--Schwarz yields ||grad f(u)||<=||u||.

## A unit GD step

Start at an arbitrary vector x, put u=grad f(x), y=x-u and v=grad f(y). Inequality (I) for (x,y) gives

    f(x)-f(y)>=(||u||^2+||v||^2)/2.

The optimum pairs give f(x)<=<u,x>-||u||^2/2 and
f(y)<=<v,x-u>-||v||^2/2. Therefore

    f(y)<=<u,x>-||u||^2-||v||^2/2,
    f(y)<=<v,x>-<v,u>-||v||^2/2.

Average these two upper bounds. Writing w=(u+v)/2 gives

    f(y)<=<w,x>-(3/2)||w||^2-(1/8)||u-v||^2
         <=||x||^2/6.                                             (U)

Also f(y)>=||v||^2/2. Combining this with the first upper bound above yields

    ||v||^2<=<u,x>-||u||^2
             =||x||^2/4-||u-x/2||^2<=||x||^2/4.                   (G)

## Upper bound for (1,H)

After its first step, (U) and (G) give f(x_1)<=1/6 and ||grad f(x_1)||^2<=1/4.
The integrated Lipschitz-gradient inequality gives

    f(x_1-H grad f(x_1))
      <=f(x_1)+(H^2/2-H)||grad f(x_1)||^2.

For H>=2 the coefficient is nonnegative, so

    W_2(1,H)<=1/3+(H^2-2H)/4.                                    (F)

## Exact value for (H,1)

For H>=2, put u=grad f(x_0). Cocoercivity against the minimizer gives

    ||x_0-Hu||^2
      =1-2H<u,x_0>+H^2||u||^2
      <=1+H(H-2)||u||^2
      <=1+H(H-2)=(H-1)^2.

Apply (U) to the following unit step:

    W_2(H,1)<=(H-1)^2/3.                                         (R)

Equality is realized in dimension one. Set delta=(H-1)/3>0 and define

    f(x)=x^2/2                       if x>=-delta,
         -delta*x-delta^2/2          if x<=-delta.

The pieces and their derivatives match. Its derivative is max(x,-delta), which is nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz; therefore f is convex, differentiable and 1-smooth. It has minimizer 0 and minimum zero.

Starting from x_0=1, the H-step gives x_1=1-H=-3delta. The unit step gives x_2=-2delta. Since x_2<=-delta,

    f(x_2)=2delta^2-delta^2/2=3delta^2/2.

The normalized terminal error is 2 f(x_2)=3delta^2=(H-1)^2/3. This proves equality in (R).

Finally the difference between (R)'s exact value and (F)'s upper bound equals H(H-2)/12, strictly positive for H>2. The result permits arbitrary real H>2 and does not use numerical certificates or computational evidence.

## Scope

This rules out an exact order-blind description of W using only the terminal scalar polynomial. It does not rule out a weaker permutation-invariant lower bound, and it does not by itself decide the asymptotic minimax target.


<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000699 -->

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000700 -->
# A concrete final-step descent from a penultimate star-outgoing multiplier

Status: new self-contained candidate, unverified. No new verification allocation is assumed. This addresses one real component of the general-q descent problem; it does not prove that every q>tau tuple has such a component.

## 1. Input and exact schedule change

Normalize L=1 and f_*=0. Let h be any nonnegative schedule of length n>=1, with final step b=h_{n-1}. Use Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0, and the standard full upper certificate

    q||x_0-x_*||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij = M >=0,

where M is a PSD quadratic form after the exact GD substitutions, lambda_ij>=0, and the function-value coefficients cancel. The index * has x_*=g_*=0 and f_*=0. The result applies to every full feasible certificate, regardless of stationarity or Huber tightness.

Suppose

    nu=lambda_{*,n-1}>0.

For any 0<epsilon<=nu/(nu+2), put c=1-epsilon and replace ONLY the last step by

    b_new=c b+epsilon.

Then the new predetermined GD schedule has the rigorous uniform bound

    W_n(h_0,...,h_{n-2},b_new) <= c q < q.                    (D)

At the largest allowed epsilon this reads

    b_new=(2b+nu)/(nu+2),       q_new=2q/(nu+2).

The strict inequality is meaningful whenever q>0; every valid finite-horizon objective upper bound has q>0 by the canonical Huber witness.

## 2. Direct proof for all smooth convex functions

Fix any legal function and initial point, using the old and new schedules with the same prefix. Write

    x=x_{n-1}, g=grad f(x),
    z=x-b g                    (old endpoint),
    y=x-g                      (unit-step point),
    z_new=c z+epsilon y         (new endpoint),
    delta=f(x)-||g||^2/2 >=0.

Because Q_{*,n-1}=-delta, isolating this term in the original full PSD certificate yields

    2f(z)+nu delta <= q||x_0-x_*||^2.                        (A)

The remaining terms have the correct signs: the PSD quadratic form is nonnegative and every other -lambda_ij Q_ij is nonnegative.

The unit-step descent inequality gives f(y)<=delta. Convexity then yields

    2f(z_new) <=2c f(z)+2epsilon f(y)
                <=2c f(z)+2epsilon delta
                <=c[2f(z)+nu delta]
                <=c q||x_0-x_*||^2,

where 2epsilon<=c nu was the only restriction on epsilon. This proves (D) without selecting any hard function, differentiating W, or assuming a stationary worst-case primal.

The unit-step inequality can itself be derived from a single smooth-convex interpolation constraint:

    Q_{y,x}=f(y)-f(x)+<grad f(y),g>
              +||grad f(y)-g||^2/2
           =f(y)-delta+||grad f(y)||^2/2 <=0.

Thus the proof uses precisely the full smooth-convex class and is dimension-independent.

## 3. Explicit nonnegative-multiplier, PSD certificate

The construction is stronger than a sign argument: it gives an explicit enlarged interpolation certificate for the changed schedule. The two auxiliary points are the old endpoint z and the unit-step point y. The new algorithm still uses n steps and no auxiliary oracle calls; auxiliary points appear only in the proof and are defined from the same penultimate point and gradient.

Let g_z,g_y,g_new denote their actual gradients, and put

    d=c nu-2epsilon >=0.

Keep c times every old multiplier except lambda_{*,n-1}; assign d to Q_{*,n-1}. Add the new nonnegative multipliers

    2c       on Q_{z_new,z},
    2epsilon on Q_{z_new,y},
    2epsilon on Q_{y,x}.

All function-value terms cancel against the new objective -2f(z_new). After substituting z_new=c z+epsilon y and y=x-g, the full polynomial identity is

    c q||x_0-x_*||^2-2f(z_new)
      +c sum_{(i,j)!=(star,n-1)}lambda_ij Q_ij
      +d Q_{*,n-1}
      +2c Q_{z_new,z}+2epsilon Q_{z_new,y}
      +2epsilon Q_{y,x}

    = c M
      +c||g_new-g_z||^2
      +epsilon||g_new-g_y||^2
      +epsilon||g_y||^2.                                    (PSD)

The right side is an explicit PSD quadratic form, including the original full matrix M, not only its row sums or energy trace. The left side uses nonnegative multipliers throughout. All interpolation data concern the same globally legal function; no incompatible local transcript is glued in.

To check the identity, combine the first two new inequalities with weights c,epsilon. Their gradient-position terms cancel because z_new=c z+epsilon y. Then combine Q_{y,x}=f(y)-delta+||g_y||^2/2. The coefficient d is exactly what balances the old nu delta term.

The displayed certificate uses auxiliary analysis points. It is not asserted to be an explicit parameter-by-parameter map into the minimal n+2-point dual. Its full PSD identity proves the actual uniform upper bound directly, so it is already sufficient for a global-minimizer contradiction. When the new steps are positive, exact finite interpolation duality also gives a minimal-format certificate at its true worst-case value, but that existential statement is not needed for (D).

## 4. Consequence at global minimizers

Suppose h is a globally minimizing length-n schedule and its full certificate is tight: q=W_n(h)=W_n^*. If lambda_{*,n-1}>0, formula (D) constructs a length-n nonnegative predetermined schedule with strictly smaller true worst-case value. This contradicts global minimality.

Consequently EVERY tight full certificate at a global minimizer must satisfy

    lambda_{*,n-1}=0.                                      (V)

The contradiction uses an actual globally valid changed algorithm, so it is not merely a stationarity-implies-tightness shortcut. It does not need q=tau or a bound on the total step mass. It also handles a final step already equal to one: then the schedule does not change and (D) would improve the allegedly tight q itself.

This conclusion leaves lambda_{*,i} for i<n-1, lambda_{*,n}, and the PSD Schur-complement defect completely open. In particular it does not establish Huber tightness of a global minimizer.

## 5. Exact checks and the remaining q>tau case

For the one-step certificate at h_0=1+a, a>1, take

    q=a^2, lambda_{0,*}=2a^2,
    lambda_{*,0}=2a^2-2, lambda_{1,0}=2,

all other multipliers zero. Its exact slack is

    a^2||x_0-g_0||^2+||a g_0+g_1||^2,

and f(x)=x^2/2 attains q. The new formula gives c=1/a^2, h_new=1+1/a and the certified bound q_new=1. This is strictly smaller than a^2. The formula preserves nonnegativity and PSD through the explicit identity above; smaller epsilon gives a continuous descent.

The hypothesis nu>0 cannot be silently replaced by q>tau. For 1/2<a<1, the tight one-step certificate at h_0=1+a has q=a^2>tau=1/(2a+3), but no star-outgoing multipliers:

    lambda_{0,*}=2a, lambda_{1,*}=2(1-a), lambda_{1,0}=2a.

Its gradient block is [[2a,1],[1,1]], its star-directed vector is mu=(2a,2(1-a)), and its full Schur complement is

    Z=(2a-1)[[1,1/a],[1/a,1/a^2]] >=0.

The quadratic matches q. Thus the remaining non-Huber defect can be carried entirely by nonzero PSD slack even when all star-outgoing weights vanish. This example is not globally optimal; it is a hostile test of extending (V) into an unsupported general tightness claim.

## 6. Related terminal extension

If a certificate instead has lambda_{*,n}=nu>0, the same argument applied to an appended step gives, for 0<=epsilon<=nu/(nu+2),

    W_{n+1}(h,epsilon) <= (1-epsilon)q.

Here use the convex combination of x_n and x_n-grad f(x_n), and isolate the terminal residual delta_n=f_n-||g_n||^2/2 in the old certificate. This is a length-(n+1) improvement and must not be counted as a length-n contradiction. It may be useful for upper-bound composition, but no asymptotic comparison with the exact Zhang--Jiang profile follows automatically.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000701 -->
# General polynomial Gram data and the failure of spectral moments

Status: exploratory algebraic formulation, not a verified fact and not a target proof. Normalize L=1, x_*=g_*=0 and ||x_0||=1.

## Lossless formal-polynomial representation

First remove zero steps from a nonnegative schedule: they repeat the same point, value and gradient and do not change the final iterate. Let m be the number of remaining positive steps. Write them as h_0,...,h_{m-1}; define

    P_0(z)=1, P_i(z)=product_{t<i}(1-h_t z), 1<=i<=m.

Let V=R[z]_{<=m+1}. The list

    1, z P_0(z), z P_1(z), ..., z P_m(z)

is a basis of V: its degrees are 0,1,...,m+1 and its leading coefficients are nonzero because each retained h_t is positive.

For any actual legal GD transcript, the unique linear map T from V into its ambient Euclidean space satisfying

    T(1)=x_0, T(z P_i)=g_i

also satisfies T(P_i)=x_i. Indeed the polynomial telescoping identity is

    P_i=1-sum_{t<i} h_t z P_t,

and its image under T is precisely the GD recursion. Thus the PSD bilinear form

    K(U,V)=<T(U),T(V)>

contains all transcript Gram data, with K(1,1)=1.

Conversely, for any PSD symmetric bilinear form K on V, its quotient by the null space is a Euclidean space of dimension at most m+2. Denote polynomial equivalence classes by brackets and take x_i=[P_i], g_i=[z P_i]. The same polynomial identity gives the exact GD recursion. The optimum sample is x_*=g_*=0. Define

    Q_ij=K(z P_j,P_i-P_j)
          +(1/2)K(z P_i-z P_j,z P_i-z P_j),

with P_*=0 and z P_*=0. These samples admit convex 1-smooth interpolating values if and only if every directed cycle on {*,0,...,m} has nonpositive total Q weight, with Q_ij attached to j->i.

The sufficiency can be seen explicitly. Let ell_i be the maximum Q weight of a path i->*, with the empty path allowed at *. Every such maximum is over finitely many simple paths, since all cycles are nonpositive. Put F_i=-ell_i. Then F_i-F_j>=Q_ij, and these are the smooth-convex interpolation inequalities. The conjugate of

    H(g)=max_i { <g_i,x_i>-F_i+<x_i,g-g_i>+(1/2)||g-g_i||^2 }

is a globally finite convex 1-smooth interpolant. Its gradient is zero at the optimum sample. All candidate function values are at most F_i, by summing their inequalities along paths to *.

Hence the original W for this fixed schedule equals

    2 sup_K (-ell_m),

where K ranges over the PSD forms with K(1,1)=1 and all the displayed cycle inequalities. Zero steps can now be restored exactly, without a density argument or any restriction on real steps. This is an algebraic rewriting of the full interpolation problem, not a simplification of its minimax strength.

The matrix of K in the monomial basis has entries M_rs=K(z^r,z^s), 0<=r,s<=m+1. These form a two-index PSD array. There is no requirement that they depend only on r+s.

## A legal Huber transcript already needs non-Hankel entries

Take one step h_0=1 and the usual Huber function with threshold 1/3:

    f(x)=x^2/2                           for |x|<=1/3,
         |x|/3-1/18                     for |x|>=1/3.

From x_0=1, the transcript is x_1=2/3 and g_0=g_1=1/3. The function is convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, with minimizer 0. Its terminal value is f(x_1)=1/6, so normalized error is 1/3.

The representing map satisfies T(1)=1, T(z)=1/3 and
T(z-z^2)=1/3; hence T(z^2)=0. In the basis (1,z,z^2),

    M=[[1,1/3,0],[1/3,1/9,0],[0,0,0]].

It is a rank-one PSD matrix. It is not Hankel:
M_02=0 while M_11=1/9.

For any scalar spectral measure mu on R, a form of the type
K(U,V)=integral U(lambda)V(lambda) dmu(lambda) would instead have
M_02=integral lambda^2 dmu=M_11. Thus even this ordinary one-dimensional legal Huber transcript cannot be represented by scalar quadratic spectral moments.

There is a further issue beyond self-adjointness. Multiplication by z does not descend to the Euclidean quotient for this example. The polynomial z-1/3 is null because T(z-1/3)=0, but

    T(z(z-1/3))=T(z^2-z/3)=-1/9,

which is not zero. Thus the null space of K is not stable under multiplication by z. No single fixed linear operator, normal or nonnormal, represents this exact transcript on its one-dimensional realized space.

A scalar moment or fixed-matrix ansatz can therefore silently exclude legal smooth-convex behavior in two different ways: imposing Hankel symmetry, and requiring shift-invariance of null relations. The general bilinear polynomial form avoids both restrictions, at the price of retaining the full interpolation problem.

## What remains unresolved

The representation identifies the extra algebraic data exactly, but no lower-bound selection of K has been proved that matches the exact S_bullet profile uniformly over schedules. Choosing K by optimizing the full displayed interpolation problem is equivalent to the original fixed-schedule performance problem, so it is not an independent mechanism for the target comparison.


<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000701 -->

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000702 -->
# Complete-certificate obstruction to the unchanged remote-edge extension

Status: UNVERIFIED exact counterexample. This refutes one specified extension
of the suffix transport, not the existence of some descent and not Huber
tightness at global minimizers. It directly addresses TASK.md's unrestricted
two-step-suffix test.

## 1. A complete tight certificate

Take n=3, L=1, h=(2,9/4,3), q=25/4, and zeta=sqrt(78)/5.
Use Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2, with
x_star=g_star=f_star=0. All unspecified multipliers below are zero:

mu_0=lambda_(0,star)=25/2+5sqrt(78)/8,
mu_1=lambda_(1,star)=5sqrt(78)/8,
nu_0=lambda_(star,0)=5sqrt(78)/4+1,
nu_1=lambda_(star,1)=9/2,
nu_2=lambda_(star,2)=5,

lambda_10=25/2-5sqrt(78)/8,
lambda_21=8, lambda_32=2,
lambda_02=5, lambda_20=4.                            (1)

All are nonnegative; lambda_10>0 because sqrt(78)<9.
The finite flow equations can be checked explicitly:
at0, out=in=35/2+5sqrt(78)/8;
at1, out=in=25/2;
at2, out=in=12;
at3, out-in=2.

For independent formal vectors (x_0,g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3), define

R0=x_0-g_0,
U=g_0+g_1,
V=g_2+(5/4)g_1,
Z=g_3+2g_2.

The FULL matrix has the exact sum-of-squares representation

M=q||x_0||^2-2f_3+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
 =(25/4)||R0-(sqrt(78)/20)U||^2
   +(1/32)||U-16V||^2+||Z||^2.                     (2)

Here GD substitution and the displayed flows cancel every f coefficient.
Thus this is a full PSD certificate, with no omitted prefix or remote entry.

A short derivation of (2) also checks every coefficient. At the first step2,
the multiplier set
mu_0=2+zeta/2, mu_1=zeta/2, nu_0=zeta,
lambda_10=2-zeta/2
gives the identity
||x_0||^2-2f_1+sum lambda Q
 =||R0||^2-(zeta/2)<R0,U>+||U||^2.
Multiply it by25/4. For a step of length1+r, r>=1, direct expansion gives

2r^2 f_i-2f_(i+1)+2Q_(i+1,i)+2(r^2-1)Q_(star,i)
 =||r g_i+g_(i+1)||^2.

Add four times this identity with i=1,r=5/4 and once with i=2,r=2.
This gives a base certificate with the same q and the same multipliers
as (1), except lambda_02=lambda_20=0, nu_0=5sqrt(78)/4,
and nu_2=6. Its slack is

M_base=(25/4)[||R0||^2-(zeta/2)<R0,U>+||U||^2]
        +4||V||^2+||Z||^2.                         (3)

Finally add the exact zero-flow multiplier change
5Q_02+4Q_20+Q_(star,0)-Q_(star,2).
Its quadratic form is
-5||U||^2-<U,V>+4||V||^2.
Combining with (3), and using
(25/4)(1-zeta^2/16)=161/32, gives (2).

The certificate is TIGHT on the globally legal one-dimensional function
f(x)=x^2/2, with x_0=1. Its GD data are

(x_0,x_1,x_2,x_3)=(1,-1,5/4,-5/2),
(g_0,g_1,g_2,g_3)=(1,-1,5/4,-5/2),
(f_0,f_1,f_2,f_3)=(1/2,1/2,25/32,25/8).

Every Q_ij, including star pairs, is zero on these data, (2) vanishes,
and 2f_3=q. Thus W_3(h)=25/4. This is a complete legal matching
primal, not merely a PSD principal block.

The selected star multiplier is nu_(n-2)=nu_1=9/2>0.
There is a genuine remote prefix-to-suffix edge lambda_02=5.

## 2. The precise extension being refuted

Apply the previously valid c_2=0 two-step transport to k=1 while
leaving the remote edge unchanged. In this certificate,

c_1=mu_2+lambda_20+lambda_21=12,
c_2=mu_3+lambda_30+lambda_31=0,
c_3=lambda_32=2.

For any 0<eta<=9/2, the proposed extension changes

h'_1=(27+eta)/(12+eta),    h'_0=2, h'_2=3,
lambda'_21=8+eta,
nu'_1=9/2-eta, nu'_2=5+eta,                          (4)

and leaves every other multiplier and q unchanged. All steps and
multipliers remain nonnegative, and every flow equation remains exact.
Thus the only issue is the full PSD condition.

Direct expansion gives

M_new-M
 =eta||g_2||^2
   +[25 eta/(4(12+eta))]<g_0,g_1>.                  (5)

The second term is the previously absent remote cut contribution
(h_1-h'_1)lambda_02<g_0,g_1>. It cannot be discarded.

## 3. Exact negative direction in the OLD full kernel

Use one-dimensional formal vectors with

g_0=47/40,  g_1=-7/40,  g_2=9/32,  g_3=-9/16,
x_0=47/40+sqrt(78)/20.                              (6)

These need not be a legal new primal: they are a legitimate test of
PSD, which is required on ALL formal vectors.

Here U=1, V=1/16, Z=0, and R0=sqrt(78)/20.
Consequently the OLD FULL slack (2) is exactly zero.
On (6), equation(5) is exactly

M_new=eta(81 eta-344)/[1024(12+eta)].                (7)

It is negative for every 0<eta<344/81. In particular eta=1 is allowed
by (4), all actual multipliers remain nonnegative, but
M_new=-263/13312<0. There is therefore no sufficiently small positive
move of this unchanged transport that preserves PSD.

On the actual matching quadratic data from Section1, in contrast,
(5)/eta is 25/16-25/[4(12+eta)]>0. Thus checking only the
matching primal would falsely suggest a gain. The second full-kernel
direction is essential.

## 4. Scope and a different successful action on this example

This is NOT a stationary tuple and NOT a globally optimal schedule.
It does not refute some other simultaneous descent. Indeed one exists
with a different certificate re-selection.

First continuously undo the last zero-flow adjustment in Section1,
moving from (1) to its base certificate (3) at the SAME h,q.
Every intermediate actual multiplier is nonnegative, and every
intermediate slack is a convex combination of the PSD matrices (2)
and (3). The same legal quadratic remains matching throughout.
The base slack (3) has no extra kernel beyond that quadratic direction,
because zeta^2/16=39/200<1.

Now use the schedule h'=(2,28/13,3), corresponding to eta=1 in (4).
An explicit full certificate with value q'=900/169<25/4 is obtained
by keeping the first-step multiplier pattern
(mu_0,mu_1,nu_0,lambda_10)
=(2+zeta/2,zeta/2,zeta,2-zeta/2),
multiplying it by q'=4(15/13)^2, and then adding the two
function-growth identities from Section1:
four times the i=1 identity with r=15/13, and once the i=2
identity with r=2.

Its slack is

q'[||R0||^2-(zeta/2)<R0,U>+||U||^2]
 +4||(15/13)g_1+g_2||^2+||2g_2+g_3||^2 >=0,

all multiplier signs hold since15/13>1 and zeta<4, and its
function-value flow is exactly2e_3. The quadratic again attains q'.
This is a real fixed-length improvement for this example.

The obstruction therefore distinguishes two mechanisms:
the same sparse transport can fail at an unnecessarily degenerate
optimal dual certificate, whereas reselecting the dual certificate
inside the fixed-h optimum face can make a different descent available.
No theorem is claimed that such re-selection removes remote edges
for every schedule, nor that the maximal-rank dual face always has
the needed kernel structure.

Next action: analyze certificate re-selection together with the
inactive-interpolation costs before attempting a universal descent.
Do not add a remote-edge-absence hypothesis and call the original
extension repaired. The eventual exact-profile mass-cap issue remains
separate even if global Huber tightness were established.

## 5. A continuous repair that RETAINS the remote edges

The same obstruction can be repaired by a different, explicit simultaneous
reweighting, without first setting the remote multipliers to zero.
This supplies a concrete alternative mechanism on this example only.

For 1<=r<=5/4, take h(r)=(2,1+r,3), q(r)=4r^2, retain
zeta=sqrt(78)/5, and set z(r)=64r^2/25. Choose

mu_0=4r^2(2+zeta/2),  mu_1=2r^2 zeta,
nu_0=4r^2 zeta+z(r)(r-1),
nu_1=8(r^2-1),        nu_2=6-z(r)(r-1),

lambda_10=4r^2(2-zeta/2),  lambda_21=8, lambda_32=2,
lambda_02=r z(r),          lambda_20=z(r).            (8)

All unlisted multipliers are zero. They are nonnegative throughout the
interval: z<=4, r-1<=1/4, so nu_2>=5; and zeta<4.
Direct substitution gives out-in=0 at0,1,2 and2 at3. At r=5/4
these are exactly (1), and both remote edges stay positive for
every r in the interval.

Use R0=x_0-g_0, U=g_0+g_1, V_r=g_2+r g_1, Z=g_3+2g_2.
The full slack is

M(r)=4r^2||R0-(zeta/4)U||^2+||Z||^2
     +A(r)||U||^2-(r-1)z(r)<U,V_r>+(4+z(r))||V_r||^2,

A(r)=r^2(161-128r)/50.                              (9)

To derive (9), scale the first-step identity by4r^2, use the two
function-growth identities with factors r and2, and add the
finite/star flow change corresponding to
C_02=r z/2, C_20=z/2.
Its quadratic contribution is
-z[r||U||^2+(r-1)<U,V_r>-||V_r||^2].
Completing the same first square yields (9).

The remaining two-by-two matrix in (U,V_r) has positive A(r), and
its determinant is exactly

r^2(5-4r)(512r^3+1664r^2+16r+3220)/1250 >=0.          (10)

Thus the FULL M(r) is PSD. It has a positive determinant in this
remaining block when r<5/4, so the extraneous kernel responsible
for (7) is removed. The legal quadratic f(x)=x^2/2 follows
(1,-1,r,-2r), satisfies every active interpolation equality, and
attains q(r)=4r^2. Consequently (8)--(10) give the exact identity

W_3(2,1+r,3)=4r^2  for 1<=r<=5/4.

In particular moving r downward from5/4 is a continuous strict
same-length descent starting from the original degenerate certificate,
with nonnegative flow and both remote finite edges retained.
The failed sparse update and this successful update start from the
SAME full certificate. Their difference is the simultaneous scaling
of the prefix-source coefficients and the remote weights (quadratic
and cubic in r), which changes the full kernel correctly.

This does not assert that a comparable homogeneous reweighting exists
for an arbitrary near-optimal schedule. It identifies a genuinely
different concrete mechanism from keeping prefix multipliers fixed.

## DOC-000703 — archive_full_20261010/project/research/max2_star_flow_transfer.md

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000703 -->
# Explicit forward transfer of optimum-outgoing multipliers

Status: unverified new certificate operation. It preserves the standard full matrix and nonnegative interpolation multipliers under the stated capacity condition.

## 1. One adjacent transfer

Use the signed smooth-convex quantities

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0,

with the optimum sample * equal to zero. Fix a positive step h_i=a>0, so x_{i+1}=x_i-a g_i. A direct expansion gives the polynomial identity

    Q_{*,i+1}+(1/a)Q_{i+1,i}+((1-a)/a)Q_{i,i+1}
       -Q_{*,i}
       =(1/a)||g_{i+1}-(1-a)g_i||^2.                       (I)

For clarity, write delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2. Then
Q_{i+1,i}=delta_{i+1}-delta_i+||g_{i+1}||^2+(a-1)<g_i,g_{i+1}>,
and
Q_{i,i+1}+Q_{i+1,i}=(1-a)||g_i||^2+||g_{i+1}||^2+(a-2)<g_i,g_{i+1}>.
Substitution yields (I), including all coefficients and signs.

Take any full feasible upper certificate with multipliers lambda and PSD slack M. Choose t>0 satisfying

    t<=lambda_{*,i},
    t<=a lambda_{i,i+1}/(a-1)       when a>1.                (C)

When 0<a<=1 the second condition is absent. Change only these four multipliers:

    lambda'_{*,i}=lambda_{*,i}-t,
    lambda'_{*,i+1}=lambda_{*,i+1}+t,
    lambda'_{i+1,i}=lambda_{i+1,i}+t/a,
    lambda'_{i,i+1}=lambda_{i,i+1}+t(1-a)/a.                (T)

All multipliers remain nonnegative by (C), and each node's flow divergence is unchanged. The schedule h and certificate level q are unchanged. The FULL slack changes by

    M'=M+(t/a)||g_{i+1}-(1-a)g_i||^2 >=0.                  (P)

Thus (T) is an explicit feasible motion in the original certificate variables, not merely a reduced row-sum operation. It neither assumes stationarity nor removes any PSD constraint.

For a<=1, the transfer adds or preserves the forward-edge multiplier. For a>1 it consumes exactly t(a-1)/a of the existing forward edge lambda_{i,i+1}. At a=1 the added square is t||g_{i+1}||^2 and no forward capacity is consumed.

## 2. Transport across a whole interval

Suppose lambda_{*,i}>0 for some i<n-1. Assume that for every j=i,...,n-2, either

    0<h_j<=1,

or

    h_j>1 and lambda_{j,j+1}>0.                             (A)

Choose t>0 at most lambda_{*,i} and at most every finite capacity

    h_j lambda_{j,j+1}/(h_j-1),  j in [i,n-2] with h_j>1.

There are finitely many strictly positive capacities, so such a t exists. Apply (T) successively at j=i,...,n-2, always transferring the same amount t. Intermediate optimum-outgoing weights receive t and then pass it on, while the consumed adjacent forward edges are distinct. The resulting standard-format certificate has

    lambda'_{*,i}=lambda_{*,i}-t,
    lambda'_{*,n-1}=lambda_{*,n-1}+t>0,

and unchanged other optimum-outgoing weights, schedule, objective level and flow divergence. Its full matrix is

    M'=M+sum_{j=i}^{n-2}(t/h_j)
             ||g_{j+1}-(1-h_j)g_j||^2 >=0.                 (B)

No arbitrary chronological crossing edge has been discarded; the operation modifies only the stated adjacent multipliers and retains the rest of the original matrix exactly.

## 3. A resulting actual descent of the algorithm

Write nu=lambda'_{*,n-1}>0 and b=h_{n-1}. Isolating Q_{*,n-1}=-(f_{n-1}-||g_{n-1}||^2/2) in the transferred certificate gives

    2f_n+nu delta_{n-1}<=q||x_0-x_*||^2.

At x=x_{n-1}, the unit-step point y=x-g_{n-1} satisfies f(y)<=delta_{n-1}. The old endpoint is z=x-b g_{n-1}. For epsilon=nu/(nu+2), c=1-epsilon, the new final step b_new=c b+epsilon gives z_new=c z+epsilon y. Convexity yields

    2f(z_new)<=2c f(z)+2epsilon delta_{n-1}
                =c[2f(z)+nu delta_{n-1}]
                <=c q||x_0-x_*||^2.

Thus the concrete predetermined schedule with final step

    b_new=(2b+nu)/(2+nu)

has the strict uniform bound

    W_n(h_0,...,h_{n-2},b_new)<=2q/(2+nu)<q.

This is a genuine fixed-length improvement. The transfer phase stayed within the minimal full interpolation certificate, and the final convexity argument is valid for the same function in every instance. If desired, its explicit enlarged PSD certificate is obtained by adding the old endpoint and unit-step point as auxiliary analysis samples; its identity is given in research/max2_penultimate_flow_descent.md.

## 4. A forced blocking pattern at global minimizers

For a globally minimizing positive schedule and ANY tight full certificate, lambda_{*,n-1}=0. Moreover, if lambda_{*,i}>0 with i<n-1, then there must exist at least one j in [i,n-2] such that

    h_j>1 and lambda_{j,j+1}=0.                             (Block)

Otherwise (A) would transport positive weight to the penultimate point and the preceding explicit schedule change would strictly improve the global value.

This is a necessary pattern in the actual full certificate. It does not say that every star-outgoing weight vanishes: an overrelaxed step with a zero forward edge can block this particular transfer. The final weight lambda_{*,n} is also outside the fixed-length transport argument.

## 5. Tightness and matching primal data

If the original q is tight at the fixed h, every matching legal primal remains matching after any feasible transfer: h and q have not changed and the new certificate is globally valid. Because M' is the sum of M and the nonnegative squares in (B), each transferred square must vanish on that matching primal:

    g_{j+1}=(1-h_j)g_j

at every transfer edge. The new positive star weights also require delta_j=0 wherever they are placed. These are consequences of the full certificate and primal complementarity, not assumptions used to discard other inequalities.

This explains the restrictive nature of the transport. In particular, the earlier nonlinear-suffix counterexample does not invalidate (I): its relevant tight-certificate capacity conditions are not supplied. Conversely, it remains invalid to bypass a zero forward edge merely by claiming that the composed objective is convex.

## 6. Scope of the new mechanism

The operation supplies a concrete way to exploit some earlier optimum-outgoing weights while preserving PSD and every multiplier sign. It isolates exact barriers h_j>1, lambda_{j,j+1}=0 rather than an unspecified missing separator.

It still does not show that a q>tau global minimizer must have a transferable weight, or that the full Schur defect can always remove a blocking edge. Those are distinct open structural questions. No implication to the exact profile U_n^ZJ is asserted.

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000704 -->
# Global minimizers and a stationary energy identity for full PEP certificates

Status: exploratory, unverified proof package. No extra verifier submission is authorized. The selected initial submission was used for fact dfde2fa326c908bf. This document does not establish the exact-profile target.

## 1. Claim under investigation

For each fixed horizon n>=1, the project minimization over h in [0,infinity)^n has a global minimizer, and every global minimizer has strictly positive steps. At any such minimizer there exist a full interpolation upper certificate (q,lambda), a legal primal Gram matrix G and values f_i, attaining q=W_n^*, for which

    <g_t,z_t>=0, 0<=t<n,                                      (S)
    q=(1/2) sum_{i,j} lambda_ij ||g_i-g_j||^2.                 (E)

Here the Euclidean vectors realize G, x_*=g_*=0, ||x_0||=1, and

    z_t=sum_{i>t}(mu_i+sum_{j<=t}lambda_ij)g_i
        -sum_{i<=t}(sum_{j>t}lambda_ij)g_i,                    (Z)
    mu_i=lambda_{i,*}.

Every unstarred sum ranges over 0,...,n; directed pairs are distinct. The assertion retains the full certificate PSD constraint and the full primal interpolation constraints. It does not claim that these displayed identities alone characterize global minimizers.

The proof below supplies the compactness, zero-step exclusion, strong-duality and multiplier-regularity arguments rather than assuming interiority or nonsingular multipliers.

## 2. Fixed-horizon compactness and positive optimal steps

Normalize L=1, x_*=0 and f_*=0. For any fixed finite h, the estimates ||g_i||<=||x_i|| and ||x_{i+1}||<= (1+h_i)||x_i|| imply W_n(h)<=prod_i(1+h_i)^2. Every fixed legal function and initial point has an error continuous in h, by continuity of its gradient. A supremum of continuous functions is lower semicontinuous, so W_n is lower semicontinuous.

Write Poly_n(z)=prod_{t<n}(1-h_t z). For n+1 distinct nodes a_k=k/(n+1), 1<=k<=n+1, the quadratic f(x)=a_k x^2/2 from x_0=1 gives

    a_k Poly_n(a_k)^2 <= W_n(h).

Define the Lagrange polynomials L_k(z)=prod_{l!=k}(z-a_l)/(a_k-a_l). On the sublevel W_n(h)<=1, interpolation of a polynomial of degree at most n gives

    sum_t h_t = -Poly_n'(0)
      <= sum_k |L_k'(0)|/sqrt(a_k) =: C_n < infinity.

Nonnegativity of the steps therefore bounds each h_t by C_n. The sublevel is closed, bounded, and nonempty because h=0 has W_n=1. Lower semicontinuity on this compact set proves attainment of the global infimum.

For positivity, first note the explicit Huber lower bound. Put S=sum h_t and delta=1/(2S+1). The function f(x)=x^2/2 for |x|<=delta, and delta|x|-delta^2/2 otherwise, is convex and 1-smooth. From x_0=1, every iterate is x_i=1-delta sum_{t<i}h_t>=delta; its terminal error is 2f(x_n)=1/(2S+1)>0.

Suppose a globally minimizing length-n schedule contains a zero. Delete that zero to obtain h' of length n-1 with exactly the same terminal point and worst-case value q>0. Set B=prod_{t<n-1}(1+h'_t), so every normalized legal instance has terminal radius at most B. If its terminal function gap is F, convexity gives F<=B||g||, and a final unit step gives

    F_new <= F-||g||^2/2 <= F-F^2/(2B^2).

We have 0<=F<=q/2<=B^2/2. The displayed scalar function is increasing on this interval. Thus appending a unit step to h' produces another length-n schedule satisfying

    W_n(h',1) <= q-q^2/(4B^2) < q,

contradicting global minimality. Therefore every minimizing step is positive. This argument also handles the all-zero proposed minimizer and uses no differentiability of W_n.

## 3. Exact finite-dimensional primal formulation

Let I={*,0,...,n}. For i!=j define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

The primal variables are the Gram matrix G of (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), with G>=0 and G_00<=1, together with the real values f_0,...,f_n. The remaining positions are defined by x_i=x_0-sum_{t<i}h_t g_t, and the optimum sample is zero. Impose Q_ij<=0 for every ordered pair. Maximize 2f_n. Necessity of the interpolation inequalities follows by applying a unit gradient step to the convex 1-smooth function f(x)-<g_i,x>, whose minimum is attained at x_i: its decrease bound from x_j gives f_j-f_i-<g_i,x_j-x_i>>=||g_j-g_i||^2/2.

For completeness, the interpolation sufficiency has the following explicit proof. Given such finite vector/value data, put a_i=<g_i,x_i>-f_i and

    H(v)=max_i {a_i+<x_i,v-g_i>+||v-g_i||^2/2}.

At v=g_i, the i-th expression is a_i and every j-th expression is at most a_i, because that comparison is exactly -Q_ij>=0. Hence H(g_i)=a_i and x_i belongs to the convex subdifferential of H at g_i. The function H is finite and 1-strongly convex, with quadratic growth. Its conjugate f=H^* is finite everywhere, convex, differentiable and 1-smooth, and satisfies f(x_i)=f_i and grad f(x_i)=g_i. Indeed, strong convexity makes the maximizing vector in the conjugate unique; the strong monotonicity of the subgradient relation bounds the distance between two such maximizing vectors by the distance between their arguments. This gives the claimed gradient Lipschitz constant. The zero sample makes zero a minimizer. Thus the primal optimum is exactly W_n(h), with dimension at most n+2.

The feasible primal set is compact: the radius/gradient estimates above bound all Gram entries; the two optimum interpolation inequalities imply 0<=f_i<=||x_i||^2/2. It is closed and nonempty, so its optimum is attained.

For positive h, the primal admits strict interpolation inequalities and G positive definite. Take n+2 distinct numbers theta_k in (0,1), the quadratic with diagonal Hessian diag(theta_k), and an initial vector having every coordinate nonzero and norm less than one. The columns x_0,g_0,...,g_n are evaluations of 1,zPoly_0(z),...,zPoly_n(z) at these nodes, times nonzero coordinate factors. Their distinct degrees and nonzero leading coefficients make this matrix invertible. No two sampled positions agree, because their different prefix polynomials cannot agree at n+2 distinct nodes. None equals zero for the same degree reason. Direct substitution gives

    Q_ij=-(1/2)sum_k theta_k(1-theta_k)(x_{i,k}-x_{j,k})^2 <0

for every distinct pair, including *. The norm inequality is also strict.

Source context: Taylor, Hendrickx and Glineur, arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem 4 and Theorem 6 treat smooth-convex interpolation and strong duality for fixed-step methods using the latest gradient with nonzero coefficient. Their proof uses a full-rank quadratic transcript. The preceding Vandermonde construction and the separation argument below provide the needed hypotheses and conclusion directly here; no general-step extension is presumed.

## 4. Full dual attainment by finite-dimensional separation

Let T_ij(G) denote the gradient/position part of Q_ij, so Q_ij=f_i-f_j+T_ij(G). Consider the open convex set of triples (a,b,c) for which some G>=0 and some f satisfy

    a_ij>Q_ij(f,G), b>G_00-1, c<2f_n.

The point (0,0,q), where q is the finite primal optimum, is outside this set. A separating hyperplane, using the independent upward variations of a,b and downward variation of c, has nonnegative coefficients lambda_ij, q_0, alpha, not all zero, and yields

    sum lambda_ij Q_ij(f,G)+q_0(G_00-1)-alpha*2f_n >= -alpha*q

for all G>=0 and all f. The strict primal point constructed above rules out alpha=0: substituting it would make the left side strictly negative whenever lambda or q_0 is nonzero. Thus normalize alpha=1.

The arbitrary f coordinates force

    out_i-in_i=2*1_{i=n}.

The arbitrary PSD matrix G then forces

    M(q_0,lambda,h)=q_0 E_00+sum lambda_ij T_ij >=0.

Taking G=f=0 gives q_0<=q. Conversely the flow and PSD constraints give an upper certificate W_n(h)<=q_0. Therefore q_0=q and the full dual is attained.

This argument establishes the direction needed here: at a minimizing positive schedule there is a certificate with q exactly equal to its true worst-case value. A feasible upper certificate by itself is never interpreted as a lower bound.

## 5. A strictly feasible certificate at every finite h

A regularity direction is needed because the optimal certificate can have many zero multipliers and singular PSD slack. We now construct a certificate with all directed multipliers positive and M positive definite at the same fixed h.

For each finite i, adding equal weight to lambda_{i,*} and lambda_{*,i} preserves the flow. Their quadratic sum is

    Q_{i,*}+Q_{*,i}=||g_i||^2-<g_i,x_i>.

Let H_max=max_t h_t, r=1/(2H_max+3), and w_i=r^(2i)>0. The gradient block A_w of the weighted sum of these cycles has diagonal w_i and, for i>t, off-diagonal w_i h_t/2. With D=diag(w_i), the matrix D^(-1/2) A_w D^(-1/2) has diagonal one and off-diagonal h_t r^(i-t)/2. Each row's absolute off-diagonal sum is at most H_max*r/(1-r)<1/2. Hence A_w is positive definite, as follows also by bounding each cross term with the two corresponding squared coordinates.

Start with lambda_{n,*}=2, and add weight one to every directed finite-to-finite pair. Those paired edges are flow-balanced. Next add K w_i to each of lambda_{i,*} and lambda_{*,i}. All directed multipliers are now positive and the required flow holds. For sufficiently large finite K, their gradient block A is positive definite, because K A_w dominates the fixed remaining symmetric matrix. The full matrix has form

    M=[[q,-mu^T/2],[-mu/2,A]],  mu_i=lambda_{i,*}.

Choosing q>mu^T A^(-1)mu/4 makes M positive definite. This constructs strict feasibility with no assumptions on which multipliers of the optimum vanish.

## 6. KKT without assumed nondegeneracy

Take a global minimizing positive schedule h and an attained certificate (q,lambda). It is also a global minimizer of q over the joint variables (q,lambda,h), subject to flow, lambda>=0 and M>=0: every such certificate upper-bounds a true W_n(h)>=W_n^*.

Here is the finite-dimensional separation argument giving the needed multipliers. Restrict directions d to the linear flow-null space. Let J be the set of zero lambda entries, and let K_0=ker M. If a direction satisfies

    dq<0, d lambda_e>0 for e in J,
    (DM[d]) restricted to K_0 is positive definite,

then a sufficiently small positive step along it is feasible and improves q. To justify the matrix assertion, decompose into K_0 and its orthogonal complement. The complementary block of M is positive definite, the kernel block has a positive first-order term, and cross blocks are first order. The Schur complement subtracts only a second-order term. The dependence on h and lambda is polynomial, so its Taylor remainder is also second order. Positivity of h persists for sufficiently small steps. Such a descent direction is therefore impossible.

Separating the image of the direction space from the open cone defined by these three strict inequalities gives an objective multiplier alpha>=0, numbers r_e>=0 supported on J, and a PSD matrix G supported on K_0, not all zero, annihilating every flow-null direction:

    alpha*dq - sum r_e*d lambda_e - <G,DM[d]> =0.

Move from the optimal certificate toward the strictly feasible certificate from section 5 while holding h fixed. That direction has every d lambda_e>0 on J and has positive compression of DM to K_0. Substituting it rules out alpha=0, unless r=G=0, which would contradict nontrivial separation. Normalize alpha=1.

Extending the annihilator by flow multipliers written as -f_i gives the coefficient identities

    G_00=1,
    f_i-f_j+T_ij(G)=-r_ij<=0,
    lambda_ij r_ij=0,
    M G=0,
    <G,partial M/partial h_t>=0 for every t.

The PSD matrix G is consequently a legal normalized primal Gram matrix by section 3. The interpolation inequalities and lambda complementarity imply sum lambda_ij Q_ij=0. Taking the trace of M G then gives q=2f_n. Thus G realizes an actual worst-case transcript. Degenerate lambda and singular M were allowed throughout.

If either J or K_0 is empty, omit its corresponding strict-cone factor and multiplier. The same argument applies in these zero-dimensional cases.

## 7. The resulting quantitative identities

Let x_0,g_0,...,g_n be Euclidean vectors with Gram matrix G. Expanding the derivative with respect to h_t gives exactly

    partial/partial h_t [sum lambda_ij T_ij]
      = <g_t,
          sum_{i>t}(mu_i+sum_{j<=t}lambda_ij)g_i
          -sum_{i<=t}(sum_{j>t}lambda_ij)g_i >.

The stationary equations are therefore (S) with z_t defined in (Z), including every edge that crosses the chronological cut t.

The x_0 row of M G=0 and G_00=1 give

    sum_i mu_i <x_0,g_i>=2q.                                  (X)

The full quadratic trace can be expanded as

    0=<M,G>
     =q-sum_i mu_i<x_0,g_i>
       +sum_{t<n}h_t<g_t,z_t>
       +(1/2)sum_{i,j}lambda_ij||g_i-g_j||^2.

Using (S) and (X) yields the announced energy identity (E). The energy is nonnegative, and all chronological cut terms have been justified individually rather than discarded.

These equations constrain both a full feasible dual certificate and a full feasible primal transcript at an actual global minimizer. They do not supply a uniform numerical comparison with the Zhang--Jiang recurrence yet.

## 8. Exact one-step primal-dual check

For n=1, h_0=3/2, q=1/4, take

    lambda_{0,*}=lambda_{1,*}=lambda_{1,0}=1,

with all other multipliers zero. Direct expansion yields

    M = v v^T, v=(1/2,-1,-1).

A matching stationary primal Gram matrix is

    G=[[1,1/3,1/6],
       [1/3,1/6,0],
       [1/6,0,1/12]],
    (f_0,f_1)=(1/4,1/8).

It is the convex combination with weights 8/9 and 1/9 of two legal one-dimensional transcripts, each starting at x_0=1:

* Huber threshold 1/4: (g_0,g_1)=(1/4,1/4), (f_0,f_1)=(7/32,1/8).
* Quadratic f(x)=x^2/2: (g_0,g_1)=(1,-1/2), (f_0,f_1)=(1/2,1/8).

The direct sum after coordinate scaling by the square roots of the weights is itself one legal two-dimensional instance, so the combination is not merely a formal averaging device. In detail, for weights a,b and scalar functions H,Q, use F(u,v)=a H(u/sqrt(a))+b Q(v/sqrt(b)) and initial point (sqrt(a),sqrt(b)); this preserves smoothness one and gives exactly the displayed Gram matrix and values.

The full matrix equality M G=0 holds. The cut vector is z_0=2g_1 and <g_0,g_1>=0, verifying stationarity. The directed energy is

    (1/2)(||g_0||^2+||g_1||^2+||g_1-g_0||^2)
      =1/6+1/12=1/4=q.

Global one-step optimality follows directly: the Huber lower bound is at least 1/4 for h_0<=3/2, and the quadratic lower bound (h_0-1)^2 is at least 1/4 for h_0>=3/2. The displayed certificate attains 1/4.

A hostile check is useful: the pure Huber transcript is worst-case for this very same optimal schedule and dual certificate, but has <g_0,g_1>=1/16 and fails stationarity. Thus it is invalid to assume that an arbitrary active worst-case function supplies the stationary primal matrix. The multiplier-separation construction selects a suitable primal matrix, and an admissible direct sum may be essential.

## 9. Exact remaining obstruction

The current identities do not force q to equal the sum-based Huber level, do not show lambda_{*,i}=0 at a global minimizer, and do not imply that all temporal gradients are orthogonal. Each such additional simplification would require its own proof or counterexample test.

A route to the target would still need a quantitative estimate combining the full PSD slack, all interpolation inequalities, the cut equations (S), and the energy (E), uniformly in horizon, whose constant matches the exact recurrence. Merely defining that missing estimate is not a result. The package above establishes a candidate rigorous interface at genuine global minimizers, with exact feasible-data checks, and records the current obstruction.

<!-- END_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000704 -->

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000705 -->
# Why creating penultimate flow first does not solve the stationary case

Status: unverified failure analysis of a specific proposed extension. The valid transfer and descent identities remain intact.

Consider a full matching stationary tuple (h,q,lambda,G,f) with q>0, h_{n-1}>0, all primal interpolation inequalities, dual PSD, complementarity, and the stepsize stationarity equations. Put p=g_{n-1}, g=g_n, delta=f_{n-1}-||p||^2/2.

## 1. Last-step stationarity selects an orthogonal terminal pair

For the SAME legal primal G selected by the full stationary tuple, one has

    <p,g>=0.                                               (1)

Here is a proof that does not assume a C2 function or differentiability of W. Realize the legal finite data by a convex 1-smooth interpolating function. Change only the final step b by a small real u. Then x_n(u)=x_n-u p, g_n(u)=g_n+O(u), and

    f_n(u)=f_n-u<p,g>+o(u).

Let D be the derivative of the full certificate matrix with respect to b, holding q and lambda fixed. The old matrix annihilates the old vector realization by PSD complementarity. Therefore its evaluation on the changed gradient vectors is O(u^2), and the full polynomial identity gives

    sum lambda_ij Q_ij(u)
       =u[<D,G>-2<p,g>]+o(u).

The left side is nonpositive for both signs of u and vanishes at zero. Consequently <D,G>=2<p,g>. The full stationarity equation <D,G>=0 proves (1). This argument keeps the same G; it does not substitute an arbitrary active hard function for the stationary one.

## 2. A positive margin in the penultimate unit-step residual

The interpolation inequality Q_{n,n-1}<=0, together with (1), gives

    f_{n-1}-||p||^2/2 >= f_n+||g||^2/2,
    delta >= q/2+||g||^2/2 > q/2.                          (2)

The strictness holds because q=2f_n>0 implies g_n!=0. If B=prod_t(1+h_t) is the elementary terminal radius bound for normalized initial distance, convexity further gives

    ||g||^2>=q^2/(4B^2),
    delta>=q/2+q^2/(8B^2).                                 (3)

This is a constraint on the actual full stationary primal, including the case q>tau.

## 3. Exact obstruction to certificate inflation followed by final-step blending

Suppose one first changes only the certificate at the SAME h, perhaps increasing its level from q to q', to create a positive new penultimate star-outgoing multiplier nu'. Every such feasible certificate must satisfy, on the old matching legal primal,

    q' >= q+nu' delta.                                     (4)

Indeed its PSD identity gives q'>=2f_n+nu' delta after the other nonnegative interpolation contributions are dropped.

The largest permitted final-step convex blend then certifies the value 2q'/(2+nu'). But (2) and (4) imply

    2q'/(2+nu')
       >= q + nu'||g_n||^2/(2+nu') > q.                    (5)

A smaller blend parameter gives no better certified level. Therefore the two-stage procedure

    raise or alter the certificate at fixed h to create nu'>0,
    then use the proved final-step convex-blend bound,

cannot prove a descent from a full stationary tuple. This is an exact finite inequality, not only a first-order obstruction, and it applies regardless of q-tau.

The original penultimate-flow descent still detects genuinely nonoptimal data, and the transport operation still preserves full feasibility. Equation (5) explains why those operations cannot be bootstrapped into a generic q>tau contradiction after full last-coordinate stationarity has already been reached.

## 4. Implication for the next mechanism

The surviving problem necessarily involves an earlier stepsize change, a nonlinear suffix response, or a quantitative global consequence of the full matrix beyond terminal coordinate optimality. The exact suffix counterexample in research/max2_earlier_reset_obstruction.md forbids carrying final-step convexity backward without additional structure.

The current terminal operations are complete useful constraints, but they do not prove Huber tightness of globally optimal schedules. Further work should not keep rediscovering last-coordinate descent under different notation.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000706 -->
# Exact terminal re-selection through the fixed-prefix maximal extension

Status: The expanded self-contained package, including arbitrary zero steps and the empty horizon, is ACCEPTED as fact 1252b8823bb5a290. See max2_terminal_reselection_selected.md for the exact submitted package and max2_terminal_reselection_errata.md for the notation note. This earlier positive-step research draft is retained for history.
This is a precise positive support result for the full optimal certificate set.
It removes only incoming edges at the UNQUERIED final sample, not arbitrary
remote forward edges at queried samples. No descent or profile theorem is claimed.

## 1. The reduced primal retains the complete prefix

Normalize L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be a
finite positive schedule, n>=1, and let P={star,0,...,n-1}.
As usual x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, and

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Consider the full Gram primal on (x_0,g_0,...,g_n), with G>=0,
||x_0||^2<=1, and objective maximize 2f_n. Keep

    Q_ij<=0 for every distinct i,j in P,
    Q_(n,j)<=0 for every j in P.                    (1)

Delete every Q_(j,n)<=0, j in P, including Q_(star,n).
No other constraint is removed. In particular all remote forward and
backward interpolation constraints inside the prefix remain.

The value of this reduced primal equals the true unrestricted W_n(h).

## 2. Exactness by an explicit globally legal extension

Take any feasible reduced transcript and realize its Gram matrix by
finite-dimensional Euclidean vectors. For j in P put

    a_j=<g_j,x_j>-f_j,
    H_j(v)=a_j+<x_j,v-g_j>+||v-g_j||^2/2,
    H(v)=max_(j in P) H_j(v),
    F(x)=sup_v {<x,v>-H(v)}.                       (2)

Each H_j is a quadratic with Hessian I, so H is finite, continuous and
1-strongly convex. Its quadratic growth makes the supremum defining F
finite and attained at a unique vector v(x). The optimality relation is
x in partial H(v(x)). Strong convexity gives

    <x-x',v(x)-v(x')> >= ||v(x)-v(x')||^2,

and hence ||v(x)-v(x')||<=||x-x'||. The maximizing affine functions in (2)
then show grad F(x)=v(x): the lower bound from testing v(x) and upper
bound from testing v(x') squeeze the first-order difference, with an
O(||x-x'||^2) error. Thus F is globally convex, differentiable, and
1-smooth.

For any i,j in P,

    H_j(g_i)-a_i=Q_ij<=0.

Consequently H(g_i)=a_i, and x_i is a subgradient of H at g_i, since the
i-th quadratic is active there and has gradient x_i. It follows that
grad F(x_i)=g_i and F(x_i)=f_i. The star sample gives F(0)=0 and
grad F(0)=0, so zero is a global minimizer. This proves legality of the
complete prefix without invoking an external interpolation black box.

Because GD through x_n uses only g_0,...,g_(n-1), the SAME predetermined
schedule on F follows all the supplied positions through x_n.
The retained terminal inequalities say

    H_j(g_n)<=<x_n,g_n>-f_n for every j in P.

Therefore

    f_n<=<x_n,g_n>-H(g_n)<=F(x_n).                  (3)

The new actual terminal value is at least the reduced transcript's
objective. Thus every reduced feasible value is at most W_n(h).
Conversely any legal trajectory is reduced feasible, proving exactness.

The constructed F is also the pointwise maximal smooth-convex extension
of the fixed prefix. Indeed, for any legal extension K, the elementary
smooth-convex interpolation inequality between each fixed sample j and
an arbitrary sample x with gradient grad K(x) gives
K(x)<=<x,grad K(x)>-H(grad K(x))<=F(x).
Maximality is useful motivation but (3) alone proves reduced-PEP exactness.

At an optimal reduced transcript, 2f_n=W_n(h), both inequalities in (3)
are equalities. Uniqueness of the conjugate maximizer implies
g_n=grad F(x_n) and f_n=F(x_n). Hence an optimal reduced primal is itself
fully interpolable: the deleted incoming terminal inequalities hold at
optimality. This statement is about optimal transcripts, not all reduced
feasible transcripts.

## 3. The reduced dual attains the SAME value

Here are the necessary finite-dimensional details, so re-selection is
not an appeal to an unattained dual infimum.

A strict reduced primal exists when every h_t>0. Choose n+2 distinct
numbers theta_l in (0,1), take a quadratic with diagonal Hessian
diag(theta_l), and an initial vector with every coordinate nonzero and
norm less than one. The columns x_0,g_0,...,g_n are evaluations of

    1, z, z product_(t<1)(1-h_t z), ...,
                       z product_(t<n)(1-h_t z)

at the distinct nodes, times nonzero initial coordinates. These n+2
polynomials have degrees 0,1,...,n+1 with nonzero leading coefficients,
so their evaluation matrix is invertible. Thus G is positive definite.

No two positions among star and 0,...,n are equal: their distinct
prefix polynomials, each of degree at most n, cannot agree at all n+2
distinct nodes; none of those polynomials vanishes at all nodes either.
For the strictly convex quadratic with Hessian strictly below I,
direct substitution gives

    Q_ij=-1/2 (x_i-x_j)^T Hquad(I-Hquad)(x_i-x_j)<0

for every distinct pair. In particular every retained inequality is strict.

The reduced optimal value is finite by Section2 and the elementary GD
bound W_n(h)<=product_t(1+h_t)^2. Its dual attainment now follows directly
from separating an open convex set, as follows. Let E_red be the retained
ordered pairs. The open convex set of (a,b,c) satisfying, for some G>=0,f,

    a_ij>Q_ij(f,G) (ij in E_red),
    b>G_00-1,
    c<2f_n

does not contain (0,0,q), where q=W_n(h). A separating hyperplane has
nonnegative coefficients lambda_ij,r,eta, not all zero, and yields

    sum_(E_red) lambda_ij Q_ij(f,G)
             +r(G_00-1)-eta*2f_n >= -eta*q

for all G>=0,f. The strict primal point rules out eta=0. Normalize eta=1.
Arbitrary values f then force out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n), and arbitrary PSD G
forces the full matrix M(r,lambda,h)>=0. Testing G=f=0 gives r<=q;
weak duality and reduced exactness give r>=q. Thus r=q and the dual
attains the exact objective value.

Extend this reduced certificate to the original full format by setting
all omitted multipliers to zero. We have proved the precise re-selection:

    At every positive h there EXISTS a tight full certificate with
        lambda_(j,n)=0 for ALL j in P.             (4)

In particular nu_n=0 can be imposed, and the remaining outgoing
terminal weights sum to exactly two. This does not say that every
optimal certificate already has this support. It does not assert that
a source vector can be held fixed during the re-selection.

This argument is the ordinary finite-dimensional separation proof only
for the reduced system just justified. It does not repeat or claim as
new the historical global minimizer/KKT interface.

## 4. Exact terminal square and monotone probability coordinates

For a certificate satisfying (4), write

    alpha_j=lambda_(n,j)/2 >=0, j in P,
    sum_(j in P)alpha_j=1,
    y_j=x_j-g_j,  delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2,
    V_alpha=x_n-sum_(j in P)alpha_j y_j.            (5)

Let lambda^P denote the unchanged multipliers on ordered pairs inside P.
Direct expansion, with delta_star=y_star=0, gives the FULL identity

    M(q,lambda,h)
      =||g_n-V_alpha||^2+R_alpha,                  (6)

where

    R_alpha=q||x_0||^2
                  -2sum_(j in P)alpha_j delta_j-||V_alpha||^2
                  +sum_(i,j in P)lambda^P_ij Q_ij. (7)

The prefix flow cancels all function values in R_alpha. In detail,
for each finite i<n it is

    out_i^P-in_i^P=2alpha_i,

because the removed terminal outgoing edge n->i supplied incoming flow
2alpha_i in the full certificate. R_alpha is thus a quadratic form
in (x_0,g_0,...,g_(n-1)).

Full PSD is EQUIVALENT to R_alpha>=0: one direction follows from (6),
and the other follows by minimizing the full quadratic over the
independent formal vector g_n. This is an exact Schur complement,
not the omission of an unobserved kernel direction.

The same identity is a necessary-and-sufficient certificate
parameterization of this selected subface: nonnegative prefix
multipliers with the displayed prefix flow, alpha in the probability
simplex, and the complete PSD remainder R_alpha.

For explicit chronological coefficients define

    w_(-1)=alpha_star,
    w_t=alpha_star+sum_(j=0)^t alpha_j, 0<=t<n.

Then 0<=w_(-1)<=w_0<=...<=w_(n-1)=1, and

    V_alpha=alpha_star x_0
            -sum_(t<n)[h_t w_t-(w_t-w_(t-1))]g_t. (8)

This keeps all earlier gradient coordinates. In particular
lambda_(n,star)=2alpha_star<=2. The formula is not a scalar-mass
replacement for the prefix PSD condition (7).

On ANY actual matching worst case for this selected certificate,

    g_n=V_alpha,  R_alpha=0,
    alpha_j Q_(n,j)=0, lambda^P_ij Q_ij=0.          (9)

Thus re-selection gives an actual terminal gradient formula for the
matching primal. It does not impose such a formula on every legal
trajectory; away from equality the square in (6) measures its defect.

## 5. Relation to the remote-edge obstruction and source variation cost

For a two-step suffix k=n-2, the selected support eliminates remote
forward edges i->n and the local edge (n-1)->n. It does NOT eliminate
remote i->(n-1) for i<k. Those edges are within the complete prefix
and remain in (7). In particular the old three-step counterexample
at h=(2,9/4,3) already has no incoming edges at its final sample3:
its hostile edge0->2 survives this reduction unchanged.

At a full stationary matching tuple with nu_k>0, the common strict
barrier derived directly from interpolation still applies:
lambda_(k,k+1)=0 across the full optimal set, with positive old slack.
The terminal re-selection may alter earlier source coefficients and
may alter which actual matching primal supplies earlier-cut stationarity.
One cannot transport all stationarity equations to an arbitrarily
chosen reselected certificate.

For any joint change from a fixed stationary base, the exact old-primal
identity remains

    q'-q >= sum lambda'_ij(-Q_ij(old))
                  -sum_t (h'_t-h_t)<g_t,z_t(lambda'-lambda)>.

Neither the terminal square (6) nor dropping the redundant terminal
incoming inequalities cancels this inactive cost or bilinear term.

## 6. Literature check and scope

The explicit maximum-of-quadratics conjugate construction is consistent
with Taylor, Hendrickx and Glineur, Smooth strongly convex interpolation
and exact worst-case performance of first-order methods,
arXiv:1502.05666, Theorem4 and Remark2. Their Theorem6 uses a full-rank
quadratic transcript to establish fixed-step PEP duality. Those sections
were read in the downloaded paper. All construction, reduced-system
exactness, separation, and full square identities needed here are
written out, rather than imported as unexplained external theorems.
A targeted Matlas query returned unrelated results; no such result was used.

This is a concrete, validly scoped route to re-selection and a complete
matrix parameterization of the resulting subface. It is not a descent
theorem at a global minimizer, does not remove the surviving remote
prefix edges, and does not resolve Huber tightness or the exact DP mass
restriction.
