<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000716 -->
# C58 complete-PEP extension: model and scaling audit

This is numerical discovery under the one-time max3 allocation in
`max3_full_objective_extension_budget.json`. Every attempted call is
recorded before model construction; its status and elapsed time are saved
afterward. Imports are conservatively included in the measured time.
Post-solve array serialization and residual diagnostics are not solver time.
No eigenvalues or multipliers are repaired or truncated. No hidden solver
fallback or retry is performed. The public runtime invokes CLARABEL0.11.1
through CVXPY1.9.3 with one computational thread.

## Complete mathematical model

For the actual supplied schedule h of length n, take independent formal
vectors x0,g0,...,gn. Let xi=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t gt and let the optimum sample
have x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Optimize over the full symmetric Gram matrix
and all n+1 function values, with no rank or support restriction:

    maximize 2f_n,
    Gram >=0, Gram_(x0,x0)<=1,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0
          for EVERY distinct ordered pair in {star,0,...,n}.

Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the equivalence with the actual
smooth-convex worst case. No terminal inequality is deleted here.

In the original formal basis, let A_ij be the symmetric coefficient
matrix for the quadratic part of Q_ij, and let Fcoef have entries+1 at
i and-1 at j when these are finite. The script constructs the full sparse
arrays directly. Both star directions and every finite direction occur.

## Exact change of coordinates used for conditioning

Let M=1+sum h and D=diag(1,1/M,...,1/M). The variables in the solver are

    Ghat=Gram(x0,Mg0,...,Mgn), fhat=Mf.

The original Gram is D Ghat D. The solver constraint is M Q_ij<=0,
with coefficient matrix M D A_ij D, and it maximizes2fhat_n. This is an
invertible change of variables and a positive constraint/objective scaling.

If the solver returns raw pair multipliers lambda and raw norm multiplier
rho, the ORIGINAL pair multipliers are the same lambda and the original
dual objective is q=rho/M. The complete original coefficient matrix is

    C_orig=q e0 e0^T+sum lambda_ij A_ij.

The scaled stationarity matrix is M D C_orig D. It is compared directly
with the RAW dual multiplier of the PSD constraint. Value flow is checked
as Fcoef^T lambda-2 e_n, using the original objective. The saved data include
both scaled and original Grams, all raw pair labels/multipliers, both
objective matrices, PSD duals, function values, all original Q residuals,
all flow residuals, complementarity, eigenvalues, and scaling metadata.

## Analytic one-step calibration

For h=(1), choose multipliers lambda_(0,star)=lambda_(1,star)=
lambda_(1,0)=1 and all others zero. Their value coefficients cancel2f1.
The full coefficient identity at q=1/3 is

    (1/3)||x0||^2-2f1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
      =(1/3)||x0-(3/2)(g0+g1)||^2+(1/4)||g0-g1||^2.

This proves the upper bound1/3 directly. The scalar Huber function with
threshold1/3, initial point1, and step1 has g0=g1=1/3, x1=2/3 and
f1=1/6, attaining the bound. Thus the calibration target is exactly1/3.

## Schedules and benchmark

For each case actual_Hm the script independently sets

    c=(1+sqrt2)(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), j=0,...,m-1.

It does not truncate, reorder, or replace any step. Binary floating-point
evaluation of the displayed exact algebraic word is saved, along with the
formula. The script compares with 1/(1+2sum h), 1/(1+sum h), and B_n from
the full finite primitive/dominant recurrences in acceptedb98bb807d514e80d.
The latter are evaluated with70 decimal digits, saving both entire
recurrence tables and maximizing split indices. These decimal tables are
not claimed as rigorous interval certificates.

The raw JSON is saved to both the worker's own research directory and the
PUBLIC project research directory before any summary is printed. Each
summary contains the file hash. A negative eigenvalue remains visible,
and an inaccurate solver status is retained verbatim. Finite numerical
agreement is neither an exact certificate nor an asymptotic result.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000717 -->
# C59: a fully specified legal H6 adversary above 4/M6

Status: **exact finite reconstruction and joint proof candidate; UNVERIFIED**.
No new fact is claimed and no verifier submission is allocated. The only
accepted mathematical predecessor is `1252b8823bb5a290`, for full
smooth-convex interpolation and its explicit conjugate construction.

This is a finite diagnosis of the actual H6 word, not an asymptotic theorem.
The word here uses epsilon=1/6. It is not the length-63 subword obtained by
fixing epsilon=1/m for a much larger horizon m.

## 1. Statement and exact input object

Put

    c=(7/6)(1+sqrt(2)), a=5(sqrt(2)-1)/7,
    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<6,
    h=H6, n=63, S=sum_(i<63)h_i, M6=1+S.

There is a completely specified convex differentiable 1-smooth function
on R^66 with unique minimizer0 and an actual GD trajectory for this h
such that

    2(F(X_63)-F(0))/dist(X_0,argmin F)^2
             > 56/2711 > 1/50 > 4/M6.              (1)

In particular, this finite word cannot have a full valid upper certificate
at level1/M6. No assertion is made about eventual large-m feasibility.

The rational construction data are the lower-triangular integer matrix T
of order65 and the64 integer values v_i in

    research/max3_c59_h6_rational_primal.json.

The PUBLIC file's SHA256 is

    7376e26ca81edc6438c0563e008c845ce439e424bd42f9f65c37a07867d6b113.

Its fields `coordinate_integer_rows_lower_triangular` and
`function_value_numerators` are part of the definition, with the common
denominator D=10^15. For reproducibility the complete defining integer
data are also appended to the shared proof finding. No numerical solver
claim is used as a premise of the construction below.

Let the rational vectors in R^65 be

    v_start=T[0,:]/D,  q_i=T[i+1,:]/D (0<=i<=63),
    u_i=v_i/D.

Define the core positions by the EXACT algebraic GD recursion

    z_0=v_start,
    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i q_i.                            (2)

Here q_63 is not used in (2), but will be the core terminal gradient.
The original numerical Gram is not treated as an exact Gram. These
rational vectors define their own positive semidefinite Gram by dot
products, regardless of any properties of the source solver output.

## 2. The finite exact checks, with their integer certificate

For these core data include the zero optimum jet. Define

    Q^core_ij=u_i-u_j+<q_i,z_j-z_i>+||q_i-q_j||^2/2,
    delta^core_i=u_i-||q_i||^2/2,

with the star's position, gradient and value all zero. The following
strict bounds are verified by integer comparisons of the defining data:

    Q^core_ij < eta=1/500000000000 for EVERY i!=j,
    delta^core_i > -eta for every finite i,
    ||v_start||^2 < 1001/1000,
    u_63 > 21/2000,
    200<S<256,  h_i>1 for every i.                  (3)

The first line checks all65*64=4160 ordered pairs, including both optimum
directions and both terminal directions. Here are the exact formulas used
to audit (3), so the finite part is not a feasibility-tolerance assertion.

The data file gives each step as

    h_t=(A_t+B_t sqrt(2))/54432

with integer arrays A_t,B_t. These are exactly the recursively defined
steps: arithmetic in Q(sqrt(2)) with a=(-5/7,5/7) and c=(7/6,7/6)
produces those integer pairs. Let

    G_ab=sum_k T[a,k]T[b,k].

Thus <q_i,q_j>=G_(i+1,j+1)/D^2 and
<q_i,v_start>=G_(i+1,0)/D^2. Set Hden=54432 and define integer arrays

    R_ij=Hden G_(i+1,0)-sum_(t<j) A_t G_(i+1,t+1),
    I_ij=                  -sum_(t<j) B_t G_(i+1,t+1).

Then <q_i,z_j>=(R_ij+I_ij sqrt(2))/(Hden D^2).
When j=star, set both of these numerators to zero. When i=star, every
term containing q_i is zero. Set v_star=0 and the corresponding gradient
Gram entries to zero. The exact pair numerator formula is

    Qa_ij=2 Hden D(v_i-v_j)+2(R_ij-R_ii)
                   +Hden(G_(i+1,i+1)+G_(j+1,j+1)-2G_(i+1,j+1)),
    Qb_ij=2(I_ij-I_ii),
    Q^core_ij=(Qa_ij+Qb_ij sqrt(2))/(2 Hden D^2),    (4)

using the displayed star conventions. The missing diagonal terms for a
star index in (4) are zero.

For the radical, use the rational interval

    Lrad/10^50 < sqrt(2) < (Lrad+1)/10^50,
    Lrad=141421356237309504880168872420969807856967187537694.

Its validity follows by squaring the two positive integer endpoints.
For each pair an upper-bound numerator with denominator
2 Hden D^2*10^50 is

    Qa_ij*10^50+Qb_ij*(Lrad+1)  if Qb_ij>=0,
    Qa_ij*10^50+Qb_ij*Lrad      if Qb_ij<0.          (5)

The data file contains all4160 integer triples from (4)--(5). Every one
satisfies `upper_numerator*500000000000 < denominator`. The largest
upper bound occurs at the directed pair (31,30) and is approximately
1.371014491112536e-12; the exact numerator and denominator are retained
in `audit.max_Q_upper_numerator` and `audit.max_Q_upper_denominator`.
This decimal is descriptive, whereas the integer comparison proves the
inequality used in (3).

The other checks in (3) use the same radical interval for S and each h,
and use, without a radical,

    delta^core_i=(2v_i D-G_(i+1,i+1))/(2D^2),
    ||v_start||^2=G_00/D^2.

The minimum core delta is already positive in these data (approximately
8.5986e-11), but only the weaker stated lower bound is needed. The audit
script is PUBLIC at `research/max3_c59_h6_exact_primal_repair.py`.
Its single finite integer check completed in under0.1second and used no
optimization, search, eigenvalue clipping, or large symbolic computation.
This is a transparent finite arithmetic component of an unverified proof
candidate; it is not a substitute for the verifier's acceptance.

## 3. One controlled auxiliary coordinate repairs all pairs

Choose

    kappa=1/512, t0=1/8,
    xi_0=1, xi_(i+1)=(1-kappa h_i)xi_i.

Every factor is positive since h_i<=S<256. For any initial segment,
the elementary product inequality

    product_t(1-a_t)>=1-sum_t a_t  (a_t>=0, sum a_t<1)

follows inductively from (1-a)(1-b)>=1-a-b. It gives

    1>=xi_i>=1-kappa S>1/2.

For i<j, positivity and h_i>1 imply

    xi_i-xi_j>=xi_i-xi_(i+1)=kappa h_i xi_i>kappa/2.

The distance to the optimum sample xi_star=0 is also greater than
kappa/2. Thus these bounds cover every distinct pair, including star.

Define the66-dimensional jets and values

    X_i=(z_i,t0 xi_i),
    G_i=(q_i,t0 kappa xi_i),
    f_i=u_i+t0^2 kappa xi_i^2/2
       =u_i+xi_i^2/65536, 0<=i<=63,
    X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0.                      (6)

Their GD recursion is EXACT by (2) and the recursion for xi. The full
interpolation residual separates by coordinates into

    Q_ij=Q^core_ij
          -t0^2 kappa(1-kappa)(xi_i-xi_j)^2/2.      (7)

This is a polynomial identity, not a claimed separability of the final
function. The second term is the residual for the single quadratic
auxiliary coordinate at the displayed samples. Since1-kappa>1/2,
its magnitude is strictly greater than

    t0^2 kappa^3/16=2^(-37)>eta.

Combining with (3) proves Q_ij<0 for ALL4160 ordered pairs simultaneously.
The same common values and coordinates are used in every pair.

## 4. One global function and the true initial distance

For each finite sample put

    Y_i=X_i-G_i, delta_i=f_i-||G_i||^2/2.

Equation (6) gives

    delta_i=delta^core_i+t0^2 kappa(1-kappa)xi_i^2/2
             >-eta+t0^2 kappa/16>0.                (8)

Add the star with Y_star=0,delta_star=0, and define

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in {star,0,...,63})
                              [<Y_i,v>-delta_i],
    F(X)=sup_v[<X,v>-H(v)].                         (9)

By accepted1252b8823bb5a290 and (7), this is one globally defined finite
convex differentiable function with1-Lipschitz gradient, interpolating
every value and gradient in (6). Therefore its GD trajectory is the one
already specified, with the actual ordered word H6.

The finite set of strict gaps in (8) makes the star's zero affine function
uniquely active in a neighborhood of v=0. Hence H(v)=||v||^2/2 there and
partial H(0)={0}. The maximizing-vector condition for (9) identifies the
minimizers of F with partial H(0), as in the accepted predecessor. Thus
argmin F={0}; no remote or nonclosest minimizer is used in normalization.

The true squared distance and terminal value satisfy

    R^2=||v_start||^2+1/64 < 1001/1000+1/64
                              =8133/8000,
    2F(X_63)=2u_63+t0^2 kappa xi_63^2 >21/1000.

Consequently

    2F(X_63)/R^2> (21/1000)/(8133/8000)
                 =56/2711>1/50>4/(1+S),

which proves (1), subject only to the explicitly supplied finite integer
checks on the defining data. If a unit initial norm is desired, use
F_unit(x)=F(Rx)/R^2 and initial point X_0/R; the smoothness remains1,
the steps remain h, and the normalized payoff is unchanged. Multiplication
of the function by any L>0 supplies the corresponding L-smooth instance
with steps h_i/L.

## 5. A concrete feature of the surviving geometry

The rational core gradients have the exact inner product

    <q_35,q_36>
      =-14715168270572693173715883215/10^30
      <-14/1000.

The extra coordinate changes this by at most t0^2 kappa^2=2^(-24),
since0<xi_i<=1. Thus

    <G_35,G_36><-1/100.

Both indices are queried. The actual object retains substantial signed
gradient correlation; it is not a Huber common-gradient line, a mutually
orthogonal gradient transcript, or an all-gradients acute-cone trajectory.
The auxiliary coordinate repairs small interpolation errors in an already
strong correlated core, in contrast to attempting to repair the grossly
illegal fixed scalar C55 template. No claim is made that this description
is a scalable generating rule.

There is also a basis-independent distinction from a bounded-jump
coordinate construction. Let d_i=G_i-G_(i-1),1<=i<=63, and let J be their
Gram matrix. If an orthonormal coordinate system makes every coordinate
of the gradient list change at most k times, then

    2 sum_(i<j)|J_ij| <= (k-1) trace(J).             (10)

Indeed each scalar coordinate of the jump list has at most k nonzero
entries. Cauchy--Schwarz gives (sum|a_i|)^2<=k sum a_i^2, hence
2sum_(i<j)|a_i a_j|<=(k-1)sum a_i^2. Sum over coordinates and apply the
triangle inequality to the resulting off-diagonal entries. Since J is
unchanged by orthogonal changes of physical coordinates, (10) applies to
every such representation.

For the exact rational core, the integer increment Gram is saved PUBLICLY
in `research/max3_c59_exact_increment_gram.json`. Its entries follow
directly from the rational vectors already defined, with denominator D^2.
Exact integer summation gives

    trace(J_core) approximately 0.44249400386221016,
    2sum_(i<j)|J_core_ij| approximately 2.306374594722243,
    2sum_(i<j)|J_core_ij|-5trace(J_core)>9/100.       (11)

Only the last, rational inequality is used. The auxiliary jump vector is
a_i=t0 kappa(xi_i-xi_(i-1)); all entries are negative and
(sum|a_i|)^2<t0^2 kappa^2=2^(-24). Its contribution is aa^T. By the
triangle inequality, adding aa^T can lower the left side of (11) by at
most

    2sum_(i<j)|a_i a_j|+5sum_i a_i^2
           <=5(sum_i|a_i|)^2<5*2^(-24).

Thus the full repaired J still satisfies
2sum_(i<j)|J_ij|>5trace(J). Equation (10) rules out every representation
in which all coordinate gradients have at most SIX chronological jumps.
In particular this actual finite adversary is outside the common two-jump
primal cone. The large core margin in (11) ensures this distinction is
not caused only by the tiny auxiliary quadratic coordinate.

## 6. Scope and next open interface

This candidate is finite, exact in its definitions, and includes all
function values, gradients, positions, actual GD equations, and the true
distance. It does not rely on a numerically feasible Gram or a nearly
positive semidefinite dual. The source solver arrays are preserved
unchanged; the rational data and added coordinate are separately labeled
new objects.

The estimate rules out1/M6 for this one actual word. It does NOT refute an
upper bound valid only at sufficiently large horizons, establish a common
pattern for the changing parameters epsilon=1/m, or prove the all-schedule
Zhang--Jiang profile. In particular the length63 block changes physically
as the enclosing horizon changes. The missing scalable interface is an
analytic rule for the correlated core that controls ALL interpolation
pairs and distance under those parameter changes. Repeating this finite
data set, extrapolating its numerical eigenvectors, or fixing only one
turning pair would not provide that rule.

No routine verification allowance was used. This remains a complete
finite proof candidate, not an accepted fact.

# C60: a nonlinear two-state legal trajectory with dense turning gradients

Status: **new explicit construction and scoped calculations; UNVERIFIED**.
No solve or verifier call is used. The numerical allocation is closed.
This branch follows the finite C59 reconstruction by seeking an analytic
correlated core. It does not assume that the C59 data belong to this family.

The construction works for every finite nonnegative schedule, including
zeros and arbitrarily large entries, with one fixed globally convex
1-smooth function. Its all-pair law and true closest-minimizer distance
are explicit. The quantitative payoff on the critical H_m remains open.

## 1. The global function, its gradients, and its minimizers

In R^2 let

    C_par={(t,s): s>=t^2/sqrt(2)},
    F_par(X)=sup_(g in C_par) [<X,g>-||g||^2/2].      (1)

The set is closed, convex and nonempty, and contains0. Completing the
square shows that the maximizer in (1) is the Euclidean projection of X
onto C_par. It exists and is unique because the squared-distance objective
is coercive and strictly convex. Thus F_par is finite and convex.

For completeness write p(X) for that projection. Its elementary normal
condition is

    <X-p(X),g-p(X)><=0 for every g in C_par.

Applying this twice and adding gives

    ||p(X)-p(Y)||^2<=<p(X)-p(Y),X-Y>.

Thus p is1-Lipschitz. Comparing the two maximizers in (1) gives

    0<=F_par(Y)-F_par(X)-<p(X),Y-X>
        <=<p(Y)-p(X),Y-X><=||Y-X||^2.

Consequently F_par is differentiable, its gradient is p, and it is
1-smooth. This proof supplies the needed global property directly.

The minimizers are exactly

    argmin F_par={(0,y):y<=0}.                      (2)

Indeed F_par>=0 by the feasible vector0. For X=(0,y), y<=0, every g=(t,s)
in C_par has <X,g>=ys<=0, so the maximum in (1) is0. Conversely, if
the vertical coordinate y>0, taking g=(0,s) with small s>0 makes the
objective positive. If the horizontal coordinate x is nonzero and y<=0,
take g=(t,t^2/sqrt(2)) with sufficiently small nonzero t of the sign of x:
the linear term xt dominates the terms of quadratic and quartic order.
This again makes the objective positive. These cases prove (2).

This F_par is not a convex quadratic: its minimizer set is a ray, whereas
the nonempty minimizer set of an unconstrained convex quadratic is an
affine subspace.

## 2. Coordinates that make every interpolation pair explicit

For a real r and v>=0, put

    u=v+r^2,
    X(r,v)=(r(1+v+r^2), -v/sqrt(2)),
    G(r)=(r,r^2/sqrt(2)).                           (3)

Then X-G=u(r,-1/sqrt(2)). For any (t,s) in C_par,

    <X-G,(t,s)-G>
      =u[r(t-r)-(s-r^2/sqrt(2))/sqrt(2)]
      <=-(u/2)(t-r)^2<=0.

The normal condition therefore proves that G(r) is the actual gradient
of F_par at X(r,v). Direct substitution into (1) gives

    F_par(X(r,v))=r^2(1+v)/2+3r^4/4.                (4)

For any two sampled states r_i,v_i and r_j,v_j, with the actual values
from (4), the complete signed interpolation residual is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2
        =<X_j-G_j,G_i-G_j>
        =-(u_j/2)(r_i-r_j)^2<=0.                   (5)

The first equality after Q follows by substituting
f_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2. The second uses the displayed parabolic
coordinates. This proves EVERY ordered pair simultaneously for one
function; no local, adjacent, or cycle relaxation is being used.

For an initial nonzero r_0 and v_0>=0, the closest minimizer in (2) is

    X_star=(0,-v_0/sqrt(2)),
    R_0^2=dist(X_0,argmin F_par)^2
            =r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2.                (6)

The optimum sample fits the same formulas with r_star=0, v_star=v_0,
u_star=v_0. Thus (5) includes both of its directions. Choosing the origin
instead would generally overestimate the true initial distance; (6) is
the normalization used throughout.

## 3. The exact GD transition, with no interpolation optimization

For ANY step h>=0, starting from state(r,v), define

    v_plus=v+h r^2,
    r_plus^3+(1+v_plus)r_plus
                      =r(1+v+r^2-h).              (7)

The cubic's left side is strictly increasing from minus infinity to
plus infinity because its derivative is1+v_plus+3r_plus^2>=1. Thus (7)
defines exactly one real r_plus, by an explicit scalar algebraic rule.
There is no nearest-feasible-trajectory problem hidden in this definition.

The vertical coordinate of X-hG is -v_plus/sqrt(2), and its horizontal
coordinate is the right side of (7). Hence

    X(r_plus,v_plus)=X(r,v)-h G(r).

This proves actual GD for the same global function. In particular h=0
leaves the state unchanged, and every finite positive step gives finite
new state. All v_i remain nonnegative, regardless of gradient sign changes.

For a supplied finite nonnegative schedule h_0,...,h_(n-1), iterate (7)
from any r_0!=0,v_0>=0. Its exact normalized payoff is

    P_par(h;r_0,v_0)
       =[r_n^2(1+v_n)+(3/2)r_n^4]
                        /[r_0^2(1+v_0+r_0^2)^2].  (8)

The function (1), all values(4), gradients(3), positions(3), and initial
distance(6) are fixed together. Thus (8) is an actual lower witness for
W_n(h), albeit not yet a profile-strength one on the critical schedules.

Some immediate identities expose the changing geometry:

    u_plus-u=(h-1)r^2+r_plus^2,
    X_plus,horizontal=X_horizontal[1-h/(1+u)].      (9)

In particular, for schedules with all h>=1, the normal parameter u is
nondecreasing, but r and the horizontal gradient can turn repeatedly.
The Hessian direction changes with r; this is not a fixed linear spectral
transcript. The vertical state accumulates h r^2 rather than discarding
previous turns.

## 4. Exact dense-jump calibration on a different schedule

Take any sigma in(0,1), v_0>=0, and define

    U_0=1+v_0+sigma,
    rho=(1+sigma)/(1-sigma),
    h_i=2U_0 rho^i/(1-sigma),
    r_i=(-1)^i sqrt(sigma),
    v_i=U_0 rho^i-1-sigma.                         (10)

These v_i are nonnegative. Substitution into (7) gives
v_(i+1)-v_i=h_i sigma and
r_(i+1)(1+v_(i+1)+sigma)=r_i(1+v_i+sigma-h_i),
so (10) is an exact trajectory of F_par. Its payoff is

    P_par=rho^n/U_0+sigma/(2U_0^2).                 (11)

This calibration is legal and can have large payoff. It is a geometric
step sequence, NOT the critical ruler word, so (11) proves no claim about
H_m or the original profile. It only checks that the mechanism supports
correlated turns without a feasibility repair.

There are n nonzero collinear gradient jumps in (10):

    G_i-G_(i-1)=(2(-1)^i sqrt(sigma),0), 1<=i<=n.

Their Gram J has trace4sigma n and twice the absolute off-diagonal sum
4sigma n(n-1). For any Gram realizable with at most k nonzero jumps in
each orthonormal coordinate, Cauchy--Schwarz coordinate by coordinate
implies2sum_(i<j)|J_ij|<=(k-1)trace(J). Hence this trajectory requires
some coordinate with at least n jumps in every orthonormal realization.
Dense chronological correlation does not require a large ambient dimension.

For a completely rational parameter example, sigma=1/4 and v_0=0 give
rho=5/3, h_0=10/3, h_1=50/9, and r_i=(-1)^i/2. Formula (11) is then
an exact expression, not a numerical simulation.

## 5. Two simple parameter limits do not yet give the desired mechanism

First fix a finite schedule and fix v_0=A>=0. Let r_0=t tend to zero.
Induction in (7) gives

    v_i=A+O(t^2),
    r_i=t product_(j<i)[1-h_j/(1+A)]+O(t^3).

At each induction step, the inverse cubic has nonzero derivative1+A
at zero, and its cubic term is O(t^3), giving the displayed expansion.
Thus the payoff tends to the ordinary scalar quadratic value

    [1/(1+A)] product_(j<n)[1-h_j/(1+A)]^2.         (12)

The implicit constants here depend on the entire fixed finite schedule.
They have NOT been bounded uniformly along growing H_m. Consequently
(12) is a calibration and an explanation of why an infinitesimal core
does not itself add a new mechanism; it is not a class obstruction.

Second put S=sum h_i and sigma=r_0^2. If v_0>=S, no horizontal sign flip
occurs and |r_(i+1)|<=|r_i|. Indeed h_i<=v_i and the right side of (7)
has the sign of r_i. Evaluating the increasing cubic at r_i shows that
its excess over that right side is h_i r_i(1+r_i^2), with the appropriate
sign, so the claimed decrease in absolute value follows. Hence

    v_n<=v_0+S sigma,
    P_par <= [1+v_0+S sigma+(3/2)sigma]
                                  /(1+v_0+sigma)^2. (13)

Along any growing-horizon choices with
v_0/[S(1+sigma)] tending to infinity, this gives S P_par tending to zero.
Thus placing the initial state arbitrarily far down the flat minimizer
direction also loses the desired scale. This rules out only the displayed
parameter regime; intermediate coupled regimes remain open.

## 6. The natural geometric calibration is small on the entire critical word

One tempting choice is to match the geometric growth ratio rho in(10)
to the physical critical parameter c_m. This means

    m>=128, epsilon=1/m,
    c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,
    sigma=(c-1)/(c+1), r_0=sqrt(sigma), v_0=0.       (14)

For THIS particular starting rule, the full actual H_m trajectory obeys

    P_par(H_m;r_0,0)
              < (2500/m^2) 2^(-2^m).              (15)

The same is true with the opposite initial horizontal sign, by symmetry.
This does not bound the family over other choices of r_0,v_0.

Here is a proof that tracks all later queried states, rather than inferring
the answer only from the first cancellation. Elementary parameter bounds are

    12/5<c<5/2, 2/5<a<3/7,
    2/5<sigma<3/7, 7/5<b0=1+a<10/7.

For the lower bound on a, use 1+sqrt(2)<17/7 and
(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon)>=127/129, giving a>889/2193>2/5.
The other inequalities follow by direct comparison of positive fractions.
Since c is greater than1+sqrt(2),

    0<sigma-a
      =epsilon[2lambda(lambda-1)+lambda^2 epsilon+c+1]
                                               /[c(c+1)]
      <2epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2).                 (16)

For the last bound the numerator in brackets is less than12, using
lambda<5/2 and epsilon<=1/128, while c(c+1)>204/25>8.

At the first actual step b0, (7) gives

    14/25<v_1=b0 sigma<30/49<5/8,
    r_1(1+v_1+r_1^2)=r_0(sigma-a),
    0<r_1<2/m.                                    (17)

We now prove that the entire remaining trajectory stays in the region

    1/2<=v<=3/4, |r|<=1/10.                        (18)

Inside this region the exact step rule gives explicit amplitude bounds.
For a level-zero step b0, the numerator factor
1+v+r^2-b0 is positive and at most1+3/4+1/100-7/5=9/25.
